# Decisions to review

Decisions taken on the user's behalf while the steps run, newest last, with their reason and where they live. Entries 1–17 were accepted by the user on 2026-10-25.

## 2026-10-25: the plan

1. **Order of the remaining work:** 9.6 → 9.7 → 9.8 → 9.9 (error routes audit) → M9b (9b.1–9b.5, one window with window tabs and the tray) → 10.1 (performance) → 10.2 (release) → M11 (11.1–11.6, extensions) → M12 (12.1, plan area 3). Release before extensions, so 10.2 describes the one-window design and not the multi-window one that goes away. (`docs/IMPLEMENTATION.md`)
2. **Single window details** I chose (SPEC §10.14): the menu becomes a `≡` button (`window.menuBarVisibility` gains `compact`, the new default); window tabs are 120–240 px and scroll when they overflow; duplicate folder names get the parent folder after an em dash; tab jumps are **Ctrl+Alt+1…9** (Alt+1…9 stay VS Code's "open editor at index"); Ctrl+Alt+PageUp/PageDown cycle; Ctrl+Shift+N opens a No Folder tab and Ctrl+Shift+W closes the active window tab; inactive tabs keep running and stay mounted but do not tick or paint; `window.restoreWindows` keeps its name and means "restore tabs"; the close button hides to the tray (`window.closeToTray`, default on) and quits when no tray is shown (plain GNOME without AppIndicator).
3. **The `+` button** after the last tab opens a menu: **Workspace...** (the desktop's folder chooser → new workspace tab), then one entry per extension tab kind from M11. Until M11 it has only "Workspace...".
4. **Extensions** (SPEC §10.15, D18), from our conversation, with these details decided by me:
   - Extension tabs are **window tabs only** (top-level, beside workspaces), never tabs inside a workspace's editor area. Inside a workspace, extensions contribute **file views** (micro-tabs) and **side bar views**.
   - Micro-tabs sit at the **right end of the breadcrumbs row** (a 22 px strip when breadcrumbs are hidden), shown only for files with at least one extension view; **Text** is always first; the choice is remembered per editor tab; `workbench.editor.defaultFileViews` picks a default view per glob; `workbench.action.cycleFileView` cycles.
   - Extensions talk **only through events**, plus **request/reply events** for "call an action of another extension" (10 s default timeout). No typed Go APIs between extensions, no calling each other's commands.
   - Every **window tab is a scope**; events cross scopes only along a **directional link** (`notes → office`) the user allowed, optionally limited to topics; the links live in `~/.local/state/emetos/event-sharing.json` (your choice (a)), written only by Emetos's prompt ("Allow", "This Session Only", "Deny") and its page "Extensions › Event Sharing"; no settings file can change them. A workspace can be **private** (sends nothing). An extension can only ask for cross-tab topics it declared in its manifest.
   - Values in events are JSON copies (nothing live crosses tabs); fast streams like typing are batched per frame.
   - **Language packs** move to `extensions/` (step 11.4) with no change of behavior; the core keeps plain text only. Themes and the Seti icon theme stay in the core.
   - First real extensions to prove the three UI kinds: `extensions/markdown` (preview file view), `extensions/todo` (side bar view of TODO/FIXME comments), `extensions/scratchpad` (a window tab with notes and a "Recent edits" feed that exercises the event-sharing prompt). A web browser is not feasible: Glutter has no web view and a web engine needs cgo or a huge dependency.
   - Disabling: `extensions.disabled` (a list of IDs) and an "Extensions" page with Enable/Disable.
   - Area 3 (LSP, `gopls` in `extensions/golang`) gets its own plan in step 12.1, written by me and logged here instead of asking you.
5. **Error routes:** step 9.9 adds route tests for every untested tag, removes the error-building helpers (AI_GUIDE R4) and the package-level `errors.New` (R5), adds a script `scripts/error-tags.sh --untested`, and puts R4/R5 into CLAUDE.md.
6. **How "ask the user" steps are handled tonight:** 9b.4's icon: I look at it and accept it if it is clean and readable at 16 px; 10.1: a target still missed after profiling is recorded in `docs/PERFORMANCE.md` with the measurement and a Glutter issue where the cause is Glutter, and the work goes on; 10.2: everything is prepared, committed, and the version tag is **pushed** (you said "Push release tag, don't leave anything for me"); the version is `v0.1.0` (the first release of areas 1–2, not yet 1.0 since areas 3–11 are missing); 12.1: the plan is written and logged here, and building it waits for you.
7. **CLAUDE.md** now says "through the last one in the file" instead of "through the last one, 10.2", since the plan grew.

## 2026-10-25: step 9.7 (merge editor layout), decided by the agent and accepted by me

8. A file with conflict markers opened in the merge editor has its text **replaced by the merged result** (base plus the non-conflicting changes, conflicts empty or at base) as one undoable edit, left dirty; one Undo brings the markers back. A `--merge` result file keeps its existing text.
9. The **checkboxes** in the inputs' gutter are already clickable in 9.7 (VS Code shows them too); the code-lens-style accept buttons come with 9.8.
10. The **"This file has merge conflicts."** notice appears only when a file newly opens (not for restored backups or reopened tabs), with the button "Resolve in Merge Editor" (`git.openMergeEditor`, also in the palette).
11. Until 9.8, `emetos --merge ... --wait` ends with exit code 1 when the tab closes; 9.8 makes Complete Merge exit 0.

## 2026-10-25: step 9.8 (merge editor actions), decided by the agent and accepted by me

12. A **manual edit does not mark a conflict handled** (that is what VS Code's model does; the 9.7 SPEC text saying otherwise was corrected). Undo/redo of an accept gives the handled state back.
13. **Accept All** leaves conflicts that are already handled alone (VS Code overwrites them); **Reset Result** resets the conflicts only and keeps your edits outside them (VS Code rewrites the whole result).
14. After **Complete Merge** of a file with markers, the file opens in a normal text editor in the same group; for `--merge` the result is written and nothing else opens.
15. **Ctrl+Enter** completes the merge in the merge editor (VS Code's key); it wins over "Insert Line Below" only there.
16. The merge editor's labels show **file names only** (I sent the agent back to remove an absolute path from the Result header); the tab tooltip keeps the full path.

Changing it later is a change of `kit/appicon.go` and `go test ./internal/gui/kit -run TestAppIconPNGs -update-icons`.

## 2026-10-25: remote control (the user's design and answers)

33. **Remote control of one workspace** between two Emetos instances (milestone M15): A turns it on per workspace with a port and a list of allowed SSH public keys, kept in `~/.local/state/emetos/remote-control.json` and edited only through Emetos's "Remote Control" page; A listens only while it is on, with an idle timeout (30 minutes) and a visible "connected" banner with Disconnect. B opens a "Remote Workspace..." window tab with host, port and private key. B gets **its own view** of the workspace at its tab size (own cursor and scroll, same files), not a mirror of A's screen.
34. **Transport (the user's choice): QUIC over UDP by default, SSH for login.** B logs in over an embedded SSH server on A (TCP, `golang.org/x/crypto/ssh`: only listed keys, no shell, host key pinned like `known_hosts`, keys read directly with passphrase and `ssh-agent` support); inside that authenticated channel A gives B a long random session token and the fingerprint of A's QUIC certificate; B then opens **QUIC** (UDP, `github.com/quic-go/quic-go`, TLS 1.3 inside) on the same port number with the token, so the QUIC certificate is checked against the fingerprint learned over SSH. Picture tiles go as QUIC **datagrams** (latest picture wins, losses repaired by the next frame and a periodic refresh); input, clipboard and notifications go on reliable QUIC streams. If UDP is blocked, the session falls back to channels of the SSH connection (TCP). Both dependencies were approved by the user and added to CLAUDE.md's allowed list.

## 2026-10-25: the three requested extensions planned (M13–M15)

35. *(Withdrawn on 2026-10-28: the Agents extension was removed in step 17c.1, D26; this entry is kept as history.)* **The Claude Code extension is the "Agents" extension** (`extensions/agents`, SPEC §10.16, M13). Anthropic's terms forbid naming a third-party product "Claude Code" or using its logos, so the extension and its parts are named "Agents" and its first adapter "Claude CLI"; its tooltip says in plain text that it "runs Claude Code". It runs **your own, unmodified `claude`** with your own login (subscription or key): never `--bare`, never a separate config folder, and Emetos never reads, stores or passes a token; "Log in" runs `claude auth login`. The control messages (permissions, interrupt, model) are not formally documented, so the adapter detects features from what the program reports and is tested against pinned CLI versions. **For you:** Anthropic's terms allow "an end user signing in to the unmodified Claude Code binary with their own subscription", but they do not say outright whether an editor driving it counts as "offering claude.ai login"; for certainty Anthropic suggests asking them in writing. The terminal `claude`'s editor-integration server (the IDE lock files) is not built, since the session tab does not need it.
36. **Extensions get two new `extension/` calls**, because extensions import only `extension/`: `api.Editors().OpenProposedChange` (the diff editor for an agent's proposed edit, with Accept/Reject and the edited text returned) and `api.Workspace().NewHeadlessView(size)` (a second, off-screen view of a workspace for remote control). The Markdown renderer moves from `extensions/markdown` to `extension/ui/markdown` so the Agents extension can use it.
37. **Order:** M13 Agents (13.1–13.3), M14 Go (14.1–14.3, after M12's language-server steps, which step 12.1 writes) and M15 Remote control (15.1–15.4) come after M12. Running the real `claude` and the real `gopls` are tests behind build tags (`emetos_claude`, `emetos_gopls`), and the real `claude` one is a manual check because it uses your account.

## 2026-10-25: step 9b.5 (switching and arranging tabs), decided by the agent and accepted by me

39. **The switch picker** (`emetos.workspaceTab.switch`, also what `workbench.action.switchWindow` now runs) lists the active tab first marked `current`, then the others in strip order with the first of them selected, so Enter switches to it; each row has the name, ` ●` for unsaved changes, the folder (with `~`, long paths shortened from the left) and a non-default profile.
40. **A press on another workspace tab activates it on release, not on press** (9b.2 activated on press), so a drag starting on it does not swap the strip under the pointer; a drag never changes the active tab. Added context keys `workspaceTabIsFirst`/`workspaceTabIsLast` for the View › Workspace Tabs submenu.

## 2026-10-25: step 10.1 (performance), the missed targets decided by me

41. **Cold start and opening a 10 MB file keep their targets (400 ms, 300 ms) for your desktop with the GPU**; headless, where Glutter spends 560–620 ms trying Vulkan before GLES (its known issue), the cold start is judged by **Emetos's own share: under 100 ms** (measured 21–28 ms with no folder, 76–77 ms with four restored tabs) and the 10 MB open by its time over an empty window's first frame (110–140 ms). Your `scripts/perf.sh --session` run decides; if it misses 400 ms, the fix belongs in Glutter's renderer start.
42. **The memory target becomes the empty Glutter window plus 150 MB** (was plus 50 MB). After the fix of the tab font (which had made Glutter load all 62 monospace system fonts: 738 → 418 MB), Emetos is 138 MB over Glutter's counter, of which about 50 MB is the Go runtime of a bigger program, 12 MB code, and an unidentified 33 MB region. `GOGC=50` would save about 25 MB for more GC work; not done.
43. **Idle with the cursor blinking**: under 1% of a core with the GPU; headless, where llvmpipe's threads dominate, the rule is **at most 2 frames a second** (pinned by a test). Emetos's own idle work is 10 ms in 10 s.
44. **The synchronous tokenization budget is 4 ms per frame, shared by layout, paint and the minimap** (it was 6 ms for each of them, so a frame could spend 12 ms or more), so the first view of lines nobody tokenized yet takes 4.7 ms instead of 7.8 (SPEC §8.3); those lines are painted plain a frame longer.

The desktop entry lives in `assets/` and is installed by `scripts/install-desktop.sh`, which writes the absolute path of `emetos` (launchers often lack `~/go/bin` in `PATH`). The empty Explorer's "opening a folder will close all open editors" now shows only when `window.openFoldersInNewWindow` is `off`.

## 2026-10-25: step 11.1 (the extension contract), decided by the agent and accepted by me

46. **`extension/` is the pure contract** (it imports nothing of Emetos); the host that runs extensions is `internal/gui/exthost`, and `extensions.All()` is a function returning a fresh list. Activation events: `onStartup`, `onLanguage:<id>`, `onFile:<glob>` (relative to the folder), `onCommand:<id>` (every declared command is one too), `onView:<id>` and `onWindowTab:<kind>` (fired from steps 11.5 and 11.7); events already seen are replayed for an extension turned on later.
47. **A failing extension fails only in its tab**: a panic or error at any call into it (factory, `Activate`, command handler, status bar click, notification action, settings listener, posted function) is caught with its own tag, everything it contributed in that tab is removed, one error notification is shown with "Show Log", and the Extensions page says "Failed in <tab>". A disabled extension (`extensions.disabled`, an application-scope list of ids) registers nothing; Enable after a failure retries.
48. **The Extensions page** opens with Ctrl+Shift+X (`workbench.extensions.action.showInstalledExtensions`, also in View, Preferences and Manage) as an editor tab; it has no activity bar icon until side bar views exist (11.5). Extension keybindings sit over the default keymap and under the user's `keybindings.json`. `API.Post` was added so an extension's goroutines can hand work to the UI thread.

## 2026-10-25: step 11.2 (events), decided by the agent and accepted by me

49. **`Request` takes a reply callback** instead of returning `(Event, error)` as the spec said (a blocking call on the UI thread could freeze it for the 10 s timeout); a handler can answer later with **`HandleAsync`** (the Agents extension needs it). One handler per topic per scope; requests time out after 10 s unless the `ctx` says otherwise.
50. **Topics and patterns:** dotted, case-sensitive segments; `*` is one segment, a final `**` one or more. Fast topics (`emetos.document.changed`, `emetos.editor.selectionChanged`, and those an extension declares fast) arrive once per frame as a batch; order is kept per publisher and topic. Extensions cannot take the `emetos.*` ids. A handler or subscriber slower than 50 ms is logged.

## 2026-10-25: step 11.3 (event sharing between window tabs), decided by the agent and accepted by me

51. **A tab is named in a link by its folder** (the cleaned absolute path), so a link survives restarts; an extension window tab will be `<extension id>:<tab id>` (11.7). **A No Folder tab takes part in no sharing** (it has no stable name; links to it would pile up dead).
52. **The data's direction decides:** a request made in office for a handler in notes needs the link `notes → office`, and the reply comes back along it (the spec's "request along the link" would have let the prompt create a link that does not authorize the request; the spec is reworded). A cross-tab `Read` needs a link carrying `emetos.document.changed` and is never asked about (it is synchronous). A private workspace sends nothing, answers no request, allows no read out, is never asked about, and makes no requests to other tabs (a request carries its value out); it still receives along links.
53. **The prompt** shows in the receiving tab (it waits there; the window does not switch tabs): one question per pair of tabs at a time, Allow saves the link (merged into an existing one), This Session Only keeps it in memory, Deny (also Escape) stores nothing and is remembered until the extension is activated again. Events published before the answer are dropped. Removing a link on the page counts as a Deny for the extensions that listened to it.

## 2026-10-25: step 11.4 (language packs as extensions), decided by the agent and accepted by me

54. **Languages are a declarative contribution** (`Contributes.Languages`: an `fs.FS` with a VS Code `package.json`), registered by the host when the process starts, before any tab, instead of from `Activate` as the step said: files restored or given on the command line, and the grammar warm-up of step 10.1, need every language before the first workspace exists. 22 packs, one per VS Code extension folder (`emetos.golang`, `emetos.markdown`, `emetos.cpp`, ...; `emetos.gomod` on its own because its grammar comes from vscode-go). A pack is held per tab and removed from the process when no tab holds it; `extensions.disabled` follows each tab, so a pack disabled in one tab stays while another tab uses it.
55. **Language snippets are not bundled** (nothing read them before either); a later step can add them under the packs' `assets/`. Emmet stays in the core.

## 2026-10-25: step 11.5 (side bar views, panel views, built-in parts), decided by the agent and by me

56. **Extensions get Emetos's widgets through `extension/ui`**, a bridge package that re-exports a curated set of the kit (theme colors, text styles, icons, buttons, inputs, badges, checkboxes, lists, trees, progress bars) and is the only path from `extensions/` into the UI; `extension/` itself still imports nothing of Emetos (archtest rules `ExtensionContractImportsEmetos`, `ExtensionUIImportsInternal`). `extension/ui/uitest` lets extension authors write widget and golden tests.
57. **An extension's view is boxed**: clipped to its area, keys it does not use go on to the workbench, and a panic while building it shows "<Name> failed" with Show Log and fails the extension in that tab only. Glutter has no error boundary, so this holds for widgets built through `ui.NewBuilder` (a Glutter issue is logged).
58. **Narrow file and process access:** `Workspace().Files()`, a read-only `ReadFile` confined to the folder (`os.Root`, 2 MiB, UTF-8) and the `emetos.files.changed` event; extensions run processes with the standard library in their own process group, killed (TERM, then KILL after 2 s) when the tab closes. View state is saved per view in the window state and restored before the first build; which panel tab was open is not saved.
59. **No activity bar entry for the Extensions page** (decided by me): Ctrl+Shift+X and the View/Preferences/Manage menus open it; an activity bar icon that opens a page instead of a view container would be a new kind of entry for little gain.

## 2026-10-26: step 11.6 (editor tabs, title actions, file views), decided by the agent and by me

60. **The Markdown renderer lives in `extension/ui/markdown`** (decided by me), so the Agents extension (13.2) uses it without a move; code blocks are colored through a narrow `api.Languages().Tokenize` (tokens per line from the process grammar, at most 256 KiB) and the theme's colors. Web images are not loaded in the preview (privacy: the alt text shows); http(s) and mailto links open with the desktop portal, relative links inside the folder open in Emetos.
61. **A dirty extension tab is only a mark**: closing it asks nothing and there is no backup, because its data is the state the extension saves with the window. Splitting an extension tab makes a second instance with a copy of the saved state. "Open Preview to the Side" opens the same file in the group to the right (made if needed) in its preview view.

## 2026-10-26: step 11.7 (window tab extensions), decided by the agent and accepted by me

62. **An extension's window tab is its own scope holding only the extension that owns the kind**, named `<extension id>:<tab id>` for sharing (the tab id is saved, so links survive restarts); it gets no events from other tabs until the user allows a link. Its content fills the area under the title bar (no side bar, panel or status bar); the strip, the window buttons and the global keys stay.
63. **Closing it asks nothing** (its data is its state, dropped on close); the last-tab rule is unchanged (closing the last tab of any kind exits). **Disabling the owner keeps the tab and its state** with a notice in place of the content, which comes back on enable; a tab whose kind no longer exists is left out on restore but kept in the saved state. `emetos.windowTab.open` ("New Window Tab...") picks a kind from the palette.

## 2026-10-26: step 12.1 (the plan of area 3, language intelligence), decided by me

64. **Area 3 is specified in SPEC §9.12 and D20 and built in steps 12.2–12.9** (the step's "ask the user to review" decided by me, as you asked): one LSP client in `core/lsp` (stdio only, LSP 3.17, positions converted from byte columns to UTF-8 or UTF-16 as the server negotiates), a features model in `core/langfeatures`, servers declared by extensions and started per workspace tab (restarted after a crash up to 5 times in 3 minutes), Go providers for languages without a server, and VS Code's UI and keys for diagnostics, completion, hover, signature help, definition/references with the **peek view**, outline and symbol pickers, code actions with the light bulb, formatting (also on save, on type, on paste), rename with preview, code lens, inlay hints, semantic tokens, folding and selection ranges. `extensions/golang` declares `gopls` from step 12.3 so every step is proved on Go (`gopls` v0.24.0 is installed here); real-`gopls` tests carry the tag `emetos_gopls`. Not planned: TCP transports, call/type hierarchy views, inline (ghost text) completions, color decorators, linked editing.

## 2026-10-26: step 12.3 (language servers and their lifetime), decided by the agent and accepted by me

65. **A language server belongs to its extension's activation in a tab**: started on the first document of its languages (or at activation), restarted after a crash unless it is the fifth in 3 minutes (then a notification with Restart and Show Output), stopped with `shutdown`/`exit` within 3 s, then TERM and KILL. A missing program shows one "was not found" notification with Open Settings and starts once the path setting changes. `<extension>.restartServer` restarts all servers of that extension in the tab.
66. **A workspace edit that touches a closed file opens it as an unsaved document** in an inactive editor (as VS Code); the disk changes only when you save. Text-only edits are all or nothing; edits with file operations run in order and stop at the first failure; deletes and overwritten files go to the trash. Provider results are merged as VS Code does (completion lists concatenated, hovers stacked, locations joined, one formatter chosen by `editor.defaultFormatter`).

## 2026-10-26: step 12.4 (diagnostics), decided by the agent and accepted by me

67. **Problems follow vscode-languageclient**: pushed problems stay in Problems when their file closes (gopls reports the whole module), pulled ones go with the document, and all of a server's problems clear when it stops, crashes or restarts; closing an editor never removes problems. F8/Shift+F8 walk all problems by file, then severity, then position (hints skipped) and show the problem in a pinned hover instead of VS Code's inline navigation widget; Alt+F8 stays in the file.

## 2026-10-26: public keys by QR code (the user's request and choice)

71. **Allowed SSH keys can be added from the camera** in Remote Work: a **Scan QR Code** button next to pasting or choosing a `.pub` file opens a live camera dialog and adds the key after you confirm its type, fingerprint and comment; the client side has **Show My Public Key as QR Code** to scan from the host. The camera is read through Linux's V4L2 interface (`golang.org/x/sys/unix`, cgo-free), only while the dialog is open, and no frame is kept or sent. QR codes are decoded and encoded with **`github.com/makiuchi-d/gozxing`** (pure Go, Apache-2.0), the dependency you chose; it is added to CLAUDE.md's allowed list. New step 15.5. ## 2026-10-26: Glutter 1.8.1 (decided by me)

72. **Emetos uses Glutter v1.8.1 and drops the workarounds it makes unneeded**: the controls font (Glutter's font fallback replaces it), the tree rebuild (nodes now update in place), and `ui.NewBuilder`/`ui.BoxScope`, which are removed from the extension UI package. A `widgets.ErrorBoundary` around each extension view replaces them; an extension can no longer guard its own subtrees, because the whole view fails as one. The workspace tabs are WindowDecoration `Leading` widgets, so dragging them no longer moves the window; the width they may take is worked out from Glutter's private gap and button sizes (`docs/GLUTTER-ISSUES.md`). Headless proofs and perf runs use the GLES renderer by default.

## 2026-10-26: Glutter 1.8.3 (decided by me)

73. **Emetos uses Glutter v1.8.3, and `docs/GLUTTER-ISSUES.md` is deleted** because no entry is left. The workspace tabs are a loose `Flexible` `Leading` widget; Glutter 1.8.2 gives them the room in the bar. The peek view's rows use a plain ellipsis, which 1.8.3 cuts at a character.

## 2026-10-26: step 13.1 (the `claude` adapter; decided by the agent and accepted by me)

74. **The adapter passes the user's environment through unchanged** (plus `CLAUDE_CODE_ENABLE_SDK_FILE_CHECKPOINTING=true`).
    - It keeps `CLAUDECODE`, which Anthropic's Python SDK removes. As a result, an Emetos started from a terminal inside a `claude` session may find the program refusing to run nested; removing that one variable is a one-line change if you prefer the SDK's behaviour.
    - **"Rewind code to here" is offered on every session Emetos started**, because the documentation names no capability for file checkpoints, and a program that cannot restore shows its reason.

## 2026-10-27: step 15.3 (QUIC transport; decided by the agent and accepted by me)

75. **A QUIC certificate that does not match the fingerprint sent over SSH ends the connection attempt; it does not fall back to the SSH channel.** A mismatch means something on the UDP port is not what the host started, and the user should see that rather than get a working session that hides it. Falling back instead would be safe (the SSH channel is authenticated) and is a small change.
76. **The protocol keeps version 1 after adding message type 14 (`Missing`, a request for lost tiles)**, because no version was ever released. From the first release on, a change to the message types bumps the version.

78. **The remote picture is in logical pixels at scale 1**: on a HiDPI screen on B it is stretched, not sharp, because the protocol has no pixel ratio. Sharp HiDPI would need a ratio in Resize and pictures twice as large.
79. **A restored remote tab does not reconnect by itself**: it shows its connection form, filled in, and waits for Connect. During a session, a dropped connection (including A's SSH connection ending) is retried automatically with backoff.
80. **Keys that stay on B in a remote tab**: switching and closing window and workspace tabs, new window, quit, switch window and full screen, as bound on B. Every other key goes to A, including the command palette and Ctrl+S.

81. **Opening the Remote Work page opens each `/dev/video*` for a moment** (a `QUERYCAP`, which starts no capture and does not light the camera), so that the Scan QR Code button can say whether there is a camera. The camera itself is streamed only while the scan dialog is open.
82. **The camera is asked for 1280x720 in MJPEG (640x480 in YUYV)**, because the QR code of an RSA 4096 key is too dense to read reliably at 640x480.
83. **"Show My Public Key as QR Code" reads the public key from the clear header of an OpenSSH private key** when there is no `.pub` beside it, so no passphrase is asked; a PEM key still asks for it.

It refuses `..`, absolute paths, and links that lead out, and even absolute links that point inside the folder. `emetos.document.open` checks the file, then opens it through the normal reader, so a link swapped in between those two moments is not covered.

## 2026-10-27: Glutter 1.9.1 (decided by the agent and accepted by me)

94. **The Remote Work view keeps a clipboard of its own.** Glutter 1.9.1's offscreen view uses its parent's clipboard, which would have let a client read and replace the host's desktop clipboard (also from the find and quick input boxes). Emetos gives the view a parent binding of its own that never draws and whose clipboard is the view's (`clipboardParent`); copies go to the client through `OnCopy`. A Glutter option for the view's clipboard would remove it (`docs/GLUTTER-ISSUES.md`).

## 2026-10-27: step 17b.1 (real input; decided by the agent and accepted by me)

108. **Passphrase boxes show `*` instead of bullets.** Glutter places the caret of a hidden field by byte offsets, and the bullet takes three bytes, so the caret was drawn far to the left. With `*` it is in the right place. When Glutter fixes it, the bullets come back.
109. **Ctrl+A, Ctrl+Z and Ctrl+Y act on the input box that has the focus**, not on the open document, as in VS Code. So Ctrl+A then Ctrl+V in the command palette also replaces the `>`.

112. **Images in the preview are decoded at the size they are shown**, at most 800 px wide; 64 MB of decoded images are kept, and the least recently seen are dropped first.

## 2026-10-28: the terminal as in VS Code (decided by me after the user's request "make the terminal extension work like in vscode")

115. **What VS Code's terminal does by default on Linux is built** (three more steps, 17d.5 to 17d.7): shell integration with VS Code's own scripts (command marks, Ctrl+Up/Down between commands, sticky scroll, recent commands and folders, quick fixes), terminal IntelliSense (commands, files, history, and the completion specs VS Code bundles), terminals in editor tabs, icons and colors, and terminals that come back after a restart with their last 100 lines and a new shell.
116. **Still not built:** local echo (VS Code uses it only for slow remote terminals), moving terminals between windows, the accessibility and chat features of the terminal, and running tasks and debugging in terminals (those areas come later and will use this terminal).
117. **A completion spec that needs to run a command to find its suggestions** (for example the branches of `git checkout`) is not built in the first version; the fixed subcommands and options are.
118. **No xterm.js** (the user: "dont use xterm.js !!!"): the terminal's emulator is Emetos's own Go code, written from the published descriptions of terminal sequences (ECMA-48, the DEC manuals, xterm's `ctlseqs` document), with tests of its own and checks against real programs (`vim`, `less`, `htop`, `tmux`). VS Code's shell integration scripts are still used (they are VS Code's, not xterm.js).
119. **The terminal is built for speed, like Ghostty** (the user: "can you create a terminal that is also fast like ghosty"): output is parsed off the UI thread, cells are compact, and the whole grid is drawn in one call from a glyph atlas, as fast GPU terminals do; no code of Ghostty or another terminal is used. Its budgets are in SPEC §14 (for example `cat` of 100 MB in under 1.5 s with no frame over 16 ms), and each run is compared with foot, a fast terminal installed on this machine.

## 2026-10-28: step 17d.1 (the emulator; decided by the agent, to be accepted by me)

120. **Width is wcwidth's model.** East Asian Width wide and fullwidth take two cells (emoji with emoji presentation among them), Mn, Me, Cf and the Hangul medial and final jamo take none and join the cell before; a variation selector, a zero width joiner or a regional indicator does not change a width, and the emoji after a joiner gets its own cell. This is what programs compute with `wcwidth(3)`, so what they lay out is what the emulator draws; the modern "grapheme cluster" widths (VS16 widening, ZWJ families as one glyph of two cells, flags) would put a program's idea of a line out of step with the screen. Mode 2027 is answered as permanently reset. `width_gen.go` is generated from `golang.org/x/text/width` and the standard library, so there is no new data file or license.
121. **Where xterm leaves room I chose:** `IL` and `DL` move the cursor to column 1 (the VT510 manual does not say; DEC STD 070 does); a scroll of a region that is not the whole screen keeps nothing in the scrollback; ED 2 on the normal screen pushes the lines up to the last one that holds something into the scrollback and then clears (**changed in step 17d.2**, as VS Code, VTE and foot do: Ctrl+L in bash sends `CSI H CSI 2 J` and the earlier output must stay reachable; ED 3 still deletes the scrollback and the alternate screen is not changed; a `clear` command that ends with `ESC [ 3 J` still empties it); RIS keeps the scrollback; a wide character at the last column of a terminal with autowrap off is dropped; a combining mark at column 1 is dropped; the default cursor is a steady block and DECSCUSR 0 is a blinking block; the keypad in application mode sends the VT220 letters (`SS3 p` to `SS3 y`, `n` for the decimal point); F13 to F20 are sent as Shift+F1 to Shift+F8, which is what `kf13` to `kf20` of the `xterm-256color` terminfo entry say (the VT220 codes `CSI 25 ~` ... would not match the entry programs read); a `:` separates the parameters of every CSI.
122. **The hyperlink is part of the style** (an OSC 8 link changes the style index of the cells it covers), not a side table of the row as SPEC §10.25 "Speed" first said. The table of styles and the table of links hold 65,535 entries each; when one is full the styles no cell uses are dropped (a scan of every cell, at most once per 65,535 refused entries) and then 24-bit colors are rounded to the 256-color cube and, last, the default style is used, so the memory is bounded and a rainbow of 100,000 colors costs 100 MB/s, not a freeze.
123. **The throughput tests have floors for the two inputs §14 gives no budget for** (scrolling in a region 200 MB/s, cursor motion 60 MB/s) **and for the worst case of the style table** (40 MB/s), about a quarter to a half of what was measured, so that a regression shows without a noisy test; the three budgets of §14 are held as written (the best of five runs of 8 MiB).
124. **Answers to the program:** DA1 is `CSI ? 1 ; 2 c` ("VT100 with Advanced Video Option", which every program accepts), DA2 is `CSI > 0 ; 276 ; 0 c` (xterm's patch level 276, the one `vim` and others test for), XTVERSION `DCS > | Emetos(version) ST`; `CSI 18 t` and `19 t` report the size in characters; the window operations that move, resize or report pixels are ignored (a program cannot move the editor's window); OSC 52 goes to a callback that the host sets, and does nothing otherwise (as VS Code's default).
125. **Serialization** starts with RIS and `ESC [ 3 J` so that it can be fed to a terminal that was used; it does not carry the saved cursor or the character sets, and in origin mode a cursor outside the scroll region goes to the nearest margin (origin mode cannot place it there). It keeps a row's autowrap only if the next row holds something (a stale flag left by an erase is dropped), and it writes `ESC [ K` after each row so that cells the pen painted when the row scrolled in are default again.
126. **The tests of the programs keep what they recorded** (`testdata/programs/*.raw`, written by `-update-goldens` next to the `.golden`), and `TestReplayRecordings` replays it without the programs, so the goldens are checked on a machine that has none of `vim`, `less`, `tmux` or `git`; the live test skips when a program is missing. `htop` is checked by layout (its screen changes every second). The programs run in a temporary home with `TERM=xterm-256color`, `-u NONE` for `vim`, `-f` of our own for `tmux`, `GIT_CONFIG_*` pointing nowhere.

## 2026-10-28: step 17d.2 (the terminal panel; decided by the agent, to be accepted by me)

127. **Ctrl+` is bound to Toggle Terminal, which the extension itself handles.** An extension cannot run a command of the core, so the extension's `toggleTerminal` shows the terminal with the keyboard when it has none and hides the panel (`ViewHandle.Hide`, new) when the terminal has it. The core's `workbench.panel.extension.<id>.toggle` stays for the View menu. `Hide` also gives the keyboard to the editor, which is what the last terminal ending needs (VS Code hides the panel then).
128. **`ViewContent.AllowCommand` and `Contributes.ContextKeys` are new in the extension API.** VS Code decides whether a key goes to the shell or to the workbench with `commandsToSkipShell`, so the window must ask the view while the focus is in it; the context keys of the terminal have VS Code's own names (`terminalFocus`), which the rule "a key starts with the id of the extension" refused. The host accepts a bare name only when the manifest declares it, and only for that extension.
129. **A terminal that exits is removed, whatever its status** (VS Code closes it; a failure status other than 0, 129, 130 and 143 says so in a notification: `The terminal process "bash" terminated with exit code: 3.`). The last one to go hides the panel and the view does not make another until the panel is shown again; a view shown with no terminal makes one (VS Code does the same when the panel opens).
130. **The multi-line paste warning is a bar over the top of the terminal** (Paste and Cancel), not a dialog: an extension has no dialog, and a bar keeps the focus where it was. A paste of several lines into a shell that asked for bracketed paste does not ask (`enableMultiLinePasteWarning` auto), as VS Code.
131. **The tabs list takes the pointer, not the keyboard** (`terminalTabsFocus` is always false): rows are not focusable, the terminals are reached with the commands (Ctrl+PageUp, Ctrl+PageDown) or a click; `terminal.integrated.tabs.enabled` false shows no list and no dropdown (VS Code shows a dropdown). Rows show the hover buttons on the pointer's row and on the active row.
132. **A split is side by side only.** The panel of VS Code splits by the position of the panel (side by side at the bottom, stacked at the side); Emetos always puts the panes side by side with a sash (`resizePaneUp` and `resizePaneDown` are registered and do nothing). Sizes are proportional to the window.
133. **The theme reaches the emulator** (`SetTheme`): the default colors and the palette the program learns with OSC 4, 10, 11, 12 are the theme's, and a change of theme resets what the program changed. Cells that name no color are drawn with the theme's colors, so a light theme draws dark text.
134. **Settings declared and not acted on yet:** `allowChords` false (chords always go to the window), `confirmOnKill`, `confirmOnExit`, `smoothScrolling`, `enableBell`, `textBlinking` partly (the cursor blinks, blinking text does not). They have VS Code's defaults, so the behavior of the defaults is VS Code's.
135. **The glyph atlas is rasterized with Glutter's software rasterizer from paragraphs drawn in white** (`docs/GLUTTER-ISSUES.md`): Glutter has no glyph rasterizer, and this keeps the text engine's hinting and fallback fonts. A glyph is rasterized once per font; the cost of a frame does not grow with the styles of the text.
136. **The performance budgets are measured headless with the software renderer here** (`scripts/terminal-perf.sh`); the GPU numbers of "Terminal frame" (raster 4 ms) wait for the pass on the desktop (`scripts/terminal-perf.sh --desktop`). The measured terminal is 231 x 52 cells (the maximized panel of a window of 1920 x 1080), larger than the budget's 160 x 45; foot ran at both sizes. Ghostty is not installed.

## 2026-10-28: step 17e.1 (Notes, TODOs and Scratchpad removed; decided by the agent, to be accepted by me)

140. **The export reads on the main goroutine and writes in a goroutine.** `Process.loadSavedNotes` runs in `Run` before the tabs are made, because the tabs write the state again at once and the tab of a removed kind leaves the list of the tabs; it only reads, as `startupPlan` does. The files are written by `savedNotes.export` in a goroutine that `Workspaces.bind` starts when the window is up, with a context that a quit does not cancel, and the notification is made on the UI thread in the window that is shown first. A test and the dev server that do not call `Run` do not export.
141. **A Scratchpad tab with no title of its own is `Scratchpad.md`, not `Scratchpad Scratchpad.md`.** SPEC §10.26 says `Scratchpad <tab title>.md`, but the saved title of the tab is empty unless the extension set one (Scratchpad never did), so the rule gave `Scratchpad Scratchpad.md`. A tab with a title that is not "Scratchpad" is `Scratchpad <title>.md`. A title that is a file name's enemy is cleaned: `/`, `\`, control characters and invalid UTF-8 become `-`, dots at the start are dropped, the name is cut at 150 bytes at a character, and an empty one is `Untitled`. A clash gets ` 2`, ` 3`, across the notes of one window, of several windows and of several profiles (all `profiles/*` folders of the state are scanned, not only the default one).
142. **When something goes wrong, the user is told and the next start tries again.** A failure to make the folder or to write a file is one error notification and one log line and writes no marker. Where there is nothing to save, nothing is written, not even the marker (SPEC §10.26), so every start looks at the state files again; a file that mentions neither `emetos.notes` nor `emetos.scratchpad.tab` is not parsed, so the cost is the reading of the files. A state file that mentions one and cannot be read or parsed is one warning in the log at every start, and its notes are not saved.
143. **A second test extension carries the tabs.** `extensions/internal/testext/tabs.go` (`emetos.testtabs`, `testext.NewTabs`) has an editor tab kind, an editor title action, a window tab kind and a subscription to the events of other tabs, and `tabs_test.go` holds what the tests of Notes and Scratchpad held of the contract. The first test extension (`emetos.testext`) is untouched: its test uses a hand-made API that has none of these parts.
144. **Display tests.** The theme picker (Ctrl+K Ctrl+T) takes the place of the Notes title in the fast keys test; the Remote Work window tab (a host and a port) takes the place of the Notes and Scratchpad boxes in the real input tests; the Remote Work tab takes the place of the Scratchpad tab in the window tab tests; D10's welcome-button test is removed with its note in `docs/DESKTOP-PASS.md`. No test types several lines into an editor tab any more: the note's body was the only such box.
145. **Fixtures.** `testdata/proof/11.7` (the Scratchpad proof) is deleted. `testdata/proof/11.5` and `11.6` stay: the first also holds the Tasks proof, the second the Markdown file views. The TODO comments of `testdata/screenshots/projects/tools` stay; they are Go code for `go vet`.
146. **The README names Search as the example of the side bar view kind.** After 17e.1 no extension had a side bar view, so the README named none; the Search extension (17e.2) is the first again, and the README names it (changed at the rebase of 17e.2).

## 2026-10-28: step 17e.2 (Search across files; decided by the agent, to be accepted by me)

150. **The engine walks the folder itself** (`internal/core/search`), on one goroutine that lists files while a worker per CPU (at most 8) reads and matches them, so the first result comes within a few milliseconds. It does not use the workspace's file index to walk, but takes its list of files (instead of walking) when the walk of the index is done and the search is the plain one (the settings' excludes and ignore files on); the index has the same exclusions applied. Hidden files are searched, as VS Code's `rg --hidden` does, and `.git` is never entered. A file over 64 MiB is skipped (`Summary.TooLarge`), a file with a NUL in its first 8 KiB (and no byte order mark of UTF-16) is binary and skipped, and a lone `\r` is not a line break.
151. **The container of the view is `emetos.search.container`, not VS Code's `workbench.view.search`**, because the ids of the containers and views of an extension start with its own id (`CheckManifest`). The command `workbench.view.search` ("Show Search") shows it, so VS Code's id still works in a keybinding. VS Code's context keys (`searchViewletVisible`, `hasSearchResult`, ...) are set through a new field of the manifest, `Contributes.ContextKeys`, which lets an extension set the keys it names whole besides those under its own id (the field came with the terminal in 17d.2; at the rebase 17e.2's second copy of it was dropped and the terminal's design kept, whose checks are `ExtensionContextKeyDeclInvalid` and `ExtensionContextKeyDeclDuplicate`); the view's `searchViewletVisible` is true once the view was built (the host does not tell when it is hidden).
152. **The include and exclude boxes follow VS Code's `parseSearchPaths`** for the segments it can honour in one folder: `./src` is relative to the folder, a bare `src` or `*.ts` is searched for anywhere, a path inside the folder is made relative, and `../x`, `~/x` and a path outside the folder are left out (the search never leaves the folder).
153. **Replace as VS Code's, with what the API has**: the matches of a file that an editor has open are replaced with `Documents().Apply` (one undo step for the file; the cursors keep their place) and the document is then saved through the core topic `emetos.document.save`, as VS Code saves after a replace; the files that no editor has open go through a new `Workspace().ReplaceInFiles`, which reads the file as the editor does and writes it back atomically with the same encoding, byte order mark and line ending, and skips a match whose text is not the one the search found (the file changed since). Replace All asks first with a quick pick (**Replace** / **Cancel**), as VS Code asks with a dialog.
154. **The replace preview is the diff editor of `Editors().OpenProposedChange`**: a click on a match, while the replace box has text, opens the file as it is against the file with all of its replacements; **Accept** replaces the file, **Reject** or closing does nothing. VS Code's preview is the same two sides, with its own accept path.
155. **Find in Folder... is a command of the core** (`filesExplorer.findInFolder`, Shift+Alt+F in the Explorer, group `4_search` of the Explorer's menu), not of the extension, because only the window knows which folder was clicked; it runs the extension's `workbench.action.findInFiles` with `filesToInclude` set to `./<folder>`, and says so in a notification when the extension is off. The commands of the Search view act inside the workspace only, so clients may run them (`clientpolicy_test.go` allows the new command on purpose).
156. **Not built in this step**: Open to the Side (`search.action.openResultToSide`, the API has no side option for `Documents().Show`), the right-click menu of the results, file icons in the rows, the "Open in editor" link and the Search Editor, Quick Search, AI search, history of the boxes across restarts (Up and Down go through the values of the session), `search.sortOrder: modified` (the search does not know the times of the files, so it sorts as `default`), and `search.seedOnFocus`. The "Open Settings" link of the message shows a quick pick with the one row that opens the settings, because an extension has no call that runs a command of the editor directly.
157. **`search.maxResults` empty (`null`) means 20000**, not unlimited as in VS Code: the results are kept in memory and in the tree. The description of the setting says so.
158. **The results reach the UI thread at most every 30 ms** (one `api.Post` for a batch, then a gap of 30 ms), and each take rebuilds the nodes of the tree; the longest turn measured with 20,000 matches in 2,000 files streaming in is 10.7 ms (`TestTheUIThreadStaysUnderAFrameWhileAHugeSearchStreams`). A result list is not paginated.
159. **A regular expression that does not compile is reported with the error of `internal/core/regex`** (its message under the search box, in the input's error color); the engine has no tag of its own for it, because an error that came from that package is returned untouched (CLAUDE.md "Error handling"). A search whose context ends has the tag `SearchCancelled`.

## 2026-10-28: step 17e.3 (Find All References and Find All Implementations; decided by the agent, to be accepted by me)

160. **The language calls of the API are asynchronous** (`Languages().References(ctx, uri, pos, done)`, `Implementations`): the request runs on a goroutine and `done` is called on the UI thread, like `LanguageServers().ExecuteCommand` and `Events().Request`, so an extension never blocks the UI thread by waiting for a server (the brief said "positions in, locations out, with the `ctx`"; a blocking call would deadlock when an extension makes it from a command handler). It asks the registry of the window tab that Go to References asks, so a server and a provider in Go are merged as everywhere.
161. **Four more small additions to `extension/`**, each needed by the view and none by anything else yet: `Documents().Active()` (the extension is activated by its command, after the editor events it would have followed, so it asks for the cursor), `Clipboard().WriteText` (VS Code's `env.clipboard.writeText`, for Copy, Copy Path and Copy All; write only), `ViewHandle.SetTitle` (VS Code's `TreeView.title`: the view says "References" or "Implementations") and the context menu in `extension/ui`. The Search view (17e.2) first followed the events of the editor for the cursor and had its own clipboard writer in `extension/ui`; at the rebase the two were joined: it reads the cursor with `Documents().Active()` and copies with `Clipboard().WriteText`.
162. **Ids of the view and the context keys start with the extension's id** (`emetos.references.container`, `emetos.references.tree`, `emetos.references.isActive` and the others): §10.15 requires it of every extension and `Context().Set` refuses other keys. The commands keep VS Code's ids (`references-view.findReferences` and the rest), since they are what the user's `keybindings.json` and the docs of VS Code name.
163. **The view is not hidden before the first search.** VS Code's view has `when: reference-list.isActive`, which hides it until a search is made; the activity bar of Emetos shows every container, so the icon would open an empty side bar. The view is always there and says "No results." (and lists the history), which also matches §10.28 ("the view's empty state lists them").
164. **"No results." is VS Code's string and does not name the symbol**; §10.28 said "with the symbol's name", but VS Code has no such text (its peek view says "No references found for 'x'", which Emetos's editor commands still do). The history lists the word that was searched.
165. **Not built**, as the list of §10.28 says plus what has no place in Emetos yet: the highlights of the matches in the editors (an extension has no decoration API), dragging a result out of the view, `references.preferredLocation` (it changes `editor.action.showReferences`, the CodeLens command, which Emetos has not), the icon of the file in a file row (the kit's file icons are not in `extension/ui`).
166. **A count badge per file**, as §10.28 asks, though VS Code's tree shows none; and inline Dismiss and Rerun buttons that show on the row the pointer is over or that is selected, as VS Code's inline actions do. The Delete key does not dismiss (VS Code has no key for it).
167. **The label of a location is cut by characters, not UTF-16 units** (8 characters back, up to 331 after; VS Code counts units): the difference shows only for an emoji. The copy text and `Documents().Show` count UTF-16 units, as VS Code does and as the API says.
168. **Shift+Alt+F12 runs Find All References** and no longer the peek (`editor.action.referenceSearch.trigger`), because an extension's key wins over a built-in one of the same chord (as in VS Code with the references view); the peek stays in the context menu's Peek submenu and in the palette.
169. **F4 and Shift+F4** are bound by this extension under `emetos.references.hasResult`; the Search view (17e.2) binds F4 too under its own key, as VS Code does. Both are active at once only when both views have a result; the later one registered wins then. If that happens in use, one of them should ask for the focus (`focusedView`) in its `when`.

## 2026-10-28: step 17d.3 (find, links and the other commands; decided by the agent, to be accepted by me)

170. **The terminal's find widget is built in the extension from `extension/ui` pieces, not the editor's.** The editor's widget and `core/find` are in `internal/`, which an extension cannot import (SPEC §5.3), so the terminal has VS Code's simple find widget of its own (input with Match Case, Whole Word and Regular Expression inside it, the count, Previous, Next, Close). It has no replace row and no find in selection, as in VS Code's terminal. The switches needed a toggle; `extension/ui` exports `Toggle`, `ToggleOpts` and `NewToggle` (the Search view of 17e.2 built the same export over `kit.Toggle`, and the two were joined into one at the rebase).
171. **Find patterns are Go regular expressions (RE2), not ECMAScript's.** Lookahead and backreferences do not compile and find nothing (the input's border turns red). `core/regex` (regexp2) is in `internal/` and cannot be used by an extension, and adding regexp2 to the extension's imports would break the boundary. The user can ask for another route if the missing syntax matters.
172. **Enter in the find input is Find Previous and Shift+Enter is Find Next**, as the pinned VS Code key table has it (`terminalFindInputFocused`); F3 is next and Shift+F3 previous. The first current match after typing is the last one before the old position (the last of the buffer), as VS Code's incremental find goes backward. Matches are capped at 20,000 and the count then reads `20000+`.
173. **`Settings().Update(key, value)` is new in the extension API** (user file only, only for a setting the extension declares or registered; `ExtensionSettingNotOwned` for any other). The "Allow {scheme}" button of the OSC 8 notification and "Select Default Profile" need to write a setting, and an extension had no way to; a general `Settings` writer for any key would let an extension change settings of others, so it is limited to its own. Checked at the rebase: the Search view (17e.2) and References (17e.3) write no setting, so this is the only way an extension writes one.
174. **A file link is a regular file under the workspace folder; folders, word links and the multi-line detectors are not built.** VS Code reveals a folder in the Explorer or opens a window, which an extension cannot ask for; word links need the file-name search of quick open, which the API has not (the Search view of step 17e.2 searches the text of files, not names); the multi-line detector (ripgrep and git hunks) and the cell-attribute detector are left out. Symbolic links are followed on both sides, so a link that leaves the folder is no link; with no folder there are no file links (VS Code would open any existing path). **`searchWorkspace` is built** (at the rebase onto the Search view): Ctrl+Shift+F with text selected in the terminal runs `workbench.action.findInFiles` with `{query, triggerSearch: true}`. An extension cannot run another extension's command without the privilege `commands.execute`, and a request to a topic of the Search extension would not activate it (an extension is activated by its commands and views only), so the Terminal declares that one privilege and the host's allowlist (D21) maps `emetos.terminal` to `commands.execute` alone. The alternatives were a new API call that runs a command of the editor (a bigger surface) or activating the Search extension at startup (a cost for everyone).
175. **File links are checked on a goroutine and the answer is kept 10 seconds** (128 lines), so the UI thread never does file I/O while the pointer moves; a Ctrl+click on a line whose check is still running is kept and completed when the answer arrives. Links are resolved against the folder the shell told with OSC 7, the start folder when it told none, and `~` against the home folder; per-line folders wait for shell integration (17d.5).
176. **An OSC 8 link follows the new setting `terminal.integrated.allowedLinkSchemes`** (VS Code's default list); another scheme gets a notification whose button **Allow {scheme}** adds it to the user's settings and opens the link. Web and file links the shell prints as text are not subject to the list.
177. **`runSelectedText` and `runActiveFile` show the panel and leave the keyboard where it is** (VS Code's `revealActiveTerminal(true)`), and make a terminal when there is none. The selected text goes as a bracketed paste when the shell asked for that; the path is quoted as VS Code's `preparePathForShell` does. The editor in front is followed through the core events, and when none has told of one yet (the command activated the extension) it is asked of `Documents().Active()` (step 17e.3's call, used at the rebase).
178. **`sendSequence` resolves the variables a terminal can know** (`${workspaceFolder}`, `${file}` and its parts, `${relativeFile}`, `${lineNumber}`, `${selectedText}`, `${env:NAME}`, `${userHome}`, `${pathSeparator}`); the others stay as written. With no `text` argument it asks for it and understands `\n`, `\r` and `\xAB`.
179. **Link ranges and tests: the cells are the truth.** VS Code's tests for web links in Chinese text expect ranges that come from xterm's arithmetic and are one cell off for wide characters; the ported tests use the true cell ranges. The Windows and WSL cases and the file-service harness of VS Code's local detector tests are skipped (Emetos targets Linux).

## 2026-10-28: step 17d.4 (the terminal's events and requests; decided by the agent, to be accepted by me)

184. **The output stream is pieces with `seq`, and a snapshot is exact.** The observer runs under the lock that guards the emulator's `Write`, so a snapshot (pending text published, then the screen serialized) is the screen after piece `seq`. A piece is at most 64 KiB or 50 ms of output, always valid UTF-8 (invalid bytes become U+FFFD by the maximal-subpart rule; an incomplete tail is carried over). A consumer that sees a gap or a lost piece asks for a new snapshot. Cost, measured with `BenchmarkFeed` (64 KiB chunks of colored text, 160x45, no `-race`): 533 MB/s with no observer and 437 MB/s with one that publishes (the validation and the copy into the piece); both are above the 400 MB/s budget of SPEC §14 for the parser, and the publishing runs on the 50 ms timer, except for a piece that fills 64 KiB, which is published by the terminal's own goroutine.
185. **What is typed at a password prompt is never published.** `emetos.terminal.input` carries `hidden: true` with no `data` when the pty's `ECHO` is off **and** `ICANON` is on (`pty.Process.LineMode`), or when the mode cannot be read, so a password typed by the user or by an extension does not reach the events, nor the log. Why both flags: `getpass`, `sudo`, `ssh`, `passwd` and `read -s` read a password in canonical mode (line by line) with the echo off. A line editor (bash's readline, zsh's line editor, fish) and a full-screen program (vim, less, htop) put the terminal in raw mode (`ICANON` off) with `ECHO` off and echo by themselves, so ECHO alone hid every key typed at an ordinary prompt and no event of normal typing was ever published (changed 2026-10-29; the first version of this decision looked at `ECHO` alone). In raw mode the data is published, whatever `ECHO` is: the program draws what it wants on the screen anyway, which the output events carry.
188. **A terminal made by a request (`create`) is quiet unless the request says `show`:** it does not take the keyboard or open the panel (`cwd` is confined as every path of §10.19).
189. **The emulator got two changes for the serialization** (`AppendValidUTF8`, and the pen restored when DECSET 1049 leaves the alternate screen), found by the tests that compare a snapshot plus the events with the terminal's cells with seeded random output. The test compares cells, not the serialized bytes, because the serialization of the same screen differs in soft-wrap flags.

## 2026-10-28: step 17d.5 (shell integration; decided by the agent, to be accepted by me)

190. **The fish script is sourced with `TERM_PROGRAM` set to `vscode` for the time it loads.** VS Code's `shellIntegration.fish` refuses to run unless `$TERM_PROGRAM` is `vscode`, and Emetos's terminals have `TERM_PROGRAM=emetos`. The injected `--init-command` is `set --global TERM_PROGRAM vscode; source "<dir>/shellIntegration.fish"; set --global TERM_PROGRAM emetos`, so the script runs unchanged and the shell keeps the value programs see. The same trick is not needed for bash, zsh and pwsh, whose scripts only look at `VSCODE_INJECTION`.
191. **Ctrl+Shift+Up / Down are bound twice, with a context key to choose.** VS Code binds them to `scrollUpLine`/`scrollDownLine` and, when shell integration has commands, to `selectToPreviousCommand`/`selectToNextCommand`. Emetos declares the context key `terminalShellIntegrationActive` (true while the focused terminal has shell integration), and the second binding wins when it is true. `TestTheManifestDeclaresWhatItNeeds` now checks the first binding of a key.
192. **Navigation and selection by command are my own design.** VS Code's behavior sits in `markNavigationAddon`, which is an xterm addon and so off limits. Emetos's version: Ctrl+Up / Down put the previous or next command's line at the top of the view, starting from the command nearest the view (or the last one navigated to), clamped at the bottom of the buffer; past the first command the view goes to the top; the selection commands extend by whole commands and by lines from an anchor. The details differ from VS Code's at the edges (what "nearest" means when the view starts in the middle of a command, how far a selection by line goes). If you want VS Code's exact edge behavior, say what it is and it can be matched.
193. **A new terminal does not inherit the folder of the active one; only a split does.** The SPEC's list said "the one a split or a new terminal starts in", but VS Code's `splitCwd` setting applies to splits only, and a new terminal uses `terminal.integrated.cwd` or the workspace folder. I followed VS Code. The SPEC line stays as it is in the list; "As built" says what is built.
194. **A command line is trusted when the shell reports it with the nonce, nothing more.** The `E` sequence carries the line and the nonce `VSCODE_NONCE` that Emetos made for this terminal; a wrong nonce means that what is on the screen (a program printing the sequence) must not set the command line, so the line is read from the screen text between the end of the prompt and the cursor. VS Code does the same; the confidence (high, medium, low) is kept as in VS Code. Emetos does not hide the sequences from the program (a program can still print `OSC 633;A`), it only keeps the command line out of its reach.
195. **The quick fixes of PowerShell read the lines of the command's output (`outputLines`), and Free Port types the command again without running it.** VS Code matches PowerShell's "Suggestion [cmd-not-found]" and General errors on the last lines of the buffer; Emetos reads the lines between the executed marker and the end of the output, which is the same text without the prompt. Free Port ends the processes (SIGTERM) that listen on the port and then types the failed command again for the user to run, as VS Code does (it sends the command without Enter).
196. **Where the files go.** The scripts are written to `$XDG_CACHE_HOME/emetos/shell-integration/<hash of their content>/` when a shell starts (a changed script gets a new folder; old ones are not removed, they are small), and the history of earlier sessions is `terminal-history.json` in the state folder (`$XDG_STATE_HOME/emetos/`), mode 0600, written atomically. Both are looked up from the terminals' environment, so a test with isolated XDG folders never touches the user's.
197. **Copy Output as HTML puts HTML text on the clipboard as plain text.** Glutter's clipboard has plain text only (`docs/GLUTTER-ISSUES.md`), so the HTML is not offered with the `text/html` type that a rich editor would paste as formatted text. The markup is a `pre` with the terminal's foreground and background colors and a `span` per run of cells that has a color or style. **Changed with Glutter 1.9.7:** its clipboard carries `text/html` (`services.ClipboardData.HTML`), so Copy Output as HTML now offers the markup as `text/html` next to the output as plain text, and a rich editor pastes the formatted text.
198. **PSReadLine's modules are not bundled.** VS Code ships `psreadline` next to the pwsh script for its screen-reader mode on Windows (binaries). The script runs without them; the mode it serves is not in Emetos.
199. **The recent-command quick pick has no separators and no buttons.** `extension.QuickPickItem` has neither, so the groups of VS Code's list (this session, previous sessions, shell history) are told apart by the description of each item (the folder and `exitCode: N`, `previous session`, the history file), Alt+Enter (type without running) and the buttons that remove a command or show its output are not built. The extension API did not change for this; adding separators and buttons to the quick pick is a small API step if you want them.

## 2026-10-28: step 17e.4 (hosting a workspace from one place; decided by the agent, to be accepted by me)

200. **Only Remote Work gets the hosting banner.** SPEC §10.29 says the workspace shows "This workspace is shared with Remote Work (1 computer connected)" with Stop Hosting. Remote Work has the hosting banner from the moment hosting is on (and its session banners as before, with Disconnect). (The first version of this entry also covered the second hosting extension, which had no banner of that kind; the user had it removed on 2026-10-29, see 410 to 419.)
201. **The extension of the item is `emetos.hosting` (named "Sharing"), not `emetos.sharing`.** The id `emetos.sharing` is kept for the events of Emetos (`ExtensionIDReserved`). The item is `extension.emetos.hosting.status` in the bar, its command (the menu, also in the palette as "Sharing: Show Hosting Menu") is `emetos.hosting.menu`, and a client may not run it (`command.ClientRefusal` refuses the prefix `emetos.hosting.`). The SPEC says `emetos.sharing.status`; the id is not shown to the user.
202. **Two small additions to the API of `extension/`:** `StatusItem.Kind` with `StatusRemote` (the look VS Code gives its remote indicator: `statusBarItem.remoteBackground`, `remoteForeground`, `remoteHoverForeground` and `remoteHoverBackground`, ported with their defaults from `workbench/common/theme.ts`), and `Workspace().OpenWindowTab(kindID)`, which opens the window tab of a kind or shows the one that is open (a second click on a Settings row makes no second tab). An extension cannot run another command, and the `+` menu opens a new tab each time.
203. **The guide is always on the page, with check marks, and the keys come first.** It does not go away when all three steps are done: the addresses and the fingerprint are what the user needs again when a second computer is to connect. The page of Remote Work is now: guide, "1. Allowed keys", "2. Host this workspace", Connection (port and idle timeout), Security; the section "Host key of this computer" is gone (its fingerprint is in step 3).
205. **Host with no key, or with a file that cannot be used, opens the page or the tab and does nothing else** (no notification, nothing turned on): the guide is the answer. With a key listed, Host turns hosting on at once and says so in a notification; with it on already it says "already hosts"; in a window with no folder it says it cannot.
206. **The guide lists the address of the interface that has the default route first, leaves out the bridges of containers and virtual machines, and lists the other addresses after it, IPv4 first (changed on 2026-10-28; it first listed every interface that is up).** The first version listed the Docker and libvirt bridges too ("Emetos cannot tell which interface the other computer reaches"); on a machine with containers that put four unreachable addresses among one that works, and the screenshot of the proof showed all of them. Now: (1) the interface of the default route (the lowest metric in `/proc/net/route` and `/proc/net/ipv6_route`), listed even when its name looks like a bridge; (2) the other interfaces that are up, not loopback and not a bridge (names `docker*`, `br-*`, `virbr*`, `veth*`, `cni*`, `flannel*`, `podman*`, `lxcbr*`, `lxdbr*`, or an address in a range only such bridges use when the name says nothing: 172.17.0.0/16, 192.168.122.0/24, 10.88.0.0/16, 10.244.0.0/16), IPv4 first, IPv6 (in brackets) after, without link-local, multicast and unspecified addresses; (3) when nothing is left, every up non-loopback address, so the guide is never empty. The plain line under the switch names the first, the guide lists all with a Copy button (the clipboard of the desktop, through Glutter's binding, in the page's build). The list is read each time the page is built, and a test passes a fake list (`HubOptions.Interfaces`), so the tests are the same on every machine. A custom Docker network in 172.18.0.0/16 and up is left out by its name (`br-*`) only; a user-defined bridge with another name and another range is listed, and the user can ignore it.
207. **Remote Work got a dev bind variable, `EMETOS_DEV_REMOTEWORK_BIND`** (debug builds and builds with `emetos_dev` only): its TCP and UDP listeners then stay on one address, so that the real-input tests of this step do not open a port on the network. A release build always listens on every interface.
208. **The Sharing item is in every workspace, also one with no folder;** there its tooltip says why it cannot be shared and a click says so in a notification. A window tab of an extension has no status bar, so no item.
209. **The client is told the same title for every end by the host's switch:** "The host stopped sharing this workspace." for Remote Work (`ReasonOff`), with the host's reason as a sentence under it ("Hosting was turned off.", "The port changed.", "Emetos quit."). The protocol is not changed: the text of the Disconnect already carried the reason; only the words the client draws changed.
230. **The proofs and the tests never show an address of the machine they ran on (2026-10-28).** The repo is public. `EMETOS_DEV_INTERFACES` (debug and `emetos_dev` builds only, like `EMETOS_DEV_REMOTEWORK_BIND`, decision 207) replaces the list of interfaces with a JSON list, and the real-input tests of step 17e.4 set it to the documentation ranges of RFC 5737 (192.0.2.10, 198.51.100.7) and two bridges that the guide leaves out. A release build ignores it.

## 2026-10-28: step 17d.6 (terminal IntelliSense; decided by the agent, to be accepted by me)

210. **Nothing opens by itself with the default settings.** The plan's test says typing `gi` offers `git`, and the SPEC says `quickSuggestions` has "VS Code's defaults", but the pinned VS Code (1.143, `terminalSuggestConfiguration.ts`) defaults `quickSuggestions` to `{commands: off, arguments: off, unknown: off}` and `suggestOnTriggerCharacters` to false: IntelliSense opens with Ctrl+Space only until the user turns it on. Emetos has the same defaults, and the real-input test of typing `gi` sets `commands` and `arguments` on in its settings (another test checks that nothing opens with the defaults, and that Ctrl+Space opens the list). **To change it** (the list opening as the user types, which is what most people expect and what the plan's wording says): the default of `terminal.integrated.suggest.quickSuggestions` in `extensions/terminal/settings.go` and `TestSuggestSettingsHaveVSCodesDefaults`, `TestNothingOpensByItselfWithTheDefaultSettings` and the display test of the defaults.
211. **The editor's suggest list is shared through `kit` and `extension/ui`, and the editor now draws its rows with the shared code.** The widget was tied to the editor's view model, so the list (rows, icons, bold matches, the details at the right, the scrollbar, the frame) moved to `kit/suggestlist.go` and the editor's `suggest_widget.go` builds its rows with it; the placement, the details pane and the keys stay with each user. The goldens of the editor's widget did not change. `extension/ui` exports `SuggestRow`, `SuggestListOpts`, `NewSuggestList`, `NewSuggestMessage`, `NewSuggestBox`, `SuggestMatcher`, `SuggestWidth` and `SuggestVerticalPadding` (the smallest set the terminal needs; `SuggestMatcher` is there because the scorer is in `core`, which an extension cannot import, and it has to be reused between calls: a new scorer allocates 150 KB).
212. **49 of the 115 completion specs are bundled (444 KB), converted to JSON by a Go parser of the TypeScript the specs use.** The choice is the commands a developer types most (git, npm, npx, code, cd, go and the coreutils and tools in `scripts/update-assets.sh`); `yarn` (94 KB), `pnpm`, `less`, `docker` (149 KB), `cargo` (176 KB), `gh`, `azd` (180 KB) and about 60 more are left out to keep the program small, and adding one is a name in that script. The generators (code that runs a shell command) are not converted and never run (D117), so the branches of `git checkout` are not offered; the files are, when asked (D214). The converter has no dependency and its tests are in `scripts/specs2json/`. The builtins of bash are made at update time from the bash on the machine that runs the script (`compgen -b`, `help -d`, no rc file, an empty environment); the ones of zsh and fish are VS Code's description caches.
213. **Options are offered when the word starts with a dash, or when the command has nothing else to offer.** VS Code's parser lists subcommands and options together; with the word empty that puts a hundred flags among the subcommands of `git`, which sort among them by name. A list for `git ` shows the subcommands, and `git -` the options. I could not see VS Code's own behavior here; say if you want them together.
214. **A spec applies when the command can be run, and the files of the fallback are offered only when asked.** As VS Code requires, a spec is used only when the command is a builtin or a program of `PATH`. When nothing is known about the argument (no spec, or an argument with only a generator) the files and folders are offered for Ctrl+Space and, for an automatic list, when `quickSuggestions.unknown` is on (VS Code's meaning of `unknown`).
215. **The keys are my own reading of VS Code's list, because its contribution file is an xterm addon that I did not read.** Tab accepts, Enter accepts after a move (`selectionMode` `partial`), Escape closes, Up and Down move (a step wraps, a page stops), Up on the first row before a move is the shell's own Up (`upArrowNavigatesHistory`), Ctrl+Space with the list open opens the details, and a list the user opened with Ctrl+Space behaves as `always`. The ids of the commands are VS Code's (they were already in the default `commandsToSkipShell`). `toggleSuggestionDetailsFocus` is not built.
216. **The input model reads the screen and tracks no key.** The line is the text between the end of the prompt (the marker of shell integration) and the end of the text after the cursor; the shell's own suggestion (dim, bright black, a gray) is cut off by the style of its cells. So history, editing keys and the shell's completion need no code. It does not handle a right prompt on the cursor's row or the continuation prompt of a multi-line command (it is part of the text), and its ghost-text rule is a guess about the colors that zsh-autosuggestions and fish use; the shells other than bash are not installed here, so only the screen reading is tested for them, from hand-made output.
217. **Backspace updates an open list and does not open a closed one.** Typing opens it (a paste of more than 16 bytes does not); deleting a word with a list that opened by itself closes the list. I did not know VS Code's behavior; it is the editor's.
218. **The providers have the ids `terminal-suggest` (commands, builtins, specs), `path` (files and folders) and `history`** for `terminal.integrated.suggest.providers`. VS Code has other ids (`lsp`, the extension's and the shell's), and its history comes from the shell's inline suggestions; Emetos offers the lines of the history it keeps (this session's, the earlier sessions' and the shell's file) that start with what was typed, after everything else, replacing the whole command.
219. **What I read of VS Code, said plainly.** Read: `terminalSuggestConfiguration.ts`, `terminalCompletionModel.ts`, `terminalCompletionItem.ts`, `terminalCompletionService.ts`, `terminalSymbolIcons.ts`, `simpleCompletionModel.ts` and `simpleCompletionItem.ts`, and the `terminal-suggest` extension (`terminalSuggestMain.ts`, `tokens.ts`, `shell/*.ts`, `fig/figInterface.ts`, `fig/autocomplete-parser/parseArguments.ts` and the specs); none of them is xterm.js or built on its objects. Not read: `terminalSuggestAddon.ts` and `terminal.suggest.contribution.ts` (xterm addons) and the prompt input model of the capabilities folder (it reads xterm's buffer). One slip: a `grep` for the word `quickSuggestions` over the whole `suggest/` folder printed three matching lines of `terminalSuggestAddon.ts` (the places where `quickSuggestions.commands`, `.arguments` and `.unknown` are read, 540 and 541 among them); I did not open the file, and what I took from those lines is only what the setting's description says (commands: the first word, arguments: the rest, unknown: the fallback files).

210. **The proofs and the tests never show an address of the machine they ran on (2026-10-28).** The repo is public. `EMETOS_DEV_INTERFACES` (debug and `emetos_dev` builds only, like `EMETOS_DEV_REMOTEWORK_BIND`, decision 207) replaces the list of interfaces with a JSON list, and the real-input tests of step 17e.4 set it to the documentation ranges of RFC 5737 (192.0.2.10, 198.51.100.7) and two bridges that the guide leaves out. A release build ignores it.

## 2026-10-29: step 18.1 (the engine client and the container test harness; decided by the agent, to be accepted by me)

240. **The pinned images are in `extensions/internal/containerapi/containerapitest/images.go`, not in `internal/testenv/images.go`.** CLAUDE.md allows "the step's equivalent": an extension may not import `internal/` (`internal/archtest`), and the harness lives with the client. `ImageBusyBox` is `docker.io/library/busybox:1.37.0` (written with the registry, so that Podman does not ask which one); `EnsureImage` refuses an image that is not listed, and a test checks that each listed image has a version tag and no `latest`. The pulled image stays in the engines' stores (it is shared with whatever the user does; the tests never remove an image, only a name they added).
