241. **Which engine, for Docker: the context picked in the view wins over everything, and the first source that is set is the only one asked.** SPEC §10.20 lists setting, `DOCKER_HOST`, current context, default socket; I added the picker in front (the user chose it in the UI), and I made the order exclusive, as the Docker CLI's is: a `containers.host` or `DOCKER_HOST` that does not answer is an error that says so, and Emetos does not fall through to the default socket and show another engine's containers. For Podman the user's socket, then the rootful one, are tried in turn (nothing was named), and the service of Emetos's own is started only then: not when `podman.host` or `CONTAINER_HOST` is set, and not with `NoService`. The rootless Docker socket (`$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/docker.sock`) is not a default (the Docker CLI does not look at it either; its installer makes a context).
242. **The versions.** Docker: `min(server, 1.47)` in the paths, and 1.41 is the least; an engine whose `MinAPIVersion` is above 1.47 is refused (`ContainerAPIVersionTooNew`) rather than spoken to with a version it may drop. Podman: the paths stay `/v4.0.0/libpod/...` for every Podman 4 and 5 (the engine accepts any 4.x there; the version of the engine is read from the `Libpod-Api-Version` header and `/libpod/version`); a Docker engine asked as Podman is `ContainerAPIEngineMismatch`. Checked against Docker 29.9.2 (API 1.56) and Podman 5.8.9.
243. **A stream that the caller cancels returns nil.** `Events`, `Logs` and `PullImage` end with nil when their ctx is cancelled (the caller asked for it) and with an error otherwise; `Events` also reports `ContainerAPIStreamEnded` when the engine closes the stream and no `Until` was given, so that the view reconnects instead of going quiet. `EventsOptions.Ready` exists because the first test showed the race it closes: an event made right after the call started could be missed. A cancelled non-stream call is an error that keeps `context.Canceled` (`errors.Is` works).
244. **The Podman service is tied to Emetos's life with `Pdeathsig` and a held thread.** `PR_SET_PDEATHSIG` fires when the *thread* that started the child ends, so the child is started by a goroutine that locks its OS thread and does not return until `Stop` (the thread then ends with it). If Emetos is killed, the kernel ends the thread and the service gets SIGTERM; the next start also removes an old socket of the same name. The service's socket is `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/emetos/podman-<pid>.sock` (or a private folder in the temporary folder when there is no runtime folder); the real-Podman test uses a private short folder instead, so it never writes into the folder of Emetos's own socket.
245. **What the tests against the real engines refuse to do, beyond CLAUDE.md.** They skip when the engine is at a `tcp://` host (they make containers, and only a local engine is meant for that); the harness refuses a bind mount of a host path, a port on any address but 127.0.0.1, a name without `emetos-test-`, and an image that is not pinned; every cleanup inspects the thing and refuses to remove it if the label is missing; the listing for the leftover check uses only the label filter; and prune calls are *not* in the suite that runs against real engines (a prune acts on whatever the engine finds unused, a label filter notwithstanding, so it is tested against the fake only). Run on 2026-10-29 on this computer: Docker 29.9.2 and Podman 5.8.9 (its own service on a private socket), both green, nothing labelled left (`IMPLEMENTED.md` has the commands).
246. **What the two engines really do, as the fake copies it.** Seen with the real engines on 2026-10-29 and kept in the fake and in the tests: Podman answers 500 (`ContainerAPIEngineError`), not 409, for removing a running container without force; its list says `Status` "" and gives `StartedAt`, Docker's gives `Status` "Up 2 seconds" and no start time; Podman writes the entrypoint of an inspect as a string, Docker as an array; Docker's `die` is Podman's `died`, and removal is `destroy` against `remove`; libpod's pod list has `Containers[].Names` as a string and its inspect has `State`; Docker's exec start works on a plain HTTP request (no hijack) and both write the multiplexed stream. The wire types read these differences (`flexTime`, `flexStrings`) and never fail a whole list for one odd field.
247. **`scripts/container-test-cleanup.sh`.** Removes, per CLI found, the labelled pods (Podman), containers (`rm --force --volumes`), networks and volumes (`--force`), in that order, and stops at the first list that fails. It never prunes, stops (other than as part of `rm --force`), or touches images. `--dry-run` prints what it would remove; `--engine` picks one CLI; `$DOCKER` and `$PODMAN` name the programs (the tests of the script use a fake CLI over the fake engine). An id or name that does not look like one is refused rather than passed on. The images the tests pulled stay.
248. **`DockerContexts` leaves out a context it cannot read** (the picker should not fail because one meta file is damaged); choosing it by name still reports why (`ContainerAPIContextInvalid`). The Docker configuration files are read up to 1 MiB.
249. **`tcp://` without TLS is allowed**, as in the Docker CLI (`DOCKER_HOST=tcp://host:2375`); TLS is on when `DOCKER_TLS_VERIFY` is set, the scheme is `https://`, or the context says so. The view of 18.2 should say plainly that such a connection is not encrypted; this step only provides the client.

## 2026-10-29: step 19.1 (connections, the keyring and the results grid; decided by the agent, to be accepted by me)

250. **The three database extensions share an extension of their own, "Databases" (`emetos.databases`, in `extensions/internal/dbconn`), listed by `extensions.All()`.** The SPEC only names shared packages, but an extension can declare a view container only under its own id and there is one activity bar icon for all engines, so the container, the "Connections" view, the commands and the form's editor kind belong to one extension. The SQL, MongoDB and Redis extensions register a `dbconn.Driver` with the one `dbconn.Hub` (made in `extensions/all.go`). A connection whose engine has no active extension is listed with "its extension is not active" and cannot be connected.
251. **The hub reads nothing until the view is first shown.** Not even `db-connections.json` is read at startup (SPEC §14 startup budget); `Hub.Store()` loads it on first use. A file that cannot be read leaves the store empty and read-only, with the reason in the view, and nothing is written over it.
252. **The Secret Service client encrypts with `dh-ietf1024-sha256-aes128-cbc-pkcs7` and falls back to `plain`.** The standard's encrypted algorithm is used whenever the service accepts it (a server key outside 2..p-2 is refused); `plain` is used only when the service refuses the first. Each operation opens its own D-Bus connection (blocking, so off the UI thread). The default `Dial` fails with `KeyringTestBusRefused` under `go test`, so no test can reach the session bus of the user by mistake; tests use `secrettest` (a private `dbus-daemon` under `timeout -k`, and a fake service on it). Items have the attributes `application=emetos`, `kind=database-connection`, `connection=<id>` and the label "Emetos: <name>".
253. **A password is asked in a password box: `InputBoxOptions.Password` (new in `extension/`).** VS Code has the same option. Without it an extension could not ask for a password without showing it. It is passed through the exthost, `quickinput.PromptOptions.Password` and `Picker.Obscure` to the kit's obscured input.
254. **Password order and fallbacks:** the variable the connection names (`passwordEnv`, `${env:NAME}`), what was typed in this session (kept in memory until Disconnect, forgotten when the connection fails), then the keyring. When the keyring cannot be used at save time, the password is held in memory for the session and a warning says so (D24); at connect time the user is told the keyring did not answer and is asked. A password pasted into a URL moves to the password field. A connection file with a password anywhere in it (URL user information, `password=`/`pwd=`/`token=` in a query or a `Key=value;` part, an option named like a secret) is refused whole.
255. **A new refusal class for clients of a hosted workspace, "outside" (`extension.RefusedOutside`):** `command.ClientRefusal` refuses the prefix `emetos.databases.` with it, because the host's connections and keyring are not for a guest. Step 18.1 will add its own prefix to the same rule, so the two branches may conflict in `internal/core/command/clientpolicy.go` and `extension/core.go` (a one-line merge).
256. **NULL, binary and times in the grid:** NULL is shown as `NULL` (dimmed, italics) and copied as the text `NULL`; the CSV export writes it as an empty cell by default (or the text given), the JSON export as `null`. Binary data shows `0x` and the first 16 bytes with its size, and is copied and exported as its whole `0x` hex. Times are RFC 3339 with the fraction they have. A long text is cut after 200 characters (hover shows 2,000; a copy has all).
257. **Sorting is on the client, by a precomputed key per row and a stable sort; it clears the selection.** Text compares case-insensitively with a byte tiebreak, numbers as numbers, NULL first; a column of mixed kinds orders the kinds (`rank`) so the order is total. A third click on a title returns to the source order. Rows that arrive later (`Appended`) keep the selection and are sorted into place.
258. **Copy and export formats:** Ctrl+C is TSV (a value with a tab, a line break or a quote is quoted as a spreadsheet does; a single cell is copied as it is); Ctrl+Shift+C adds the titles. The exports are CSV (RFC 4180) and JSON (array of objects; a title used twice gets `_2`; NaN and infinities as strings) and run row by row from a goroutine that stops when its `ctx` ends. The SQL `INSERT` export and the save dialog are left to step 19.3 (they need the dialect and a `FileChooser` that saves; it has only Open).
259. **Small additions the step needed:** `ui.NewScrollable` and `ui.ScrollableOpts` in `extension/ui` (the editor's overlay scrollbars over Glutter scroll views driven by a controller; the grid's rows come from a builder); the form has a **Test Connection** button (the driver connects and disconnects, nothing is saved); the command `emetos.databases.newConnection` takes `{engine}` but the fields prefilled from a container (`emetos.containers.connectDatabase`) are not built here, because no container extension exists in this branch.

## 2026-10-29: step 18.2 (the Docker extension: the explorer and its actions; decided by the agent, to be accepted by me)

260. **The questions before a removal are a box at the top of the view, not a quick pick or a dialog.** The extension API has no modal dialog; Search asks with a quick pick, but a quick pick cannot be looked at in a golden, shows only labels, and floats away from the thing it asks about. The box is in the view of the section (the view is revealed), says the count, the names and the sizes, has one button per choice and Cancel, and a second question replaces the first. If you prefer the quick pick, `ask` in `containerui/actions.go` is the one place.
261. **Logs are one panel view with a tab for each container; Inspect, History and files are editor tabs.** SPEC says "a panel tab per container", but a panel tab has to be declared in the manifest, so there cannot be one for each container. The panel "Containers Logs" has its own strip of tabs (buttons that wrap) and the same view shows the output of Run Command. The read-only documents are editor tabs of one kind (highlighted JSON, Copy All); a long line is cut at the edge (the list has no horizontal scroll).
262. **The sections are five views in one container, and Images, Networks and Volumes appear only while an engine answers.** As Container Tools has them. Contexts stays (Docker) so that a context can be picked while the engine is missing. Networks, Volumes and Contexts start folded; a Compose project is open when it is first seen.
263. **Command ids are Container Tools' with the prefix in place of `vscode-containers`:** `containers.containers.start`, `containers.images.run`, `containers.pruneSystem`, `podman.containers.start` for Podman. The prefix is the Config's.
264. **Dangling images are shown by default and marked; a title action hides them.** Container Tools hides them by default; SPEC says "dangling images marked". The choice is saved with the view. Intermediate images are listed too while they are shown (`all=true`), as Container Tools does.
265. **Explore Files is a folder tree under a running container, read through `ls -1Ap` and `cat`.** No archive endpoint: exec works on both engines, and a container with no `ls` (distroless) says so in a notification and its folder closes. A file over 1 MiB is cut and says so; a binary file is not shown. What was listed is kept until Refresh or until the container stops.
266. **The sizes in a question come from `system df` and from the lists, per item only for images and volumes.** The API gives no size for a single container, so the question for containers has the count and names (and the writable layers of all stopped containers for Prune); an image's size is its own size, with a line that layers other images share stay. If `df` fails the question is asked without sizes.
267. **The Pull progress is in the Images view, not in a notification.** The notification API has no progress. The view shows a row with the last word of the engine and a bar (percent of the layers that told their size), with a cancel button; a notification says how it ended.
268. **`Config.ListFilters` lets a test list only what it made.** The real-engine test must not list the user's containers; the engine filters (label `dev.emetos.test=1`, the pinned image). It is empty for the real extension. Also: `extension/ui` gets `TokenLooker`, and four `debugIcon.*` colors are registered (rule 7) for the state icons.
269. **Two small corrections to the fake of 18.1:** it now sends the `untag` and `delete` events of an image (real engines do, and the view follows events only), and a container added in the state "exited" has just finished (its status was "Exited ... 15 billion years ago"). The proof uses a small program, `containerapitest/fakeengine`, that serves the fake on a socket; the setting `containers.host` points the extension at it, so no `emetos_dev` knob was needed.

## 2026-10-29: step 18.3 (Docker: build, Compose, run, push, scaffolding and language servers; decided by the agent, to be accepted by me)

300. **Compose and Push stream into tabs of the logs panel too, not only Build.** SPEC says the build output goes into a panel tab; Container Tools runs all of them as tasks in the terminal. Emetos has one place for the output of a program the extension runs (the "Containers Logs" panel, a new tab kind), so Compose Up, Down, Restart, Logs, the Compose group and Push use it: the command line is the first line, stderr is red, the status line says whether it ran, finished or exited with a status, and closing the tab stops the program (SIGTERM to its process group, SIGKILL after 3 s). Compose Logs and `up` without `-d` run until the tab is closed. Only Run Interactive and Open Shell need a terminal and use the Terminal.
301. **The Explorer's context menu gives the commands of extensions the pressed row and the selection, and sets `resourceLangId` for its rows (host change).** An item of an extension in `MenuExplorerContext` had no way to know which file it was run on, and no `when` could ask for a Dockerfile. `internal/gui/app/fileops.go` now sets `resourceLangId` (what the editor would call the file; empty for a folder), `resourceFilename`, `resourceExtname` and `resourcePath` for the row, and runs the commands that an extension declares with `{"uri", "uris"}` added to the arguments the item has when they are an object (`extension.ResourceURIs` reads them; `exthost.Scope.IsExtensionCommand` tells an extension's command from the editor's own, which take no such argument). The editor's context menu needed nothing (`editorLangId`, and the command reads `Documents().Active()`). This is an addition to `extension/` and to the window that no later step has to repeat.
302. **"Select Services" is a quick pick that toggles.** Container Tools uses a multi-select quick pick; the quick pick of the extension API chooses one item. Each pick of a name toggles it and shows the list again, and the first item, "Done: N of M chosen", ends the choice (Escape cancels; nothing chosen says so). The services last chosen for a file are kept in memory for the session only (Container Tools keeps them in the workspace state). A real multi-select in the API would be a better answer for this and for Podman's "create a pod from selected containers" (18.4): I did not add one.
303. **`emetos.terminal.create` got `show` (a change in the Terminal extension, M17d's).** The Terminal makes the terminals that other extensions ask for "quiet": not active, panel not shown, keyboard not taken (it was written for the clients of a hosted workspace). Open Shell has to be seen, and an extension cannot show another extension's panel; running `workbench.action.terminal.focus` would need the `commands.execute` privilege, which the host gives to the Terminal alone, and I did not widen that map. So `create` takes `show: true`, which does what the person at the computer opening a terminal does: a request without it stays quiet. Two lines in `extensions/terminal/events.go`, one test, one sentence in §10.25. If you would rather not have it, the alternative is a privilege or an event the Terminal handles to reveal itself.
304. **Down, Down (select services) and Restart ask first, and need the engine; Start, Stop and Restart of a Compose group do not ask.** Down removes containers and networks, so it follows the safety rule of §10.20 (the question names the containers of the project that the engine knows, by the `config_files` label for a file, and says volumes are kept); Restart of a file is a down followed by an up, so it asks too. Stop and Restart of a running group keep everything (as Stop and Restart of a container do, which do not ask). The question is the box of the Containers view (decision 260), which is revealed when a command runs from a file; for that reason these commands need the engine and say "Docker is not running" otherwise.
305. **The language servers are declared when their programs are found on PATH when the extension is made.** The manifest is a value, made when Emetos starts, so "found on PATH" is decided then: a server installed later is declared at the next start. The path settings (`containers.dockerfileLanguageServer.path`, `containers.composeLanguageServer.path`) only replace the program of a server that is declared, so a program that is reachable only through the setting is not found. I considered declaring both servers always, with `OwnUI` so that a missing program is silent, which would make the settings useful; the step and SPEC say "declared only when found on PATH", so I did that.
306. **Scaffolding covers the SPEC's list and nothing more.** Platforms: Node.js, ASP.NET Core, .NET Console, Python (Django, FastAPI, Flask, General), Go and Other (a plain image). Left out of Container Tools' list: Java, C++ and Ruby (SPEC names Go, Node.js, Python, .NET and a plain image), the Windows images of .NET, `compose.debug.yaml` and the debugging configuration (no debug adapter for containers), and "Add Compose Files to Workspace" for an existing Dockerfile. Changes to the ported text: `**/Containerfile*` is in `.dockerignore` next to `**/Dockerfile*`; the default port of ASP.NET Core is 5000 (Container Tools reads a random one from `launchSettings.json`); Python's Django `wsgi` module is looked for in the folders next to the entry file, and the Flask and FastAPI modules take every slash of the path as a dot (Container Tools replaces the first only). `requirements.txt` is never replaced; every other file that differs is asked about one by one (Overwrite, Overwrite All, Cancel). The Containerfile name for Podman is a field of the Config, used by 18.4.
307. **Build details that SPEC left open.** The command line is Container Tools' default, `build --pull --rm -f <file> -t <tag> <context>`, with the program written as `docker` in the tab (the path `LookPath` found is what runs). `BUILDKIT_PROGRESS=plain` is set so that the output is the same text with or without a terminal. The tag is asked in an input box (the folder's name, lowercase letters and digits, `:latest`; the last one given for the file is offered again in the same session) or given as `{"tag": "app:1"}`, which is also how the proof builds without a person to answer. A problem whose file cannot be found (the build named a file outside the folders it was looked for in) is put on the Dockerfile that was built; output with no position (a missing Dockerfile, a failed pull) makes no marker, only the notification. The marker covers the whole line.
308. **`scripts/container-test-cleanup.sh` now also removes labelled images, and the harness lists them as leftovers.** This supersedes the sentence of 247 that the script never touches images: Build Image makes images, and an `emetos-test-<random>` image that a failed test left would otherwise stay. The list is `image ls --quiet --filter label=dev.emetos.test=1` (an image with two names is listed twice, so the script removes each id once), removed with `image rm --force` after the containers, never `--all`, never prune. `Harness.RemoveImageAtEnd` removes an image by its name when the test ends, after looking at its label and refusing a name without the prefix; the fake gained `AddLabelledImage`, and the cleanup test has labelled and unlabelled images in it.
309. **The real-engine tests of this step.** `TestBuildImageOverTheRealDocker` builds a Dockerfile of the test (`FROM` the pinned BusyBox, `LABEL dev.emetos.test=1`) into `emetos-test-<random>:latest`, checks the label and the view, and builds a second Dockerfile with a mistake to see that the real BuildKit's message becomes a marker on line 3; `TestComposeUpAndDownOverTheRealDocker` runs `compose up` of a one-service project in a folder named `emetos-test-<random>` (so that is the project name; the service and the network carry the label), checks the container, answers the Down question with Cancel (nothing changes) and then with Down. The cleanup of the Compose test is `docker compose -p <name> -f <file> down` for that project only. `docker build --pull` asks the registry about the pinned tag, so the build test needs the network once the image is there; both tests skip when the `docker` program (or its compose plugin) is not installed. The tests run the CLI with the environment of the test, which is the user's, so they use the same engine and context as the harness found.

## 2026-10-29: step 19.2 (SQL drivers, dialects and the schema explorer; decided by the agent, to be accepted by me)

270. **`emetos.sql` is the language pack and the driver in one extension; one driver object serves every window tab.** The SPEC names `emetos.sql` "SQL Databases", and the SQL language pack already had that id, so the pack's manifest is kept (languages, activation on startup) and extended with the command and the engines. `sql.New` is gone: `sql.NewHub(dbHub)` makes the one `Service` and driver (in `extensions.All()`, like Remote Work's hub) and `sql.FactoryOf(hub)` the extension of each tab. Each tab registers the same driver in the Databases hub, which meant two small changes to `dbconn`: `dbconn.APIDriver` / `Hub.ActionsIn(api, ...)` (a menu entry that opens an editor must open it in the tab of the menu, and `Actions` has no tab), and a driver's unregistering no longer closes the connections of an engine that another registration still serves (closing one tab used to close the connections of the others). The events `emetos.sql.connected` / `disconnected` carry `{"connection": id}` (the ID, not the name: a name is the user's text and the ID is what other extensions can look up) and are published by each tab's extension in its own scope, from the callbacks of the `Service`. `emetos.sql.` is refused to clients of a hosted workspace (class `outside`), so the prefix is on the same rule line as `emetos.databases.`: a merge with step 18.1's prefixes is one line.
271. **Four engines, MySQL and MariaDB as one.** `postgres`, `mysql` ("MySQL / MariaDB"), `sqlite`, `sqlserver`. The two MySQL servers share the driver and every catalog query (`information_schema`, `SHOW CREATE`), and the real-server tests run both. The engines' own fields: "Show system databases and schemas", "Connect timeout" (default 15 s), SQL Server's named instance and SQLite's "Create the file if it does not exist". A SQLite file must be absolute or `~/...` (a relative path would depend on where Emetos was started) and is opened `mode=rw` unless the connection says create, so that Test Connection and a mistyped path never make an empty database. Every driver is used through its `driver.Connector` and `sql.OpenDB`: nothing is registered with `database/sql` (it is process-global state), and the pool is 5 connections with 2 kept idle.
272. **Levels, system objects and large schemas.** PostgreSQL: databases > schemas; MySQL: databases; SQLite: schemas (the attached files; `main` is shown, so an attached file is browsable); SQL Server: databases > schemas. PostgreSQL's connection serves one database, so browsing another one opens a pool for it with the same settings and password (the password stays in the closure of the connector, in memory, until Disconnect, as D24 allows). System databases and schemas, and SQLite's `sqlite_*` tables, are hidden unless the connection has the field "Show system databases and schemas" (DBeaver does the same; the SPEC did not say). A listing is cut at 10,000 objects and says so in a last row, so a schema with a hundred thousand tables does not build a hundred thousand rows. Partitions of a PostgreSQL table are listed as tables.
273. **TLS modes map to each driver as follows** (the mode names are PostgreSQL's): PostgreSQL passes them to pgx; MySQL builds a `tls.Config` (`verify-ca` is a custom chain check without the name, which the driver cannot do by itself); SQL Server: `disable` is `encrypt=disable`, `prefer` is `encrypt=false` with the certificate trusted (found by test: against SQL Server 2022, which encrypts by default, `encrypt=false` alone checks the self-signed certificate and fails), `require` is `encrypt=true` with the certificate trusted, and **`verify-ca` and `verify-full` both check chain and name**, because go-mssqldb has no way to check one without the other. Real servers cover disable, prefer and require; verify-ca and verify-full are tested against a local TLS server with a generated CA (right CA, other CA, wrong name).
274. **Read-only is the engine's own mechanism where it has one, run on every new connection of the pool, and only a hint for SQL Server.** `SET default_transaction_read_only = on`, `SET SESSION TRANSACTION READ ONLY`, `PRAGMA query_only = ON` run through a connector wrapper (`initConnector`) that closes the connection when a statement fails; SQLite opens `mode=ro` too. SQL Server has no such statement: `ApplicationIntent=ReadOnly` is sent (only when a database is named; go-mssqldb refuses it otherwise) but it only tells an availability group where to route the session, so a read-only SQL Server connection still accepts writes until step 19.3's own check refuses them (SPEC §10.21 "Safety" already says "and the session is set read-only where the engine has it"). The real-server test for read-only is skipped for SQL Server with that reason.
275. **Cancel runs `pg_cancel_backend` / `KILL QUERY` from another connection for PostgreSQL and MySQL, and ends the context for SQLite and SQL Server.** The SPEC says "pgx's cancel request, MySQL's KILL QUERY": pgx's default on a context that ends is to close the connection (a deadline in the past), which would end an open transaction, so pgx is also given `CancelRequestContextWatcherHandler` as a fallback, and the session id (`pg_backend_pid()`, `CONNECTION_ID()`) is read when a `Session` is taken. `go-sql-driver/mysql` closes the connection when a context ends and the server goes on with the statement, so `KILL QUERY` is the only real cancel there. The `Session` type (`Begin`, `Cancel`, `Close`) is new in this step because the dialect's cancel needs it; step 19.3 builds the query editors on it. Real servers cover a one-minute statement cancelled after two seconds and the session used afterwards, for all four.
276. **Generated statements are safe to run unfilled, and SQL Server names have three parts.** INSERT has `NULL` values with a remark per column; UPDATE and DELETE end in `WHERE key = NULL` (never true) or `WHERE <condition>` (a syntax error) when the table has no primary key; Drop is only ever text in an editor. CREATE is the catalog's own statement where the engine keeps one (MySQL, SQLite; views and routines everywhere) and is written from the catalog for PostgreSQL and SQL Server tables (columns, NOT NULL, DEFAULT, the primary key as a named constraint, foreign keys, the other indexes). SQL Server statements say `[database].[schema].[name]`, as VS Code's SQL Server extension does: a connection is in one database at a time and the explorer lists them all (found by the real-server test: `SELECT TOP (1000) * FROM [sales].[orders]` ran in `master`).
277. **New Query and Select Top 1000 open an unbound untitled `sql` editor in this step.** Binding to a connection and database, and running the statement, are step 19.3's (`// TODO(step 19.3)` marks both). The API an extension had for this did not exist: `Documents().OpenUntitled(languageID, text)` is new in `extension/` (implemented over the same code as File > New File; the fake API records it). The text is set as the first state of the document (`Document.SetText`: the undo history is empty, so Ctrl+Z cannot empty the editor), and, as VS Code's, an untitled document with a text counts as changed.
278. **The filter is the tree's typed find in "filter" mode, in the shared view.** `ui.FindMode` and `ui.FindModeFilter` are exported from `extension/ui`; `dbconn`'s view sets the mode, so the MongoDB and Redis explorers have the same filter. It filters the rows that are read; the SPEC says "lazy loading, a filter, Refresh" and a filter that read every closed table of every schema would defeat the laziness. `dbconn.Extension.TreeRows` and `ExpandTree` are exported for the tests of the engine extensions (they cannot reach the view's tree model; `dbconn`'s own tests do).
279. **The real-server tests of the SQL extension run on step 18.1's harness, and there is no SQL Server container.** `sqltest.Runner` starts a server for a test; `sqltest.Docker()`, `Podman()` and `OnHarness(h)` implement it over `containerapitest` (it replaced the first version, which called the `docker` program). The rules are the harness's: pinned images listed in one place (`containerapitest/images.go` now also lists `postgres:17.6-alpine`, `mysql:8.4.6` and `mariadb:11.4.8`), `emetos-test-<random>`, label `dev.emetos.test=1`, `127.0.0.1` only, no mounts, removed in `t.Cleanup` also when the test fails, a check that nothing labelled is left, and the password in the body of the create call (never in an argument or a log). **SQL Server has no container test**: its image starts only with `ACCEPT_EULA=Y`, and setting it would accept Microsoft's license terms on the user's behalf, which the user does not do. Its driver, its connection string, its dialect and its statements are tested against fakes only (the fake `database/sql` driver and connector), so what only a real server shows (its catalog queries against the real catalog views, `WAITFOR` cancel, TLS) is not tested; if the user accepts the terms one day, a `server` entry in `extensions/sql/docker_test.go` and an image in `images.go` are all that is needed. The image `mcr.microsoft.com/mssql/server:2022-CU20-ubuntu-22.04` was pulled into the user's engine by the first version of the tests; it is left there for the user to remove (`docker rmi`); no step removes it.

## 2026-10-28: step 17d.7 (terminals in the editor area, the rest of the tabs list and persistent sessions; decided by the agent, to be accepted by me)

220. **Sessions are saved in the state of the window by the extension's own `SaveState`, and "onExit" and "onExitAndWindowClose" both revive.** The panel's tabs list is the state of the view and each terminal in an editor tab is the state of its tab; no file of the extension's own is made, so the lines the programs printed stay where the window state is (mode 0600) and nowhere else, as the brief asked. VS Code tells the two settings apart by whether the window or the application closes; Emetos has one process for all its windows and writes the state of a window when it closes, so there is nothing to tell apart: `never` does not revive, the other two do. The processes end with Emetos (VS Code keeps them only across a reload of a window); a restored terminal is a new shell.
221. **A restored terminal shows only the text of the normal buffer, then VS Code's line `History restored`.** `Emulator.SerializeText` writes the last 100 scrollback lines (`persistentSessionScrollback`) and the screen down to the cursor, with styles, wide characters, links and wrapping, and nothing of the modes, the title, the palette or the alternate screen: the new shell sets its own, and a program that was on the alternate screen (vim) leaves its last normal screen, as with VS Code's `serializeNormalBuffer`. More than 4 MiB of text is saved as none. A terminal whose program cannot start is left out of the restored session; a folder that is gone gives the default folder; a profile that is gone gives the program that was saved, else the default profile.
222. **A terminal in an editor tab is not in the tabs list, and `terminalCount` counts both.** As in VS Code, the list holds the terminals of the panel; the commands that act on "the terminal" take the one with the keyboard, then the last one of a tab that had it, then the active one of the list. A split of a terminal in a tab is another terminal in a tab of the same group (VS Code opens a group beside it; the extension API has no group to ask for), and `createTerminalEditorSide` is not built. A terminal that a request asks for without `show` is always made in the panel, whatever `defaultLocation` says, so it does not take the editor area of the person at the computer.
223. **The extension API got four small additions instead of a terminal-specific hook** (SPEC §10.15 "As built (step 17d.7)"): `EditorTab.SetIcon(icon, color)`, `EditorTabContent.AllowCommand`, `Editors().BeginDrag` and `ui.CodiconNames()`. Each is useful to any extension (a tab with its own icon, a tab that wants some keys, a drag toward the editor area, a picker of icons) and none names the terminal; the tab icon color is limited to the sixteen `terminal.ansi*` ids because the window's colors have no lookup by id, which a later step may add.
224. **A tab that was made and could not be shown calls its `OnClose`.** `Editors().Open` and the drop of a drag make the content first and show the tab after; when showing fails the content was never closed. Calling `OnClose` there lets the terminal (which left the panel to move into the tab) know that the tab is gone, and it goes back to the panel. This changes `dropTab` for every extension: an extension that has an `OnClose` and a tab that fails to open now gets the call.
225. **The drag of a row of the tabs list is made of pointer events, and Escape does not cancel it.** The row listens to the press, move and release of its own pointer (they follow the pointer out of the row), finds the list's place from the pressed row (22 px a row, 180 px wide; the list is not asked for its bounds, so a list scrolled by the wheel is approximated) and, once the pointer is out of the list, hands over to `Editors().BeginDrag`. A key handler for Escape is not added: the pointer being taken away cancels it, and releasing the button over nothing drops nothing. Inside the list a drag moves the tab as VS Code's `moveGroup` does (after the target's tab when it came from above, before it when from below, inside its own tab for the same group, and below the last row the end).
226. **`confirmOnKill` is still not acted on, for a terminal in a tab too.** Closing the tab of a terminal with a child process ends it without asking: the editor tab API has no way to veto a close, and the setting was declared and not acted on since 17d.2. Adding the veto is an API of its own and a dialog; it is listed under "Not built".
227. **New Terminal with a Folder asks for the folder when it has no argument, and Join Terminals is a quick pick.** VS Code's `newWithCwd` with no `cwd` simply makes a terminal in the default folder, which is a command that does nothing new; with a box for the folder (the workspace folder is proposed, `~` and relative paths are understood) it does what its title says. `joinActiveTab` (VS Code joins the selected rows) is not built: the list has no multiple selection; `join` lists the other tabs and joins the one picked to the tab of the active terminal.
228. **Size to Content Width is the width of the longest line of the screen, up to 1000 columns, with a bar and Shift+wheel.** VS Code (`toggleSizeToContentWidth`) makes the terminal as wide as the longest wrapped line of the viewport when that is wider than the pane and shows a horizontal scroll bar; Emetos does the same with the emulator told that many columns, a sideways scroll of the pane (`hscroll`, clamped to the content) and a thin bar at the bottom. The second use goes back to the width of the pane. A terminal whose content fits stays as it is (VS Code's check). The 1000 is a bound of mine.
229. **The copy of an editor tab (a split of the editor) starts a new shell with the same lines, and the choosers are quick picks.** The window's copy of a tab asks the extension for its saved state and makes another tab with it; for a terminal that is a restored session, so the copy has the old lines, the line `History restored` and a new shell in the same folder (VS Code starts a plain new terminal). The icon picker of VS Code is a grid with a search box; here Change Icon is a quick pick of every Codicon with its name, and Change Color has a first item, Default, that takes the color away (VS Code's list has the colors only). Both are what the quick pick offers today.

## 2026-10-29: step 18.4 (the Podman extension; decided by the agent, to be accepted by me)

340. **"Create Pod from Selected Containers" makes new containers in the new pod from the settings of the chosen ones; the chosen containers are not touched.** Podman cannot move a container into a pod after it was made, and `podman container clone` has no API. So the command asks for the pod's name, inspects each chosen container, creates the pod (publishing on it the ports the containers published, because the containers of a pod share one network) and a container `<pod>-<name>` in it from the image, command, entrypoint, environment, working folder, user, terminal, mounts and labels (not the labels of a Compose project and of Podman's own bookkeeping, so that a copy is not shown as part of that project). Nothing is removed or stopped, so it asks no question; the prompt says the chosen containers stay as they are. If the pod is made and a container cannot be, the pod stays with those that were made and the notification names the one that failed. The alternative (an empty pod, or removing and recreating the chosen containers) is worse: the first is "Create Pod...", the second is destructive.
341. **A container of a Compose project stays in its project's group even when it is in a pod; the pods have their own section.** SPEC says "grouped by Compose project, or by pod for Podman". podman-compose may put the containers of a project in a pod it makes (older versions always did), and the Compose group's commands (Logs, Start, Stop, Restart, Down) matter more there than a pod group. So the order is: the groups of pods (containers with a pod and no Compose project), then the Compose projects (by Docker's label `com.docker.compose.project` or podman-compose's `io.podman.compose.project`), then "Other Containers". The group of a pod has the menu of the pod (Inspect, Generate Kube YAML, Start, Stop, Restart, Pause, Unpause, Remove...), the group of a project has the Compose menu. The infra container of a pod is listed in its pod's group like any other.
342. **Connections: the list of `podman system connection list`, a first row "Automatic", and a pick that is the view's own.** The rows say rootless or rootful (by the socket: `/run/podman/...` or a name ending in `-root` is rootful), default and read-only. A connection that is a Unix socket or a `tcp://` address can be used; one over `ssh://` cannot (SPEC "Not built": no ssh to engines), and the notification says why. Using one is a choice of the view (saved with it, like a Docker context): the engine is looked for at that address, and the programs of the CLI run with `CONTAINER_HOST` set to it, and the command lines typed in the user's terminal (Open Shell, Run Interactive) get `--url <address>` after `podman`, so that they talk to the engine the view shows. Podman's own default connection is never changed. "Automatic" goes back to the search of SPEC "Which engine". The list is read at each connect and Refresh, not at the start of Emetos's life; a Podman with no program on `PATH` shows the reason in the section and the view still works through the API.
343. **Generate Kube YAML and Generate Quadlet open untitled editors, through `Documents().OpenUntitled`, which I took from step 19.2 verbatim.** SPEC says "opened in an editor tab" for the YAML and "an untitled editor" for the unit; both are untitled editors (language `yaml` and `ini`) so that the user can edit, save and then Play Kube the file. The method, its host implementation (`internal/gui/exthost/api.go`, `internal/gui/app/extensions.go`), the fake (`fakeapi`) and their tests are the hunks of the commit of 19.2 (`5b11fed`), copied without a change so that the two branches merge cleanly (the same text on both sides). When an editor cannot be opened (the scope has none) the text is shown in a read-only tab of the extension instead. Nothing is written to disk by either command.
344. **Kube Down asks first and names the pods of the engine that the file made; with none it does nothing.** `podman kube down` removes pods, so it follows SPEC "Safety": the question lists the pods (a `Pod` of the file by its name; any other workload, such as a Deployment, by the pod `<name>-pod` or `<name>-pod-N` that Podman makes of it), the number of containers that go with them, that volumes are kept, and how many pods run. Cancel runs nothing (tested: no process, nothing sent to the engine). A file none of whose pods is in the engine says so in a notification and runs nothing. The names are read from the YAML by a small reader of `kind` and `metadata.name` (no YAML module is allowed), not by a full parser. Play Kube asks nothing: it makes things, and removes none.
345. **The Quadlet unit holds only what the user gave the container.** The unit is made from the inspect of the container and of its image: the environment, command, entrypoint, labels, user and working folder that the image has itself are left out, and so are the variables Podman sets (`container`, `HOME`, `HOSTNAME`, `TERM`) and the labels of Podman's bookkeeping. It writes `ContainerName`, `Image`, `Entrypoint`, `Exec`, `Environment`, `PublishPort` (a port only exposed is not published), `Volume`, `Network` (not the default one), `WorkingDir`, `User`, `HostName`, `Label`, `Restart=` from the restart policy (`always` and `unless-stopped` are `always`, `on-failure` is `on-failure`) and `[Install] WantedBy=default.target`, with systemd's quoting (`%` doubled, `$` doubled in command lines, words and assignments with spaces in double quotes). A container in a pod gets a comment that a `.pod` unit is needed, not generated. A header comment says where to save it and which commands to run; Emetos never does either. Not written: healthchecks, resource limits, `Notify`, `AutoUpdate`, `RunInit`, secrets, pods and networks as their own units. The keys that need Podman 5 (`Entrypoint=`) are written without a check of the version.
346. **The menu items of Podman on a Dockerfile, a Compose file and a YAML file say "Podman: "; Podman declares no language server.** The Explorer's and the editor's menus get the items of both extensions on the same files; with equal titles they could not be told apart. Docker's titles stay as they were. Podman declares neither the Dockerfile nor the Compose language server (Docker's extension does, for the same languages: two servers on one file would answer twice). The view's icon in the activity bar is Codicon `server-environment` (Docker's is `package`), Podman's panel is "Podman Logs", its settings are `podman.host`, `podman.composeBuild`, `podman.composeDetached` and `podman.imageBuildContextPath`, its events `emetos.podman.changed` and `emetos.podman.buildFinished`. Scaffolding writes `Containerfile` ("Add Podman Files to Workspace...").
347. **A client of a hosted workspace may not run the commands of the container extensions (`containers.` and `podman.`): a new rule of class "outside" in `internal/core/command/clientpolicy.go`.** The rule of step 19.1 ("a database or a container engine is outside the workspace", decision 255) listed only `emetos.databases.`; steps 18.2 and 18.3 added Docker's commands (`containers.*`) without adding their prefix, so a client of a hosted workspace could have run `containers.containers.remove` through `emetos.commands.execute`. I added the prefixes of both extensions in a rule of its own (so that it does not touch the line step 19.2 changes) and the test. **This is a fix of an omission of an earlier step, flagged to you because it changes what clients may do.**
348. **Small changes to the engine client and the fake that Podman needed.** `Container.ComposeProject` also reads podman-compose's label `io.podman.compose.project` (Docker's label wins); `PodSpec.Ports` publishes ports on a pod (libpod's `portmappings` of the pod); the fake's `pod prune` removes the containers of the pods and emits their `pod remove` events, as Podman does, and `fakeengine -kind podman` serves a scenario with pods for the proof. The Podman CLI runs with `CONTAINER_HOST` when an engine was named (setting or connection), and Buildah's `Error: building at STEP "RUN x"` of a failed build is shown in Problems on the line of that instruction (looked for by its text; the first of two equal ones).
349. **What the tests do with the real Podman, beyond CLAUDE.md.** `TestPodsKubeAndQuadletOverTheRealPodman` makes a labelled pod `emetos-test-<random>` with one labelled container of the pinned image (the harness removes both, after looking at the label), asks the extension for its Kube YAML, plays that YAML under a second `emetos-test-<random>` name, takes that pod down through Kube Down's question (Cancel first: nothing changes), and removes the first pod through the question of the view. What I found with the real Podman 5.8.9 (2026-10-29): `podman kube generate` of a pod does **not** write the pod's labels into the YAML (only `app: <name>`), so the test adds the label of the tests to `metadata.labels` before it plays the file, and the view, which lists only labelled things, shows the played pod only because `kube play` carries that label to the pod; the first version of the test stopped before playing an unlabelled YAML, as designed. A cleanup removes the played pod by its exact random name (not by its label, which a pod that `kube play` made without it would lack) when the test failed in between. The extension is configured without Connections in that test, because `podman system connection list` reads the user's own configuration. No prune, and `podman ps -a` and `podman pod ps` filtered by the label show nothing afterwards. `TestBuildImageOverTheRealPodman` builds a Containerfile whose image carries the label.

## 2026-10-29: step 19.3 (SQL query editors and the results panel; decided by the agent, to be accepted by me)

320. **SQL Server is never started in a container.** Its image needs `ACCEPT_EULA=Y`, which accepts Microsoft's licence terms on the user's behalf, and the user accepts no third-party terms. The Tests line of step 19.3 in `docs/IMPLEMENTATION.md` now says PostgreSQL, MySQL and MariaDB for the real-server tests (`extensions/sql/docker_queries_test.go`, which filters `sqlserver` out of `servers` and never sets `ACCEPT_EULA`), and SQL Server's splitting (`GO`, batches with counts, blocks, `CREATE PROCEDURE` to the end of the batch), classification and execution are tested against fakes: `dialect/split_test.go`, `classify_test.go`, and `run_fake_test.go` (a scripted `database/sql` driver with several result sets, `mssql.Error` with a line, a GUID, `USE`, `GO 2` repeating a batch).
321. **Splitting follows each engine's own client, and a leading comment belongs to the statement after it.** MySQL has `DELIMITER` and no block tracking (a `CREATE PROCEDURE` with `BEGIN ... END` needs `DELIMITER`, as in the `mysql` client); SQLite tracks the `BEGIN ... END` of a trigger, PostgreSQL the `BEGIN ATOMIC` of a function (PostgreSQL 14), SQL Server `BEGIN ... END`/`TRY`/`CASE` and the procedure bodies that run to `GO`. SQL Server also splits at a semicolon outside a block (the SPEC says "GO for SQL Server" only, but a script of five SELECTs with no `GO` would otherwise run as one batch under Run at the cursor). `GO 5` runs the whole batch five times (the statements of the batch in order, five times), not each statement five times. A comment on the line of the previous semicolon belongs to the previous statement; the comments on the lines before a statement are part of its text (the error positions stay exact because `Text == src[Start:End]`). The statement "under the cursor" is the one that contains it, else the one that ended earlier on the same line, else the next, else the last.
322. **Classification is a check of the first words, and "read-only" is that check plus the engine's own mode.** `SELECT ... INTO`, `CALL`, `EXEC`, a WITH that holds INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE/MERGE, `BEGIN READ WRITE` and a SET of `default_transaction_read_only` and its kin are writes (the last two would undo the session's read-only mode in PostgreSQL and MySQL); reads, transactions, USE and the other SETs are allowed on a read-only connection. A SELECT that calls a function that writes is a read to the check; PostgreSQL, MySQL and SQLite refuse it in the session, SQL Server does not (decision 274). Destructive is DROP, TRUNCATE, ALTER and a DELETE or UPDATE with no WHERE at its own level (a WHERE only in a subquery does not count); the question is a quick pick (Run, Cancel) before the whole run, not one question for each statement, and names the reasons.
323. **One connection of the pool for each editor, transactions as the engine's own statements, and the first failure stops the run.** A `Runner` takes a `Session` when the first statement runs and keeps it (a temporary table and an open transaction survive between runs); Begin, Commit and Rollback send `BEGIN`/`START TRANSACTION`/`BEGIN TRANSACTION` and so on (not `database/sql`'s `Tx`, so that a `COMMIT` the user types, and `SAVEPOINT`, work, and the runner follows them by classification). The run stops at the first statement that fails (as `psql` with `ON_ERROR_STOP`; the SPEC does not say) and counts the statements not run. Closing an editor rolls its transaction back with a note; disconnecting asks first (`dbconn.DisconnectGuard`, an optional interface of a driver; the view asks in a quick pick). A statement ended by Cancel leaves a PostgreSQL transaction aborted, as the server does. DDL that commits implicitly (MySQL) is not tracked. A statement that the engine ends quietly after a Cancel (MySQL's `KILL QUERY` makes `SLEEP` return 1, no error) is still marked cancelled, and stops the run. A PostgreSQL `timestamptz` is shown in UTC (pgx gives the zone of the machine); SQL Server's `datetimeoffset` keeps its offset.
324. **What a run keeps: 10 runs of an editor, 1,000 rows at first, 64 MB for a set, 256 MB for an editor.** Fetch More works on the cursor of the **last statement's last set** only: `database/sql` closes the rows when it goes to the next result set, so the sets of earlier statements, and a set that is followed by another result set of its statement, are cut at the limit with a note. The oldest runs lose their rows (not their messages) when an editor passes 256 MB. Nothing of a result goes to disk or to a log, and `queryFinished` has no SQL and no value; it is published once for each statement (the SPEC says "query").
325. **Errors: a marker for a file, the cursor for an untitled document.** `Problems().Set` takes files only (the Problems panel opens its entries as files), so an untitled editor, the usual one for New Query, gets no squiggle: the cursor moves to the error and the Messages tab has a link "Line 3, column 12". A file gets the marker (owner `sql`), cleared by the next run. Making markers work for untitled documents would change the Problems panel and the editors' diagnostics; left as a follow-up. SQLite's error code 1 (every error that has none of its own) is not written.
326. **pgx runs every statement without its statement cache (`QueryExecModeExec`).** The default prepares each text and fails with "cached plan must not change result type" when a table is altered between two runs of a `SELECT *`, which is what an SQL editor is for. A statement is parsed on each run; the real-server test alters a table between two reads.
327. **New API in `extension/` and the shared packages:** `StatusItem.Color` (the connection's color tints its status bar item, the SPEC's wording; white or black text by lightness; `workbench.StatusEntry.Color`), `FileChooser().Save` with `FileChooserOptions.Name` and `Save` (the export needs a save dialog; the 19.1 text said so; it goes through the same scope function as Open, with `Save` set), `dbconn.DisconnectGuard`, and `resultgrid.Export.Columns` and `Each` (the INSERT writer needs the rows). The fake API has `Save` too. `ScopeInfo.Instance` (and `WorkspaceInfo.Instance`, not in the JSON payload) numbers the scopes of the host: the extension "Databases" and the SQL extension each have an API of their own in a window tab, so the menu of the Databases view finds the query editors of its tab by the scope (`api.Scope()`), not by the API object (found by the real-input proof; the fake API shares one object in the tests). The host sets it in `NewScope`; the fake API leaves it 0.
328. **Completion has a catalog cache of its own.** The SPEC says "from the explorer's cache", but the explorer's rows are the view's tree model and are not reachable from a provider that runs on its own goroutine; `Catalog` reads the same dialect queries, shares one reading among the callers, keeps the answer 5 minutes, forgets it when a statement that changes the schema ran in an editor (the check of the words says `Schema`) or the connection closes (a Refresh in the view does not clear it). Names that are not in the default schema are offered after the schema's name. The keywords are the dialect's list, not the grammar's.
329. **Bindings, the panel and the export.** A binding is `{connection, database}`; the bindings of files are in the panel view's saved state (the SPEC says "the workspace state"; that is where a view's state lives), untitled documents are not kept across a restart. The panel shows the results of the active SQL editor (the one that ran last until another one is activated). Export is the result set shown, as the grid sorts it, with the rows that were fetched (not a re-run of the query without the limit), to a file of mode 0600; the SQL export asks for the table and guesses it from the SELECT. The panel's buttons are CSV, JSON and SQL (the SPEC says "a save dialog"; each button opens it). A press anywhere in the panel gives it the keyboard so that Escape cancels a running statement.

## 2026-10-29: step 20.1 (MongoDB: connections, the explorer and the playground language; decided by the agent, to be accepted by me)

280. **The extension is `emetos.mongodb`, listed by `extensions.All()` with the language packs (after Go modules), and starts with Emetos.** It contributes a language (`.mongodb`), and the test that keeps `packfs.Names` in the order of the packs (`cmd/emetos/languagepacks_test.go`) treats every extension with a language as a pack, so "mongodb" is the last of `packfs.Names` and the factory (`mongodb.FactoryOf(hub)`, made over the one hub of `extensions.All()`) is the last of the packs. It activates on startup because its whole activation is `Hub.RegisterDriver`; it connects to nothing until a connection is opened. Step 21.1 (a `.redis` language) will meet the same list.
281. **"New Playground" opens its document with step 19.2's `Documents().OpenUntitled(languageID, text)`; there is no second API.** An extension can run no command of the editor (only the privileged `commands.execute` of the Terminal), nor create an `untitled:` document with `Apply` (a `create` makes a file), so a playground needs an API that opens an untitled editor with text in it. Step 20.1 was first written with its own `Documents().NewUntitled`; when it was rebased onto `main`, which has 19.2's `OpenUntitled` for the same thing, I kept 19.2's and dropped mine (with `UntitledMaker` and the tag `ExtDocumentUntitledUnavailable`): the window opens the document as New File does, with the text as the first state of the document (nothing to undo into), and the document counts as changed, so closing it asks. One difference is lost: `NewUntitled` also gave the new editor the keyboard focus, `OpenUntitled` does not. The tab-less failure is `ExtDocumentShowUnavailable` (a window tab that is not on screen), already tested by 19.2. Step 20.2 uses the same call.
282. **A `Driver` is shared by the window tabs, and the playground opens in the tab of the menu (found in 19.1, fixed by `dbconn.APIDriver` from 19.2).** `Hub.Actions` asks the first registered driver for the menu, so in a second window tab "New Playground" acted through the first tab's API. The driver now implements `dbconn.APIDriver` (`ActionsIn(api, c, n)`, as the SQL driver does) and the view calls it with the API of the tab the menu was opened in; `Actions` without an API opens in the tab of the extension that registered the driver (`TestAPlaygroundOpensInTheWindowTabOfTheMenu`). The other limit I found (the unregister function of `RegisterDriver` closed every open connection of the engine when one tab went away) is fixed in `dbconn` on `main`. The servers themselves are shared (one manager for every tab).
283. **The values of a playground are the types of the driver's `bson` package, and numbers are read as mongosh does.** `bson.D` keeps the order of the keys (the later value of a repeated key wins, as in JavaScript), whole numbers in the range of int32 are int32 and every other number a double (`5` and `5.0` and `1e3` are int32; `NumberLong("...")` is the way to a 64-bit integer, as it is in mongosh, because JavaScript has no integers), `\/` in a regular expression is stored as `/` (the same pattern; it makes `Format` and `Parse` agree), and the flags are `imsxu` (a `g` is refused: it means nothing in a query). Step 20.2 runs the values without converting them.
284. **The subset is a little wider than the list of §10.22, and a little narrower than mongosh.** Added because they are what people type: `use("db")` and `use "db"`, `show databases` and `show tables`, `db["name"]`, dotted collection names, `.toArray()` and `.pretty()` (accepted and ignored), `new` before any helper, `NaN`, `Infinity`, hexadecimal numbers and a trailing `;`. Refused, with the place and a sentence: variables, functions, loops, template strings, `undefined`, arrays with gaps, octal escapes, BigInt, `Date(...)` without `new` (it is a string in JavaScript), `db.stats()` and the other methods of `db`, and every collection method that the list does not have (`findOneAndUpdate`, `watch`, `count` on a collection...). The arguments are checked against the method when the text is read, so a mistake is shown before anything runs.
285. **`Parse` never fails as a whole; the unit is an item (a statement or its error) with a span.** After a mistake the parser goes on at the next statement: after a `;`, or at a token on a new line when no bracket is open, or at a line that starts with `db.`, or `use ` or `show ` in its first column, even when a bracket is open (a line like that is never the inside of a document), so one bad line does not hide the file. Places are the byte offset and the zero-based line and column in bytes (as the events of the editor give them); the parser never loops or recurses without a bound (100 levels of nesting, `MongoParseTooDeep`). An error about the token that starts the next line points at the end of the last token of the statement: the statement ended too soon. `Script.At(offset)` picks the statement under the cursor (the one that holds it; else the one before it on the same line; else the one after it on the same line).
286. **22 `MongoParse*` tags, one per kind of mistake, each made in the function that sees it.** The lexer (strings, comments, regular expressions, numbers, characters) and the parser (`expect`, `value`, `object`, `array`, `helper`, `statement`, `use`, `show`, `dbStatement`, `collectionCall`, `checkArgs`) make their own errors with the place in their metadata (`offset`, `end`, `line`, `column`, `endLine`, `endColumn`); `playground.Place` reads it back. The route tests are a table (`errors_test.go`), with the line and the column of each. Fuzzing: `FuzzParse` (no panic, spans in order and inside the text, an error always has a place) ran 25 seconds with `-parallel=1` and `GOMEMLIMIT=2GiB` and found nothing; seeded random damage of valid scripts and seeded random values written with `Format` and read again run in every test run.
287. **Explorer details the SPEC leaves open.** Databases are listed with `authorizedDatabases` (a user with no rights on the server still sees its own); the user's databases first, then `admin`, `config` and `local`; collections by name with `system.*` last; document counts are the estimate from the metadata, asked 8 at a time and for the first 500 collections only (a database with thousands of collections lists at once), a count that fails is left blank and does not fail the listing, and a view is not counted; the schema is a tree built from `$sample` (so the server sends only the sample, which is cut at 30 seconds), the share of documents that have a field is shown when it is under 100 % and is of the documents of its parent, six levels and 300 fields per level are followed, and a collection with no documents says "No documents to sample". Nothing is cached below the view's own cache (until Refresh).
288. **The menus: what changes the server is text, and `Open Collection` waits for step 20.2.** Create and drop (database, collection, index) open a playground with the statement and a comment saying what it does; the menu never runs one. I did not put "Open Collection" in the menu, because §10.22 shows the first 20 documents "in the results" and the panel of results is step 20.2; "New Playground" on a collection writes `db.getCollection("name").find({}).limit(20)` meanwhile. A per-node *Refresh* is not possible with `dbconn.Action` (it has no way to reach the view); the title action Refresh drops everything. `use` is written as a call (`use("a b")`) for a name its short form cannot read.
289. **TLS, authentication and timeouts.** The password is set on the credential of the driver's options and is in no connection string; a connection with no password and no user sends no credential; `authSource` is the one of the form, else the database of the connection (its *Database* field, which is also the path of the connection string and the database "New Playground" starts with), else the driver's `admin`, and `$external` for X.509; *prefer* (which MongoDB cannot do) and an empty mode leave TLS to the connection string; *verify-ca* is a `VerifyConnection` that checks the chain against the CA file and not the name; the CA file and the client certificate are read when the connection is opened (off the UI thread). The driver looks for a server for 10 seconds and Connect pings, so a wrong address, user or password is an error at once; the error is the driver's message (`MongoConnectFailed`) and never has the password. Closing a connection is told to the driver in the background (`Hub.Disconnect` runs on the UI thread and a server that does not answer must not hold it), and **Test Connection of a connection that is open** (the hub connects and disconnects with the same ID) keeps its server apart, so the open one stays. `mongodb.schemaSampleSize` is read on the UI thread and handed to the goroutines that list a schema. The tests against a real server (build tag `emetos_docker`) start a throwaway `mongo:7.0.14` named `emetos-test-<random>` with the label `dev.emetos.test=1`, a port on 127.0.0.1 only, a root user with a random password given to the engine in the body of the create call (not in a command line), and remove it in `t.Cleanup`; the container is made by the harness of step 18.1 (`containerapitest`) through the `Runner` of `extensions/sql/sqltest` (`sqltest.Docker()`), and the pinned image `docker.io/library/mongo:7.0.14` is `containerapitest.ImageMongoDB`, listed in `PinnedImages`.

## 2026-10-29: breadcrumbs that do not fit (decided by the agent, to be accepted by me)

380. **Breadcrumbs scroll sideways instead of shrinking their segments (SPEC §9.7 said they shrink with an ellipsis).** Every segment is as wide as its icon and name, as VS Code's `flex: 0 1 auto; white-space: nowrap` items are, and the row is in a `kit.ScrollableElement` (the one of the tab strip: horizontal, wheel turned sideways, a scrollbar of 3 px, `titleScrollbarSizing` "default"). The old squeeze made a segment narrower than its icon and padding and Glutter reported a `RenderFlex` overflow of 2 px on each folder (found by the arena test with a deep temp folder). The SPEC's "Emetos does not scroll the bar" and the "scrolling of a long path" in the not-ported list are replaced.
381. **The last segment is revealed as VS Code's `revealLast` does, after the frame, when the names of the segments change and when the bar's width changes.** VS Code sets `scrollLeft` to the `offsetLeft` of the last segment (the bounds clamp it): a short last segment ends at the right edge, one wider than the bar starts at the left edge. VS Code does it on a change of the items and on `revealLast`; it does not on a plain resize (the scroll position is only clamped). Emetos also reveals on a resize, as the task asked, so a user who scrolled by hand loses that position when the window is resized or when the cursor moves into another symbol (the symbol segments are part of the names, as VS Code's items are). The offset is measured with `kit.Bounds` probes around the row and around each segment (all segments are wrapped alike, so a segment that stops being the last keeps its state).
382. **Settings left out:** `workbench.editor.titleScrollbarSizing` and `titleScrollbarVisibility` are not read; the bar has their defaults (3 px, auto). No keyboard `reveal` of a focused segment (the keyboard focus of the bar is not ported, SPEC §9.7).

## 2026-10-29: step 21.1 (Redis: connections, the explorer and the key editor; decided by the agent, to be accepted by me)

290. **`dbconn` got two optional interfaces for a driver, a reload and the node hover.** Step 19.1's `Driver` had no way to act on a node that was opened, to drop what it kept when the view was refreshed, or to say that what it lists changed. `Opener.Open(connection, node)` (Enter or a double click on a node: a key opens in the editor), `Refresher.Refresh(connection)` (called for each open connection by the Refresh command) and `Hub.Reload(id)` with `SubscribeReload` (the view lists the open rows of that connection again, asking the driver and not the server) are found by a type assertion, so a driver without them, such as the SQL and MongoDB ones, is unchanged; `view.go` draws `Node.Tooltip` as a hover (19.1 declared it and never showed it). The three database steps run in parallel, so these small additions may need merging with what steps 19.2 and 20.1 added to the same files (`view.go`, `extension.go`, `hub.go`; the new code is in `hooks.go`).
291. **Keys are scanned in pages the user extends, and the scanned keys stay in memory until Refresh.** A page is `SCAN` calls until `redis.scanCount` keys (500, 10 to 10,000) or 100 calls (a pattern that matches little would otherwise scan the whole keyspace in one go), then one pipeline of `TYPE` and `PTTL`; the page is added only when all of it was read, so a cancelled or failed load leaves nothing half done. The tree of folders is built from the keys held, one level a time (`keytree`: folders first, natural order). The database's last row, "Load more keys...", scans the next page (a database of a million keys is never listed whole). A database's key count shows beside the loaded number ("500 of 12,345"); with a filter only the loaded number, since the total does not say how many match. The cost: a big keyspace needs many clicks of Load more; there is no "Load all".
292. **The filter takes text or a pattern.** SPEC says "a pattern filter (`MATCH`)"; most people type a word, so a text with no glob character matches the keys that contain it (`*text*`), and one with `* ? [ ] \` is the pattern. It belongs to a database of a connection, stays through Refresh, and is shown in the row of the database.
293. **A connection lists the databases that have keys, and the one it selects.** `INFO keyspace` is what a server tells without `CONFIG GET databases` (which managed services refuse); an empty database 3 is therefore not listed, and the Database number field of the connection is how to open one. A cluster has only database 0, with the count of all its masters.
294. **Connection fields and TLS.** The engine adds Database number, Key separator (`none` is a flat list), Mode, Sentinel master name and "More Sentinels or cluster nodes"; the form's common fields (host, port, user, TLS, CA file, color, read-only, password) do the rest. TLS `prefer` is refused with a message (a Redis server cannot negotiate TLS), `require` does not check the certificate, `verify-ca` checks the chain and not the name, `rediss://` with no TLS choice is `verify-full`. Sentinels are sent no password of their own (go-redis's `SentinelPassword` is not set): a Sentinel with `requirepass` is not supported. go-redis runs with `DisableIdentity` and no maintenance notifications, so Emetos sends no `CLIENT SETINFO` and no `CLIENT MAINT_NOTIFICATIONS`, and with `ContextTimeoutEnabled`, so a closed tab cancels its command.
295. **A string is saved with `SET key value KEEPTTL`** (Redis 6.0 or newer; an older server answers with an error that is shown). The JSON view saves compact when the value was stored compact and as shown when it was stored indented, and refuses text that is not JSON. Binary values (invalid UTF-8 or a NUL byte) open in Hex and are read-only; a value over 1 MiB shows its first MiB, read-only, with a note. A text box that must not change (Hex, cut, read-only connection) takes the old text back on every edit, because Emetos's text box has no read-only mode that keeps the selection and the copy.
296. **Rows are changed one command each, in place when that is safe.** Hash, set and sorted set patch their rows after `HSET`, `SADD`, `ZADD`, `HDEL`, `SREM` and `ZREM`; a list (indexes move) and a stream (new entries come first) are loaded again. Removing from a list by index is `LSET` of a marker that no value has, then `LREM`: Redis has no better way, and another client writing to the same list between the two commands may remove the wrong item. A stream entry and a set member cannot be edited, only added and removed (Redis cannot change them). Removing one row asks nothing; more than one asks first. A sorted set is read with `ZSCAN` as the spec says, so its rows are not in score order (the grid sorts on the client).
297. **New Key is a form tab, and it refuses a name that exists.** SPEC says "a form: type and first value"; it is an editor tab with the name (starting with the folder it was asked in), the type, the first value (the Add fields of the type, with no list position) and an optional TTL. The check of the name and the write are two commands, so a key made by another client in between is overwritten for a string and JSON and extended for the others (as `SET` and `HSET` do). Rename uses `RENAMENX`, so it never overwrites.
298. **Delete asks in a notification, closes the key's tabs, and every writing control is also behind the read-only wrapper.** A read-only connection has no menu entry or button that writes, and `redis.ReadOnly` makes its 18 writing methods fail before any command is sent; both exist so that neither a mistake in the menu nor in the editor can write. Errors and logs carry no key, field or value (the metadata is the command and the kind of failure), as SPEC §10.24 says of values; a key name can be a session id.
299. **Small changes outside the extension, found while building it.** `resultgrid` drew the text of a selected cell in `list.inactiveSelectionForeground`, which no built-in theme defines (transparent), so a selection was a blank bar as soon as the grid lost the focus (when a button of the key editor was pressed): it now keeps the text's own color, as the tree does. `kit.InputBoxOpts.Monospace` (new, with a test) lets the key editor show JSON and a hex dump in the monospace font. `go.mod` gets `github.com/redis/go-redis/v9 v9.23.0` (BSD-2-Clause, with `cespare/xxhash/v2` and `go.uber.org/atomic`, both MIT), and `internal/archtest` lists it among the modules an extension may import. The events of §10.23 (`connected`, `disconnected`, `commandFinished`) are left to step 21.2, which names them. A driver is registered by the first window's extension instance and serves every window (`Hub.RegisterDriver`: the first one keeps the engine), so a key opens in that window; a fix needs the driver to be one object for every window (as the SQL extension's `sql.Hub`) and `dbconn` to pass the window to `Open` as `Hub.ActionsIn` / `APIDriver` (step 19.2) already do for the menus, and belongs to the step that finishes the three extensions.

## 2026-10-29: step 21.2 (Redis: the console, Pub/Sub, Monitor and server information; decided by the agent, to be accepted by me)

310. **Each console command runs on a client of its own, closed to cancel it.** go-redis does not interrupt a read when the context is cancelled (only a deadline does), so `BLPOP key 0` could not be cancelled; the client's default read timeout of 5 s would also cut a `BLPOP key 7`. `Do` makes a client for the command with no read timeout, and a watcher closes it when the context ends; the console also stops waiting on `ctx.Done`, so Cancel answers at once. The cost is one connection (and an AUTH/HELLO/SELECT) for each console command, which is nothing next to a person typing.
311. **Replies are formatted as `redis-cli` does in a file, not in a terminal, and a string with a line break is shown raw.** `(integer)`, `(nil)`, quoted and escaped strings, `1) `, `1# k => v`, `1~ m`, `(double)`. A string that has a line break (INFO, CLIENT LIST, a multi-line value) is written as it is, since a quoted string with `\n` is unreadable for INFO; a value written by `SET note "a\n"` is therefore shown as `a`. A map is shown in the key order go-redis gives (sorted by key, because it hands maps over as Go maps), so `HGETALL` of RESP3 is sorted by field, not in the server's order.
312. **The read-only guard asks the server first.** The flags of `COMMAND INFO` (`write`, `admin`...) decide, with a built-in list of write commands (and of subcommands of `CONFIG`, `CLIENT`, `SCRIPT`, `FUNCTION`, `ACL`, `MEMORY`, `DEBUG`) for a server that has no `COMMAND INFO` or when a command is not known to it. A refused command is never sent and no confirmation is asked for it. `SUBSCRIBE`, `PSUBSCRIBE`, `MONITOR`, `SYNC`, `PSYNC` and `SSUBSCRIBE` are not console commands in any mode; the console says which tab does them.
313. **A confirmation can never run another command.** Every question gets a token (the run's generation); the answer carries it; a new line, Cancel, a closed console or a newer question makes the old token stale and its answer is dropped. Verified against a real Redis (a stale "Run" did not flush; a confirmed `FLUSHDB` after `SELECT 7` emptied database 7 only). `redis.confirmDangerous` can be turned off by the user; a read-only connection is not asked, it refuses.
314. **MONITOR uses a raw connection.** go-redis' `MonitorCmd` cannot be stopped (`Stop` blocks), so the monitor dials the server, sends AUTH (and HELLO/SELECT as needed), then `MONITOR`, and reads lines until it is closed. It warns first in a notification. The lines on screen hold the values of the commands (that is what Monitor is for); they are not logged, not in events, and are dropped with the tab.
315. **Pub/Sub and Monitor never block the UI thread and never grow without a bound.** A goroutine reads, a ring keeps the last 1,000 messages or lines, and a feeder (at most 10,000 pending, a flood past that is dropped) hands batches to the UI thread with `api.Post`. Pause freezes the list, not the subscription. The tabs belong to a connection and end with it.
316. **`.redis` files are a language with a grammar in `packfs`, run by Ctrl+Enter and Ctrl+Shift+Enter.** This changes `internal/core/language/packfs` (a "redis" pack, with a detection and a grammar test) and not `extension/`. Ctrl+Enter in a `.redis` file is also the editor's "insert line below"; the extension's binding has the condition `editorLangId == redis`, so it only wins there, and the real-input test shows it run the line (no line was inserted). A file remembers its connection (shown in the status bar); with none chosen and exactly one connection open it uses that one, else it asks.
317. **Completion of command names comes from the server, with a built-in fallback.** `COMMAND DOCS` (names and summaries, 400 or so on Redis 8), else `COMMAND`, else a list of the common commands; asked once for each connection in the background and dropped when the connection closes. Before the answer the built-in names have no summaries (a test that waits for names must wait for a summary).
318. **The events carry no secret, key or command.** `connected` and `disconnected` `{connection}`, `commandFinished` `{connection, ok, millis}` for the commands of the console and of `.redis` files. The key editor's own commands are not events (they are not the user's commands). The id of a connection is the only identifier.
