319. **The console, Pub/Sub and Monitor are three pages of one panel tab "Redis", not three tabs.** The title strip of the panel (`paneloverflow.go`, step 17b.6) gives the tabs the room first and the actions of the view what they leave; with three Redis tabs next to the seven of Go, the filter of Problems had no room at 1280 px (`TestRealInputProblemsFilter` failed). One tab leaves it room, and Docker, Podman and Mongo will want the same. The pages are chosen by a small switch at the head of the tab; the commands (`Open Console`, `Open Pub/Sub`, `Open Monitor`) select the page and reveal the tab. The "Clear Console" title action of the strip was dropped (the page has its Clear button); the command stays. Also: **Two commands for other callers: `emetos.redis.run {connection?, lines}` and `emetos.redis.openKey {connection, db, key}`.** They have no menu entry of their own for the arguments; they exist so that the real-input test and, later, other extensions can drive the console and the key editor. With no arguments they ask in the editor (a picker of the connection, an input box).

## 2026-10-29: the title strip of the panel (decided by the agent, to be accepted by me)

370. **The actions of the open view keep their room and the tabs get what is left, as VS Code does.** Step 17b.6 gave the tabs the room first and the actions what they left (none under 130 px), so with the tabs of Go, Redis, Containers and Podman the Problems filter was gone at 1280 px. VS Code's `layoutCompositeBar` (`paneCompositePart.ts`) lays the composite bar out in `max(50, contentWidth - padding - getToolbarWidth())`, the toolbar of the active view being measured first. Emetos now does the same: `workbench.panel` asks the actions for their width, builds them in exactly that width, and fits the tabs (`fitPanelTabs`) in what is left; the tabs that do not fit go into the "..." menu, the active tab stays, none is cut. SPEC §10.13 "The title strip of the panel" has the rule.
371. **The actions declare their width instead of being measured.** `kit.TitleActions` is a widget with `TitleActionsWidth(room float64) float64` (the width it takes when offered at most `room`; 0 for nothing; the same when asked again with its own answer), and `workbench.Options.PanelActions` has that type. Glutter's intrinsic sizing is not usable (`LayoutBuilder`, which the actions need to shrink, has no intrinsic size, and it would measure after the tree is built, which is when the tabs are already fitted). Problems, Output and an extension's `ViewActions` (`kit.IconButtonRow`) implement it; a new kind of view with actions implements it too, and the compiler says so.
372. **VS Code's widths for the Problems filter: 200 px at most, 150 at least.** (`views.css`, `.panel > .title .viewpane-filter-container`; Emetos had 240 and 80.) With less room than the 150 px plus the buttons the box is left out and the switches stay (112 px); with less than that nothing shows. The Output dropdown keeps its 160 and 60 px (VS Code sizes its select box to its content). VS Code's growth of the filter to 400 px while it has the focus is not built.
373. **The tabs are left at least 50 px (`MIN_COMPOSITE_BAR_WIDTH`), or the active tab with the "..." button when that is wider.** VS Code clips the bar when the toolbar is too wide; Emetos makes the actions give way first (shrink to their least, drop the filter box, drop the buttons that do not fit, then go), so no text is cut and the active tab shows whole. Only a strip too narrow for the buttons and the active tab clips it. The 4 px gap between the actions and the buttons stays; VS Code's 10 px margin after the filter is the 6 px of the existing layout.
374. **The test workarounds of the old rule are gone.** `TestRealInputProblemsFilter` no longer closes the side bar with Ctrl+B; `TestDesktopPanelTabsAreWholeAndOverflow` no longer asserts that nothing overflows at 1280 px (with the tabs of every extension some go into the menu, which is expected) but that the active tab shows, no text is cut, and the filter of Problems is in the strip at 150 px or more, clear of the tabs. The one-tab "Redis" panel of decision 319 stays (it is tidier than three tabs); only the reason in SPEC was changed.

## 2026-10-29: step 20.2 (MongoDB: running playgrounds, results and editing documents; decided by the agent, to be accepted by me)

350. **The results are in a panel tab of the MongoDB extension, "MongoDB Results"; what is shared is the grid.** §10.22 says "the shared panel and grid of §10.21", but the panel of 19.3 is the SQL extension's (`queries`, a tab "Query Results") and an extension may not import another (`ExtensionImportsExtension`), so the panel cannot be reused as code. `extensions/internal/resultgrid` is shared (the Table view is that grid over the documents); the tabs, the Messages and the toolbar are written again for documents, in the same look and with the same lines as SQL's. A common panel package would need a refactor of 19.3; I left it out. Step 22 may want one.
351. **A statement becomes a `Call` and the backend runs it (`Translate`, `Backend.Exec`).** The alternative, raw `runCommand` documents, would skip what the driver does (retryable writes, sessions, `_id` for inserts, the server selection that keeps a cursor on one server with a read preference). So the real backend calls the driver's own methods, and the pure translation (statement to `Call`) is the table-driven part of the tests, with a fake `Backend` for the rest. `createIndex`, `dropIndex` and `drop` are the commands `createIndexes`, `dropIndexes` and `drop` (the shell does the same); `find().count()` counts the documents that match (as the shell's `cursor.count()` does without `applySkipLimit`). The options a method takes are listed in `optionNames` and any other is refused with its name (`MongoRunUnknownOption`), as the parser refuses an unknown method.
352. **Results that are no documents are written as documents.** A count is `{count: n}`, `distinct` has one `{value: v}` for each value, `show dbs` and `show collections` are `{name, ...}` and a write is the document the shell prints (`acknowledged`, `insertedId`, `matchedCount`, ...); so every result has the same three views and the same tab. The summary of a run counts the documents of the reads only.
353. **The database of a statement is the last `use` before it in the playground, else the binding, else the connection's, else `test`; a run with a parse error runs nothing.** The first makes Run at the cursor work in a file that starts with `use shop` (the SPEC does not say); the second follows mongosh, which reads a script before it runs it, and avoids half a playground changing data: the Messages tab lists every mistake with its place and a file gets markers. Run at the cursor on a line that has no statement (a comment, an empty line) runs the next statement, else the last (`nextItem`, the rule of SQL's `dialect.At`); the parser's `Script.At` is unchanged.
354. **Safety is a check of the words of the translated call, and the destructive set is the SPEC's.** `Classify` says read, write or destructive: the reads are the finds, counts, `distinct`, `getIndexes`, `show`, an aggregation with no `$out` or `$merge` and the commands on the list `readCommands`; every command not on it is a write for a read-only connection (a refusal is safe, a wrong "read" is not). Destructive: `drop`, `dropDatabase` (also written as `db.runCommand`), `deleteMany` and `updateMany` with an empty filter (also inside a `bulkWrite`). MongoDB has no read-only session, so a function that writes inside a `$function` or a server-side script is not seen; the server's roles are the second line.
355. **Paging, and what a run keeps.** The batch size is the page, so the server sends no more than the page shows; a `find().limit(n)` with `n` not above the page gets a batch of `n + 1`, because a batch of exactly `n` leaves the cursor open on the server (found on `mongo:7.0.14`) and the panel would offer a "More" that reads nothing. A set stops at 10,000 documents (`maxSetDocs`, then "Reading stops at ..."). Only the last run of an editor is kept (SQL keeps 10, the SPEC asks for none here); the cursors of a run are closed when it is replaced, when the editor closes and when the extension is deactivated (the tab keeps the open cursors under a lock for the goroutine of the deactivation).
356. **The JSON view is a highlighted list of lines, not an editor.** §10.22 says "a read-only editor", but an extension cannot make a read-only editor in a panel (`Documents().OpenUntitled` is editable and a panel holds widgets). The view is the list the container extension uses for its read-only documents (tokens from the JSON grammar, `Languages().Tokenize`), one line per row, a click selects the document the line is in (a band behind its lines), the list keeps its place when More adds documents. The selection works in the three views (a tree row, a JSON line, a table row); Edit, Delete and Copy act on it, and a set of one document needs none.
357. **Editing: an untitled `json` document and Ctrl+S, never a file.** The step says "an editor tab"; an extension has no virtual file system and no hook on the save of a document, and a file in a cache folder would write the data of a collection to disk (the results are never written, decision 324's rule). So the document opens with `Documents().OpenUntitled("json", text)`, and the command **Save Document to the Server** takes Ctrl+S while the active editor is such a document (the context key `emetos.mongodb.editingDocument`, set when the editor changes; the default Save As is still on Ctrl+Shift+S). The untitled document stays "changed" after the save (it is, as every untitled document with text). The confirmation is a quick pick (Replace, Cancel, then a row for each changed field) because the API has no richer dialog; Replace is the first row, so Enter confirms, as in the SQL confirmation. Types: the relaxed text shows an int64 or a date as a plain number or `$date`, so the saved document takes the **stored** value of every field whose relaxed text did not change (`Merge`, also inside arrays of the same length); only changed fields take the parsed value. `_id` cannot change and must be there. It is **last write wins**: no check that the document is as it was read; the text says "replaceOne({_id})" and a missing document is reported. Edit and Delete are offered only for whole documents (a find without a projection, with an `_id`) on a connection that is not read-only; the commands say why when they cannot.
358. **Delete Document is `deleteOne({_id})` after the quick pick** ("This cannot be undone."); the document leaves every result that shows it, also when the server says it was not there.
359. **Cancel is the context of the statement.** The driver stops waiting and drops the connection of the operation; the server may finish it (no `killOp`). `maxTimeMS` is an option of the methods that have it, turned into a deadline of the call. A cancelled run says "Cancelled after ..." and counts the statements not run.
360. **Error codes.** A command error has its code and `codeName` from the driver; a write error has a code and no name, so a small table (`codeNames`: DuplicateKey 11000, DocumentValidationFailure 121, Unauthorized 13, BadValue 2, NamespaceNotFound 26, MaxTimeMSExpired 50, WriteConflict 112, Interrupted 11601) fills the name in, and "Error 11000 (DuplicateKey): ..." shows. The code is in the metadata of the error (`ServerError`), never parsed from its text. A failed `Distinct` is read with `Err()` first: the driver's `Decode` of a failed result says "unexpected EOF" instead (found on the wire test).
361. **Tests: a small server on the wire for the real backend, and the proof against a labelled container by hand.** `wire_test.go` now answers `find`, `getMore`, `insert`, `update`, `delete`, `distinct` and the index commands and reads OP_MSG document sequences, so every call of `driverBackend.Exec` is tested without Docker, with the codes of failures. The proof (`cmd/emetos/mongodb_display_test.go`, tag `emetos_display`) needs a MongoDB: it is skipped without `EMETOS_PROOF_MONGO_PORT`, and the run starts a labelled `mongo:7.0.14` container by hand (the command is in the test) and removes it. The test host is `localhost`, not an address.
362. **Events and settings.** `commandFinished` is published for each statement and for each document saved or deleted (`ok`, `millis`, the connection's ID; no text, no value). `mongodb.pageSize` (1 to 1000, 20) and `mongodb.confirmDestructive` (true) are registered. The view's title actions are Run and Cancel as in SQL; the keys are Ctrl+Enter, Ctrl+Shift+Enter, Escape (while running) and Ctrl+S (while editing a document), and the editor's context menu has Run, Run All, Select Connection and Save Document.

## 2026-10-29: step 22.1 (a container's database, and the refusal to clients; decided by the agent, to be accepted by me)

390. **A container is a known database by the repository of its image, and the item is offered for the seven images of §10.24 only.** The reference is read without the tag, the digest and the names of Docker Hub (`docker.io/`, `index.docker.io/`, `registry-1.docker.io/`, `registry.hub.docker.com/`, `library/`), in lower case: `docker.io/library/postgres:17.6-alpine` is `postgres`, `docker.io/valkey/valkey:8` is `valkey/valkey`, `mcr.microsoft.com/mssql/server:2022-latest` stays whole. `bitnami/postgresql`, `redis/redis-stack` and the like are not offered: their environment is another one, and a prefill that guesses wrong is worse than none. Connect to Database is in the menu of one running container of such an image, and the command `containers.containers.connectDatabase` (`podman.containers.connectDatabase`) offers only those. `mariadb` and `mysql` are the engine `mysql`, `valkey` is `redis`.
391. **User and database come from the image's own variables, and the password from nowhere.** PostgreSQL: `POSTGRES_USER` (else `postgres`) and `POSTGRES_DB` (else the user, as the image does). MySQL: `MYSQL_USER` else `root`, and `MYSQL_DATABASE`. MariaDB: `MARIADB_USER` then `MYSQL_USER` else `root`, and `MARIADB_DATABASE` then `MYSQL_DATABASE`. SQL Server: `sa` and no database. MongoDB: `MONGO_INITDB_ROOT_USERNAME` with the database `admin` (where the image creates the root user and where it logs in; the form's database is the auth source), no user and no database when it is unset (the image then has no authentication). Redis and Valkey: neither. `POSTGRES_PASSWORD`, `MYSQL_ROOT_PASSWORD`, `MYSQL_PASSWORD`, `MARIADB_ROOT_PASSWORD`, `MSSQL_SA_PASSWORD`, `SA_PASSWORD`, `MONGO_INITDB_ROOT_PASSWORD` and `REDIS_PASSWORD` are never looked at, and the payload (`DatabaseTarget`, `dbconn.Prefill`) has no field that could hold one. The user types the password in the form.
392. **The host is the published address, the loopback address for "every address", or the host of a TCP engine; an unpublished port opens no form.** A binding on `127.0.0.1` or any one address is used as it is; `0.0.0.0`, `::` and an empty address become `127.0.0.1` (not `localhost`, which may resolve to `::1` first), or the host of the engine when the engine is `tcp://` (a Docker on another computer, where the published ports are). The first binding with a port is used. A container that does not publish its server's port, or is not running, gets a warning that says what to do (`run it with -p`, `start it first`) and publishes no event: guessing the address of the container on its bridge would work on Linux only for Docker's default bridge and not for Podman's rootless networks.
393. **The payload has one more field, `name` (the container's name, which names the connection); each container extension publishes its own topic.** §10.24 says `{engine, host, port, user, database}`; the name saves the user from typing what the container already has. The topics are `emetos.containers.connectDatabase` and `emetos.podman.connectDatabase` (the extension's id and `.connectDatabase`, `Config.ConnectDatabaseTopic`), because an extension publishes only the topics under its own id; the database extensions listen to both (`dbconn.ContainerTopics`). The menu item runs the command `containers.containers.connectDatabase` / `podman.containers.connectDatabase`, which is refused to clients with the other commands of the prefixes.
394. **The event to form flow is: event, the database extension of the engine, a request to the Databases extension.** The form is the Databases extension's editor kind, which another extension cannot open, and an extension cannot run a command of another (`commands.execute` is the Terminal's only), so each of SQL (`postgres`, `mysql`, `sqlserver`), MongoDB and Redis calls `dbconn.HandleContainerDatabase`, which listens to the two topics and, for an engine of its own, asks the request `emetos.databases.openForm` (handled by the Databases extension) with a `dbconn.Prefill`. The command `emetos.databases.newConnection` takes the same arguments (`engine`, `name`, `host`, `port`, `user`, `database`) as the §10.24 text says. The Databases extension and Redis are now activated on startup: an event reaches only the extensions that are active, and Redis was active only when a view of it or a command was used. Activating the Databases extension reads nothing (the connection file is read when its view is first shown).
395. **Only the container extension's own event is believed, and a prefilled value is cleaned.** The database extensions act on an event only when its publisher is the owner of the topic (`emetos.containers` for `emetos.containers.connectDatabase`). (The first version also made `emetos.databases.openForm` refuse a request from a client of the removed extension, with the tag `DatabasesOpenFormRefused`; the refusal and the tag went with that extension in step 23.1.) A text of the payload with a control character, longer than 256 characters or holding `${` is left out of the form: the form expands `${env:NAME}` when a connection opens, and what a container's environment says must not be able to name a variable of this computer. The form opens unsaved; nothing is written before Save.
396. **The class `outside` has more prefixes than §10.24 names.** The real command ids are `emetos.sql.*`, `mongodb.*` and `emetos.redis.*`, not `sql.*` and `redis.*` as §10.24 writes them: the rule keeps `sql.`, `redis.` and adds `emetos.redis.` (Redis was not refused at all before this step), `emetos.mongodb.`, `emetos.containers.` and `emetos.podman.`, so that a command named either way is refused. `emetos.commands.list` marks them from the same rule (no change was needed there; the test registers them and reads the list).
397. **The refusal is tested over the manifests of the extensions, in `cmd/emetos`.** Only `cmd/emetos` sees both `extensions.All()` and `internal/core/command` (an extension may not import the core and the core may not import the extensions). `TestEveryCommandOfTheContainerAndDatabaseExtensionsIsRefusedToClients` checks every command of the six extensions (and the Databases extension) against `ClientRefusal`, and `TestNoCommandOfAnExtensionThatLooksOutsideIsForgotten` extends the scan of risky ids to every extension's commands (words: database, container, docker, podman, sql, mongo, redis, kube, quadlet). None of the six adds a command with `Commands().Register` at run time, so the manifests are all of them.
398. **Tests and their helpers.** `containerapi.ParseInspect` (the decoding of an inspect answer, for the table of §10.24's images from JSON of Docker's and Podman's shapes), `fakeapi.EmitAs` (an event as another extension published it) and `containerui.ContainersListed` (for the tests that run a command on a container of a fake engine; the same kind of export as `dbconn.TreeRows`) are the only additions outside the step's own files. The event to form flow is tested with the real SQL, MongoDB, Redis, Databases and container extensions (Docker's and Podman's configuration) over fake APIs and a fake engine (`extensions/connectdb_test.go`); the real postgres test is `TestConnectToDatabaseOnARealDocker` (`emetos_docker`) and its Podman twin (`emetos_podman`, written and vetted, not run: Podman's socket is not running on this machine). Both share one body (`connectdb_real_test.go`, tagged for either), which lists only the labelled containers, so the user's own are never listed or inspected.

400. **The log lines of the fake Docker engine use 203.0.113.7 (RFC 5737) instead of 172.18.0.1.** The new Containers shot of the README shows the logs of `shop-web-1` from the fake engine (`containerapitest/fakeengine`), and its lines had Docker's default bridge gateway as the client address. No test reads those lines; the rule "no real IP addresses in proof images" is kept to the letter.

The user's own containers are never listed or touched (only `docker rm -f <that name>`).

The connection files of the shots are written by the script into the neutral folder, with the paths of that folder in them.
403. **The README has a new section "Containers and databases"** (Docker and Podman, Databases and connections, SQL, MongoDB, Redis, Connect to Database, security notes), the old "MongoDB" subsection moved into it, and the short list entries of the bundled extensions point to it. The shots are `docker.png`, `sql.png`, `mongodb.png` and `redis.png` in `docs/screenshots/`.
404. **The coverage table of area 11 got six rows** (Containers and Podman, the Databases view and connections, SQL, MongoDB, Redis, Connect to Database and the refusal to clients) with the main test files of each, all `done`, with what is not built in the last column.


## 2026-10-29: step 23.1 (removing an extension and every mention of it; decided by the agent, to be accepted by me)

410. **The privilege `events.publishAny` is removed with the one extension that held it.** After the removal no extension on the host's allowlist may declare it, so nothing could use it and its tests would need a fake allowlist entry. `Manifest.Privileges` stays with `commands.execute` (the Terminal's, for Search Workspace); a manifest that declares `events.publishAny` is now refused with `ExtensionPrivilegeUnknown` like any unknown name, and a test says so. The tests of the core request topics use `emetos.terminal` as the privileged extension. If you want the privilege kept for a future extension, it is one constant, one check in `eventsAPI.Publish` and one test.
411. **`command.ClientRefusal` and its classes stay, and "clients" now means the clients of a hosted workspace.** The list is read by `emetos.commands.list` and `emetos.commands.execute` (the Terminal's Search Workspace). With the removed extension gone, nothing but those topics asks it, and Remote Work's clients drive a second view of the window and do not go through it; the list is kept because the commands it names still reach outside the workspace and D25 says so. The class `remote` names `emetos.remotework.*` and `emetos.hosting.*`. Flagged: if you think the list is dead without its extension, it can be removed with `emetos.commands.execute`.
412. **The Terminal lost what only the removed extension used:** the `by` of a client (`by` in a request is ignored; the stamp is the id of the requesting extension), the marks of clients on a tab (a second line on the row, its height in the drag of tabs, its golden `panel_tabs_client_*`), the topic `TopicClientGone` and its subscription, `openOpts.by`, and the rule that a request of that extension never takes the screen (a request without `show` is quiet for every extension). Everything else of 17d.4 stays.
413. **The Databases extension no longer refuses `emetos.databases.openForm` to a client** (`DatabasesOpenFormRefused`): the only publisher it named was the removed extension, and an event or a request carries the id of its real publisher. A request from another extension is answered as before; the container topics are still believed only from their owner.
414. **The two error Tags that named the removed extension were renamed** `CoreCommandRefused` (a command that a client may not run) and `CoreDocumentVersionStale` (an edit made against an old version): the tags of the failures of the core request topics, in the family of `CoreCommandNotFound` and `CoreDocumentReadOnly`. Their route tests were kept and renamed.
415. **The old state is left out, and the user's files stay.** A window tab of the removed kind is dropped at the restore with one line at information level (the existing rule for a tab whose kind no extension has), a restored editor tab or view state of an unknown extension is dropped or kept as the 17c.1 rules say, and nothing deletes the extension's state files and keys in the state folder of Emetos (the user's data). The test builds the old tab kind and the file names from the rot13 form of the name at run time (`removedExtensionWord`), so that `scripts/check-removed-name.sh` stays clean.
416. **The rule that lets an extension import the terminal emulator stays** (`internal/archtest`, with a neutral comment): the emulator is a library with no UI, and its boundary rules are tested; only the removed extension used the exception, but removing it would change a tested rule for no gain.
417. **The Sharing item stays generic over the hosts it is given** (`hosting.NewFactory(hosts ...)`), and `extensions/all.go` gives it Remote Work's. Its tests keep a second fake host ("Other Host") so that the listing, the counting and the choosing of several hosts stay tested.
418. **Proof images and fixtures.** Deleted: the five proof images about the removed extension (`16.2-*`, `16.3-*`, `17d.4-*`, `17e.4-*-hosting` and `17e.4-*-client-stopped` of it), the fixtures of its scripts (`testdata/proof/16.2`, `16.3`, `17d.4`, `testdata/screenshots/` for it) and the README screenshot. `17e.4-hosting.png` showed its entries in the Sharing menu and was made again by `TestRealInputHostWithRemoteWorkFromTheStatusItem`. The golden images of the Extensions page problem (`extensions_page_problem_*`) and of the title bar (`titlebar_extension_*`) were made again because their text named it. The other README screenshots did not show it.
419. **Documents.** The entries of steps 16.2 and 16.3 of `docs/IMPLEMENTED.md` and their decisions (69, 87 to 92, 105, 106, 180 to 183, 186, 187, 204) are removed; the other entries and decisions are reworded; D21 of the SPEC is marked withdrawn and D32 records the removal; M16 of `docs/IMPLEMENTATION.md` keeps step 16.1 only. `scripts/check-removed-name.sh` is the test: it builds the word from its rot13 form and searches the text and the file names of the repository. Git history is not rewritten by this step.

## 2026-10-29: the plan of M24, Git and Source Control (the user's request; details decided by the agent, to be reviewed by the user)
420. **The new extension is `extensions/gitscm` (`emetos.gitscm`, "Git"); `extensions/git` stays the language pack.** VS Code splits the same way (`git-base` has the grammars, `git` the source control). The pack is a language pack with no activation, counted in the list of bundled languages (D13) and in §5.2, and a user who turns Git off keeps the highlighting of `.gitignore` and commit messages. The command ids, settings and context keys keep VS Code's names (`git.*`), whatever the extension's id.
421. **The workbench owns the Source Control UI and the Git extension is a provider (D34).** The Source Control view, the Graph, the Timeline, the quick diff, the file decorations and the blame text are host features driven by new APIs (`SourceControl`, `Decorations`, `Timeline`, `EditorDecorations`), as in VS Code, and not widgets that the extension draws with `extension/ui`. The cost is a larger contract (listed in SPEC §10.30); the gain is UI tests with fake providers and a second provider with no change of the host. The Timeline and the decorations are generic services although Git is the only provider, because VS Code's are.
422. **Git 2.30 is the minimum.** VS Code branches on versions down to 2.13; Emetos supports the git of the last five years (`git stash push --staged` needs 2.35 and says so). A user with an older git sees the "missing" welcome content.
423. **Git's messages are made English with `LC_MESSAGES=C` and `LANGUAGE=en`, and `LC_ALL` is removed from the environment of git's commands.** VS Code sets `LC_ALL=en_US.UTF-8`, a locale most Linux systems do not have. Removing `LC_ALL` keeps the user's `LC_CTYPE` for hooks and the editor while the messages that the error classification reads stay English. Hooks see `LC_MESSAGES=C`.
424. **Git's askpass and editor programs are Emetos's own binary, reached through three scripts of three lines that hold no secret (D35).** The binary gets the flags `--git-askpass`, `--git-ssh-askpass` and `--git-editor`, handled before any window code; it talks to the extension over a Unix socket in a 0700 folder of `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, with a random token given only through the environment and the peer's user id checked. Nothing is cached or stored; git's `credential.helper` and the SSH agent own the user's saved secrets. VS Code needs Node for the same job.
425. **The tests use the real `git` by default, with no build tag (D36).** The Docker tests are tagged because they touch the user's engine; `git` in a temporary, fully isolated repository touches nothing, so a skipped test would only hide regressions. The harness removes every source of the user's git configuration and sets `GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES`, so no test can see the project's own repository. HTTP authentication is tested against `git http-backend` behind a Go handler; SSH is tested by its prompt strings, with no `sshd`.
426. **A repository is trusted before Emetos runs `git` in it (D37); the user chose this on 2026-10-30, replacing my first plan of hardening plus one notification.** The first time Emetos would run git in a repository it asks (Trust Repository, Trust All Repositories in the parent folder, Don't Trust); the answer is kept by real path in `$XDG_STATE_HOME/emetos/git-trust.json`, not in git's configuration; until then no git process runs in the repository and the Source Control view says why with a button. Emetos's own background commands stay hardened after the answer (no hooks, no `core.fsmonitor`, pager, external diff or textconv, no optional locks) and `safe.directory` is respected. `filter.*.clean` still runs for `git status` once trusted (turning it off breaks Git LFS).
427. **A multi-file diff editor is built (step 24.17).** VS Code opens a group's changes, a commit and a stash in it (`git.viewChanges`, `viewCommit`, `stashView`, "Open All Changes"), and the Graph and the Timeline lead to it. Emetos has only the two-file diff editor, so the plan adds an editor kind that stacks its sections, built lazily.
428. **`git.openMergeEditor` stays the merge editor's own command (step 9.8); the extension adds the Merge Changes group, `git.runGitMerge` and `git.acceptMerge`.** Complete Merge publishes a new core event `emetos.mergeEditor.completed` and the extension stages the file on it (VS Code's `acceptMerge` stages in the same place). The merge editor itself is not changed otherwise.
429. **Format on save of modifications takes the quick diff's original (the index version) when a repository has the file (step 24.11)**, as VS Code does, and the file on disk otherwise; decision 3 of step 12.8 ("not source control") is superseded.
430. **Publish Branch pushes to any remote the user picks, and creates nothing on a service (D33).** `git.publish` is VS Code's command without its GitHub publisher; with no remote it asks for one (`git.addRemote`). There are no remote source providers, so Clone asks for a URL.
431. **Autofetch is off, as in VS Code, and its one question is asked once, after the first good remote operation.** The answer Yes writes `git.autofetch: true` to the user settings; No and Ask Me Later are kept in the profile's state. No `git fetch` runs on a schedule before an answer.
432. **A stage or revert of one change never goes through the work tree.** The new index text is built from the index version and the selected changes and written with `hash-object` and `update-index --cacheinfo`, so the file the user is editing is never rewritten (VS Code does the same: `hash-object -w --stdin`, then `update-index --cacheinfo`; the plan keeps that, with `--path` so the file's filters and attributes apply).
433. **The hover of the blame text accepts `command:` links, only to commands of the extension that made the hover.** VS Code needs `isTrusted`; the compiled-in extensions are trusted, but a link must not reach another extension's command (the same limit as the commands of events, §10.15).
434. **The activity bar gets badges (step 24.3).** §10.4 said they were not built; the Source Control icon's count needs them, and they are generic (`activityBarBadge.*`).
435. **A terminal gets git's variables through a host service, `TerminalEnvironment`, not an event (step 24.23).** The Terminal extension reads the contributions when it starts a shell, as VS Code's environment variable collection works; it is the API the pairing of Git with the terminal needs, without either extension importing the other.
436. **Not built:** the repositories' artifacts tree (`scm.repositories.explorer`, off by default in VS Code and not asked for), Local History in the Timeline, `git.branchRandomName.*` (needs a Node package's word lists), the `worktree-created` tasks, and the AI features. They are listed in SPEC §10.30 "Not built". (Worktrees were on this list in the first plan; the user asked for them on 2026-10-30, step 24.19.)
437. **Remote Work clients may use every Git command, with no refusal class (D38; the user's choice, 2026-10-30).** A connected client can fetch, pull, push, sync and clone with the host's credential helpers, SSH keys and agent, as if at the host's keyboard, like the host's Terminal. The prompts and the trust question appear in the host's window, which the client sees. The host's protection is who may connect (§10.18).
438. **Worktrees follow VS Code 1.143 (step 24.19).** Open Worktree in Current Window replaces the active workspace tab's folder and Open in New Window opens a new workspace tab, because Emetos has one window (D17). A worktree is a repository of the Source Control view (the `worktree` icon, detected when `git.detectWorktrees` is true, as VS Code's default is false), shares its parent's trust, and `git.worktreeIncludeFiles` and `git.worktreeSymlinkFolders` copy and link ignored files after creation as VS Code does. The `worktree-created` tasks are not built (Emetos has no such task type).
439. **Details of the trust question (step 24.5).** Discovery looks at the file system (a `.git` folder or file) so that no git runs to find a repository; the dialog lists the programs the local configuration names, read by Emetos's own reader of `.git/config`; Don't Trust lasts the session (VS Code's restricted mode is also asked again); submodules and worktrees of a trusted repository, and what Clone or Initialize Repository makes, are trusted without a question; the runner refuses to start git in an untrusted repository (`GitRepositoryNotTrusted`), so a later step cannot forget the rule; the proofs use a dev-only knob, `EMETOS_DEV_GIT_TRUST`.

## 2026-10-30: step 24.2, the Source Control API and its model (decided by the agent, to be reviewed by the user)
440. **A source control is told by its `ID` and its `RootURI`, not by its `ID` alone.** SPEC §10.30 said "the id is unique per extension", but the context key of every menu of VS Code's git extension is `scmProvider == git` for all of its repositories, and the Git extension (step 24.5) has one source control for each. `ID` is VS Code's provider id and may be shared; two `Create` calls of one extension with the same `ID` and `RootURI` fail with `ExtensionSCMDuplicate`. A source control also has a `Handle()` (`scm1`, unique in the scope), which the arguments of the menus carry, so that a command knows which repository it was run for.
441. **The menu constants have the MenuId names of VS Code as values** (`SCMTitle`, `SCMResourceContext`, ...), not the keys of the contribution points (`scm/title`, ...) that the SPEC example wrote. The existing constants (`MenubarFileMenu`, `EditorContext`, `GlobalActivity`) are MenuId names, and the contract's constants must equal those of `core/command` (a test compares them), so the registry of menus needs one convention. `SCMTitle` and the others are also constants of `internal/core/command`.
442. **The arguments of a menu command are one list of objects, not separate arguments.** `command.Handler` takes one `args any`. A menu of the Source Control view gives `[]any{{"sourceControl": handle, "group": id, "uri": uri, "contextValue": value}, ...}` in the order VS Code passes its arguments (the source control for the menus of a repository; for the others the selected rows, or the row alone when it is not selected, a group as its object, a resource as its object and a folder as the objects of the resources below it), and `extension.SCMArgs` reads it. The model builds them (`scm.SelectionMenuArgs`, `scm.SourceMenuArgs`).
443. **`Command` is the existing type of the code lenses with two fields added**, `Tooltip` and `Args`, instead of a second type with the plan's name. A command of the Source Control API uses `Args` (one JSON-compatible value); the language providers keep `Arguments`. The host runs a command with `Args` when it is set, else with the JSON arguments (one value, or a list of them).
444. **The ids of the commands of the model are VS Code's** (`workbench.scm.action.setListViewMode`, `setTreeViewMode`, `setSortKey.name|path|status`, `repositories.setSortKey.*`, `repositories.setSelectionMode.*`, `scm.setActiveProvider`), not the names the SPEC gave (`toggleViewAsTree`, `setSortBy*`), which VS Code does not have; none is in the palette, as in VS Code (`f1: false`). The two selection mode commands write the setting `scm.repositories.selectionMode`, as VS Code's do, and the model follows the setting.
445. **`SourceControl.SetActiveRepositoryName` is in the contract** (VS Code's proposed `activeRepositoryName`), because `activity.test.ts` tests it and step 24.19 (worktrees) needs it: the context key `scmActiveRepositoryName` is that name, else the name of the root folder. Also added to the plan's list: `Handle()` (440).
446. **The model is safe for any goroutine and announces changes from the goroutine that made them** (`Model.Subscribe`); the view (step 24.3) hands them to the UI thread. The sorts and the tree of a group are made lazily and once for each version of its resources; the tree sorts a folder when it is first asked for its rows. The sort uses the root collation of `golang.org/x/text` with the options of VS Code's `compareFileNames` (numeric, no case, no accents), as `core/workspace` does.

## 2026-10-31: step 24.3, the Source Control view (decided by the agent, to be reviewed by the user)
447. **The letter and the color of a resource row come from a file decoration lookup, as in VS Code, not from `ResourceDecorations`.** VS Code's `scmViewPane` asks the file decoration service for the letter (`M A D U`), the color and the tooltip of a resource; its `ISCMResource.decorations` have only the icon, the tooltip, the strike-through and the faded mark. `extension.ResourceDecorations` therefore gets no letter field and SPEC §10.30 stays as it was (the Git extension gives the letters through `api.Decorations()`, step 24.6). Until that API exists the window keeps a map of decorations by URI (`gui/app` `scmFileDecorations`), which `dev.scm.fake` fills and the view reads through `Options.Decoration`. The color is a theme color id, looked up with the new `kit.Colors.Named` (the theme the colors came from); the ten `gitDecoration.*ResourceForeground` colors are registered with VS Code's defaults (`core/theme/colors_scm.go`).
448. **The Repositories view is `HideByDefault` and has VS Code's condition** (`scm.providerCount` set and not 0), so it is in the "..." menu of the Changes view's header and in `workbench.scm.repositories.focus`, as in VS Code, and not open on its own. The Changes view shows the repository headers itself when more than one repository is selected (`scm.alwaysShowRepositories` forces them).
449. **The folds of the view are kept by provider and root, not by handle.** A handle (`scm1`) is new in every run, so the saved state keys the folded repositories, groups and folders by `StorageKey()` (provider id, NUL, root URI, as VS Code's `getProviderStorageKey`). The state is the window's `SCM` entry of the state file (`scmWindowState{Model, View}`), read before the extensions start; a source control that arrives within 5 seconds of the start is matched with the saved list of visible repositories as in `scmViewService.onDidAddRepository` (`Model.Restore`, `FinishRestore`). The folds of hidden repositories are kept too.
450. **Welcome content is generic (`Contributes.ViewWelcome`) and the view name `scm` is an alias for `workbench.scm`, `explorer` for `workbench.explorer.emptyView`.** The manifest check refuses a piece with no view, no contents, or a `when` or `enablement` that does not parse (`ExtensionViewWelcomeInvalid`). The host (`exthost.Scope.ViewWelcome`) returns the pieces that hold, ordered by group and by the place after the `@`. A piece of the Changes view is drawn by the new `views/welcome` (Markdown paragraphs with links, a paragraph that is only `[Label](command:id?args)` is a button, disabled by `enablement`); the default text "No source control providers registered." shows when no piece applies. **The Explorer's empty view does not draw the pieces yet**: its own welcome has a layout of its own and the first piece for it comes with the Git extension's "Clone Repository" (step 24.5 or later); the scope already serves them (`HasViewWelcome(command.ViewExplorerEmpty)`).
451. **A link in welcome text opens through the same `command:` convention as VS Code, with the query as JSON**; a web link opens in the desktop's browser. A paragraph with several links draws them through the Markdown `Flow` view (links are not clickable inside Glutter's rich text, so a line made only of one link is a button, as the Git welcome texts are written).
452. **The sort of a group is made on the goroutine that changed the model.** `View.onModelEvent` calls `Group.Sorted` for the visible groups in list mode, so that the UI thread finds the 100,000 sorted resources made when it builds the rows (the sort took 200 ms on the UI thread, over §14's 100 ms for the first frame). The tree of the tree mode belongs to the UI thread and is still made there. `kit.ListViewModel.SetItems` builds one map instead of two, for the same reason.
453. **`kit.DarkModern()` and `kit.LightModern()` are made once** (`sync.OnceValue`), because `kit.Colors` now holds the pointer of its theme (`ByID`) and the theme widget decides whether to notify by comparing two `Colors`.
454. **The status bar items of the active repository** are `status.scm.0`, `status.scm.1`, ... at the left end, priority 10000 going down by 0.001 (VS Code's `StatusBarAlignment.LEFT, 10000`), and, with several repositories, `status.scm.provider` (the repository's name, `repo` or `repo-pinned`, the command `scm.setActiveProvider`). A status command's text may use `$(name)`; the Codicon aliases that `codicon.csv` does not list (`git-branch`, `git-branch-create`, `git-branch-delete`) are in `fonts.codiconAliases`; **the other 113 aliases of `codicon.css` (`plus`, `x`, `pencil`, `file`, `star`, ...) are still missing** and `scripts/update-assets.sh` should read them from the CSS one day.
455. **The badge counts what `Model.ActivityBadge` says** (VS Code's `scm.countBadge`, `scm.providerCountBadge`), drawn on the icon of the container by `Views.SetBadge`: a pill of 16 px in `activityBarBadge.background` with 9 px bold text, `99+` above 99, and the tooltip "Source Control - 4 pending changes". The badge is taken away when the window's shell goes (after the panels stopped following the views: a badge that goes would otherwise make a widget that is being removed build again).
456. **Dragging a row uses the tab drag of the window** (`extEditorHost.BeginDrag`, the same session an extension's tab uses), because the editor area already decides the group and the place; a drop on a group that no longer exists fails with `ScmDropGroupMissing`.
457. **The 100,000-resource test measures the processor time of the thread**, not the wall clock (`RUSAGE_THREAD` through `golang.org/x/sys/unix`, already a dependency), because the other agents' builds load the machine and a wall clock measured 2 to 4 times the work. The first frame is the best of three attempts; two of 60 frames of the steady scroll may pass 16 ms; under the race detector the budgets are ten times as long. Measured at a load average of 54: first frame 80 to 108 ms, longest steady frame 11 to 19 ms. On the idle machine the figures are in the IMPLEMENTED entry.
458. **`dev.scm.fake`** reads a JSON fixture (path relative to the folder Emetos runs in) and adds source controls with groups, resources, status items and decorations, plus the commands and menu items a Git-like provider has (`dev.scm.stage`, ...), once for the window, so goldens and proofs need no provider. It is a dev command (debug and `emetos_dev` builds, D15); SPEC §15.2 lists it.

## 2026-10-31: Glutter 1.9.8 and the signal that ends the program (decided by the agent, to be reviewed by the user)
459. **SIGINT and SIGTERM end Emetos as a Quit without any dialog (SPEC §10.3, "Quitting on a signal").** Documents with unsaved changes are backed up as the hot exit's "Back up" does (kept for the next start), the state files are written, the application ends as an agreed quit (extension host, language servers, debug adapters, terminals and child processes stop as on a normal quit), and the exit status is 0. A backup that fails is logged and does not hold the exit; all the writes together are bounded by 30 s and the whole end by 40 s; the first signal gives the default action back, so a second signal ends the process at once. The code for the quit already existed (`Workspaces.watchSignal`, step 9b.1) and worked, but `widgets.RunApp` ignored the context; Glutter 1.9.8 adds `RunAppContext`, which Emetos now uses, as the last resort only. **Where the quit runs:** Glutter ends a run whose context ends as `ExitApplication(AppExitTypeRequired)` at once, asking neither the observers nor `OnExitRequested`, so passing it the signal context would end the loop before the backups, and running the backups after `RunAppContext` returned would touch documents and trackers whose widgets are already disposed (and the extension host already stopped). I kept the quit on the UI thread, before the loop ends, where the documents are read as in every other quit ("only the UI thread touches widgets"), and gave `RunAppContext` a context of its own that ends when the loop returned or 40 s after the signal (`signalQuitGrace`, the 30 s of the backups and 10 s more), so a UI thread that is stuck cannot keep the process alive. Without a hot exit the signal still does not ask: a terminal that sent Ctrl+C or a system that sends SIGTERM has nobody to answer a dialog.

## 2026-10-31: step 24.4, the commit input and the action button (decided by the agent, to be reviewed by the user)
460. **The box and the button are rows of the Changes list**, as the tree of `scmViewPane.ts` has them: the list keeps one flat model and its rows now have two more kinds. A row that holds a widget of its own is *embedded* (`kit.ListOpts.Embedded`, `ListViewModelOpts.Blocked`): the list draws no hover, selection or click on it, the keyboard passes over it, and a text field in it keeps the arrow keys, Home and End. The height of the box row is what the box wants, which it reports after the frame (`InputBoxOpts.OnHeightChange`), so growth moves the rows below it a frame later; `ListViewModel.RefreshHeights` makes the new heights known. Up and Down from the groups do not reach the box or the button (VS Code focuses them from the tree); they take the keyboard by a click, Tab, `Ctrl+Shift+G` (the view gives it to the box of the first repository when no row has the focus, as `SCMViewPane.focus` does) and `SCMInput.Focus`.
461. **The box is `kit.InputBox`, not the editor widget.** The step allowed either if the plain-text editing keys, undo, selection, clipboard and input method behave the same: they come from Glutter's `EditableText`, the box already had a multi-line form (the find widget's), and the editor widget would bring a document, a language and decorations the box does not use (VS Code's own is a code editor with most contributions removed). `InputBox` got `MinLines`, `MaxLines`, `FontSize`, `LineHeight`, `FontFamily`, `PaddingV`, `Radius`, `OnHeightChange` and `CollapseSelectionOnEscape`, with the old behavior as the default. The input is not a code editor: it has no snippets, suggestions, color decorators or format on type.
462. **A list of 100,000 rows of different heights needs its own sliver** (`kit/sliverrows.go`). Glutter's `SliverVariedExtentList` adds up the heights of the rows before a row for each child it lays out; with the input and the button in the list the first frame was fine but a jump to the end took 40 to 60 ms (budget 32 ms, §14). The sliver answers from the tops of the rows with a binary search, through the hooks `RenderSliverFixedExtentBoxAdaptor` has for a carousel, and tells the exact extent of the list so that a jump to the end is not cut short by the estimate. A `// Glutter:` workaround, listed in `docs/GLUTTER-ISSUES.md`. **Withdrawn with Glutter 1.9.9 (609):** the sliver is gone; `SliverVariedExtentList` does this itself.
463. **The keys are VS Code's, and Escape has three stages.** `scm.viewPreviousCommit` is Up with the caret at the very start of the text and `scm.viewNextCommit` Down at the very end (`scmInputIsInFirstPosition`, `scmInputIsInLastPosition`: line 1 column 1, and the end of the last line); `scm.forceView...` are Alt+Up and Alt+Down anywhere. SPEC §10.30 had Ctrl+Up and Ctrl+Down, which VS Code does not bind; the SPEC is changed. Escape: `scm.clearValidation` while a message is shown, else (the box collapses a selection itself, as the editor's `cancelSelection` does: `CollapseSelectionOnEscape`) `scm.clearInput` when nothing is selected. The context keys are set in a scope of the box that the key dispatcher finds through its focus node (`kit.FocusScopes`, as the find widget's), and the handlers read `scmRepository` from the context they are run in.
464. **The message history is the session's, per provider and folder, in the model** (`core/scm/history.go`, VS Code's `HistoryNavigator2` of 100 messages). VS Code keeps it in the workspace storage across restarts; the plan says "of the session", so Emetos does not save it. `Input.SetValue` (the provider's) puts the old text into the history and the new one after it, `SetTransientValue` (typing, `scm.clearInput`) does not, and a repository that is closed and found again has its messages and its unsent text back. The extension's `OnChange` sees every change, the history moves too.
465. **The validation function takes the text only and runs on each change** (the contract of step 24.2 has no caret offset, so the caret does not trigger it, and there is no 200 ms throttle: the function is the provider's and must be fast). The message is drawn under the box while the box has the keyboard (the box's own overlay, over the button as VS Code's context view is) and not after Escape took it away or the box lost the keyboard; the border keeps the color of the level until the text changes. VS Code's `showValidationMessage` with its 5, 8 and 10 second timeouts is not in the contract, and a scroll of the list does not clear the message.
466. **`scm.showInputActionButton` is registered and draws nothing.** It shows the toolbar at the right of the box, whose only item in VS Code is the AI "Generate Commit Message" (the menu `SCMInputBox`); Emetos has no such feature and no such menu (SPEC "Not built").
467. **The list's keyboard is left to a text field.** A key that comes from a text field in the list (the box) is not the list's: before this step the list took Up, Down, Home, End and Enter from it. The list also no longer focuses a row when the keyboard goes to a text field inside it (it focused the first row and scrolled to it). Both are generic and tested in `kit`.
468. **The proof shows the warning as the border of the box, not as its message text.** VS Code draws the message while the box has the keyboard and closes it when the keyboard goes elsewhere; the dropdown of the button takes the keyboard, and the message would cover the button anyway (it opens over the row below the box). The one image of the plan (input, message, button with its dropdown open) therefore shows the box in the color of the warning (VS Code keeps that after the message closes), the two-line text, the button and its menu; the message text is in the goldens of `internal/gui/views/scm` (`input_validation_*`).
469. **The real-input test types ASCII only.** The virtual keyboard of the display tests has the US layout (`realinput.strokes`), so letters such as é or ñ cannot be typed through the compositor. `TestRealInputSCMCommitBox` clicks the box, types a subject with spaces and capitals, a new line (Enter) and a second line, and runs Ctrl+Enter, Escape, Up, Alt+Down and the button for real; a text with non-ASCII letters is set with `dev.scm.input` and accepted with Ctrl+Enter, and the widget tests (`internal/gui/views/scm`, `internal/gui/app`) type such letters through the input method path (`EnterText`).
470. **Dev commands.** `dev.scm.fake` takes `input` and `dropdown` in its arguments and, per source control in the fixture, `placeholder`, `hideInput`, `actionButton` and `validation`; `dev.scm.input` sets or reads the box and reports the commands the fake provider ran (SPEC §15.2). The fake commit command records the text and empties the box, as a provider does, so that Up has a message to bring back.

## 2026-10-31: Step 24.5, the Git extension: repositories and status (decisions 480 to 495)

480. **The trust guarantee is checked in the runner, not in the extension.** `git.Config.Trust` decides for every command whether its repository (the working directory, `-C`, `--git-dir`, `--work-tree`, `GIT_DIR`, `GIT_WORK_TREE`, `GIT_COMMON_DIR`) is trusted, and `Runner.execute` refuses with `GitRepositoryNotTrusted` before it starts a process; a runner without a `Trust` function trusts nothing. A later step that forgets to ask the question gets an error instead of a process. `GIT_DIR`, `GIT_WORK_TREE` and `GIT_COMMON_DIR` are also removed from the environment git is started with.
481. **`api.Dialogs()` is new** (`extension/dialogs.go`): `Ask(DialogSpec{Type, Message, Detail, Buttons}, func(DialogResult))`, modal in the window, one at a time, answered with the index of the button (`Cancelled` for Escape or the close button). It was needed for the trust question; the host shows it with `kit.Dialog`. The buttons of `kit.Dialog` now wrap (`widgets.NewWrap`) instead of overflowing a row, as VS Code's `dialog-buttons` does (`flex-wrap`); a golden `dialog_trust_{dark,light}.png` shows it.
482. **`Workspace().Watch` is a subscription of the window's watcher** (`Watcher.Subscribe`, a `Target` with a sink), not a second watcher: roots are shared, so the git directory of a parent repository costs one watch. The watcher is the one of the window (inotify, with the polling fallback), so a machine that has run out of inotify instances polls and the user gets the existing notice.
483. **`emetos.window.focusChanged` is published by the app** (`autosave.go`, which already follows the focus of the window) in the scope of the active window tab; the Git extension refreshes when the focus comes back.
484. **`SettingSpec` gains `AlsoTypes`, `Scope` and `EnumDescriptions`**, because `git.path` is a string, null or an array and VS Code scopes most git settings (`resource`, `machine`); `CheckManifest` refuses an unknown scope or type (`ExtensionSettingDeclInvalid`).
485. **Discovery runs no process** and finds `.git` folders and files, `gitdir:` and `commondir` lines in Go (`git.FindRepository`, `git.RepositoryAt`); the tests compare it with `git rev-parse` on the fixtures. A repository whose `.git` is damaged is not listed; git's own ownership check (`safe.directory`) is met only after the trust answer, when the first command fails with `UnsafeRepository`, and the repository is then listed in Manage Unsafe Repositories (nothing adds a `safe.directory` without the confirmation).
486. **The ignore matcher of the watcher is Emetos's own** (`.gitignore` of every folder above the path and `info/exclude`, with the rules of gitignore(5); a test compares it with `git check-ignore` on 33 paths). It does not read `core.excludesFile`: a path ignored only there starts one status per burst (the safe side). A tracked file is never dropped.
487. **A burst makes one status because the window is fixed**: the timer starts at the first change and the status runs 500 ms later, whatever else arrives (VS Code's `onDidChange` is `debounce`d the same way). Emetos's own commands hold a counter that suppresses the watcher, and one status runs when the last one ends.
488. **The notification about the programs of a configuration is shown for the repositories the user was not asked about**: those covered by "Trust All Repositories in ..." (other than the one of the question, whose dialog listed the programs) and the submodules and worktrees of a trusted repository, once per session and repository; a repository that the user trusted by itself gets none. The list comes from Emetos's own reader of `.git/config` and `config.worktree`, which runs nothing.
489. **The Source Control view shows the welcome content when its list has no row**, not only when there is no source control, because a repository that waits for the trust question has a source control with no group, and the view must say how to trust it. When no piece holds and a source control exists the (empty) list is shown; the text "No source control providers registered." stays for no source control.
490. **The grouping of a status moved off the UI thread**: `splitStatus` and the building of the host's resources run on the goroutine of the command (`Timings.Group`); the UI thread hands the four lists to the host. With 100,000 changed files this took tens of milliseconds on the UI thread.
491. **A bug of 24.2 fixed**: `core/scm.Source.SetStatusBarCommands` compared the items with `==`, which panics when `Args` holds a map (the Git items do), and `Source.mutate` did not release the lock of the model when the change panicked, so the window froze (the host contained the panic and then waited for the lock). The items are compared with `reflect.DeepEqual` for `Args`, and `mutate` releases the lock by `defer`.
492. **`git.Find` looks in the `PATH` of the environment it is given** (the last `PATH=` wins), not in the process's, so a test that gives the extension an environment decides which `git` is found; a fake `git` that is too old no longer falls back to the real one.
493. **`ResourceDecorations.Letter` is an interim field** until the decorations provider of step 24.6: the view takes the letter and the color of a row from the provider's status when no decoration says otherwise. It is removed in 24.6.
494. **The repository cache** (VS Code's `repositoryCache.ts`, 30 URLs and 10 folders each, LRU) is in `$XDG_STATE_HOME/emetos/git-repositories.json` (0600, atomic writes) and is only filled by the extension for now; the Clone command of step 24.16 asks it for an existing clone.
495. **The Git manifest does not declare `git.openMergeEditor`**: the workbench registers it already (`core/command/builtin.go`, "Resolve in Merge Editor"), and a second declaration made the host log `CommandDuplicate` at every start. The other commands of VS Code's palette list that the later steps implement are declared by the extension.

## 2026-10-31: step 24.6, file decorations (decisions 500 to 515; decided by the agent, to be reviewed by the user)

500. **A provider of file decorations answers at once, from memory** (`FileDecorationProvider.Decoration(uri) (FileDecoration, bool)`), not with a promise as VS Code's does. A provider that learns later (Git's `git check-ignore`) fires the URI when it knows. The SPEC said so (§10.30); the consequence is that VS Code's cases about promises (the async result, the cancelled request of #48502) have no counterpart, and the cancel token is not in the contract.
501. **`FileDecoration` has `Priority` and `Icon`.** VS Code's `mainThreadDecorations` gives every extension decoration the weight 10 and the Git extension's `Resource.priority` (conflict 4, ignored 3, modified 2, others 1) is not used for decorations there; the SPEC wanted those priorities, so the host's weight is 10 + `Priority`, which orders the answers of several providers and the colors a folder takes from the files below it (a conflict colors the folder before a modification does). `Icon` is VS Code's `ThemeIcon` badge (a Codicon name that wins over the letters).
502. **A slow provider cannot freeze the views.** The service calls a provider with its lock held, on the thread that asks; an answer over 1 ms is logged with the provider's name and after 20 of them the provider is not asked for new files any more (what it answered stays; its `Fire` or a new registration asks it again). `Service.Defer` runs a function once the lock is free: the host uses it to fail an extension whose provider panicked (the end of the activation disposes the provider, which needs the lock). The host asks the provider about the files of a `Fire` 2,000 at a time, one turn of the UI thread each, so that a repository of 100,000 changes does not make one 230 ms turn.
503. **The cache is bounded at 250,000 answers** (SPEC: "bounded"): the answers by (URI, provider), "nothing" included, the "nothing" answers dropped first when it is full, and an answer that still does not fit is given and not kept (a file that is not kept does not bubble into its folders; the log says so once). 100,000 decorated files fit.
504. **URIs are compared unescaped, by path segment.** The documents spell a file `file:///home/a b/c.go` (not percent-encoded) and the Git extension's `fileURI` uses `net/url` (`a%20b`); the service splits both into the same segments, so a file is one place whichever spelling an extension or a view uses. A file whose name holds a literal `%20` is the same place as the one with a space (a name that is not a valid escape is kept as it is).
505. **A folder with bubbled marks shows the dot and not its own badge**, as VS Code's `getDecoration(uri, true)` does (`containsChildren` makes `onlyChildren`): a submodule folder shows `S` only while nothing below it is marked. The SPEC sentence "with the folder's own mark" is read this way; the folder's own color still takes part in the color chain.
506. **The breadcrumbs bar is not decorated, its picker is.** VS Code's three breadcrumbs controls pass `showDecorationColors: false` and no badges for the segments of the bar, and the picker lists files and folders with `explorer.decorations`; Emetos does the same (`BreadcrumbsOptions.DecorationColors/Badges`, `kit.ListItem.URI`, `ListOverlayOpts.FileDecorations`). The step said "the breadcrumbs".
507. **Which setting each view reads.** Explorer and Open Editors: `explorer.decorations.colors` and `.badges` (VS Code's Open Editors reads those too); tabs: `workbench.editor.decorations.colors` and `.badges` (this step registers `.badges`; no badge on a compact pinned tab or on the tabs of the title bar, as VS Code's `fileDecorationBadges = false` for compact sticky tabs; the tooltip of the decoration is appended to the title with " • "); Search results: `search.decorations.*`; the Source Control view: colors off and badges on for resources (and off for a resource that has an icon), badges for the folders of the tree.
508. **`search.decorations.badges` no longer hides the count badge of a result row.** Step 10.27's code read the setting as "show the count"; in VS Code the count badge is always there and the setting is for the badge of the file decoration (`searchResultsView.ts` passes it to `setFile`). The count is always shown now, and the setting means what its description says.
509. **In a selected row of a list with the keyboard focus the selection colors win over the decoration's**, for the name and the badge (VS Code's `.monaco-list:focus .selected .monaco-icon-label, ::after { color: inherit !important }`); in an unfocused list the decoration color stays.
510. **The provider of incoming changes (`↓A`, `↓D`, `↓R`, `↓M`, `↓~` of `GitIncomingChangesFileDecorationProvider`) is not built here**: it needs the history provider and the common ancestor of the current branch and its upstream (step 24.20). VS Code's cap of 250 URIs for one event (a limit of the process boundary) is not needed: the extension runs in the process.
511. **The interim `ResourceDecorations.Letter` is removed** (decisions 447 and 493): the letter, the color and the tooltip of a Source Control row come from the decorations service, like VS Code's. `dev.scm.fake` registers a provider of its own ("dev.scm.fake") with the letters of its fixture and fires them (a resource may say `"propagate": true`); `core/scm.Decorations.Letter` and `scm.Options.Decoration` are gone, and the Git test helper `letters` reads the statuses of the repository (the tests of the groups).
512. **Git's decorations are computed and diffed on the goroutine of the status.** The map of path to decoration is made next to the groups, and so is the list of files whose decoration differs from the previous map (the provider's generation tells the UI thread whether the diff still fits; if the provider changed meanwhile, it diffs again there). The provider fires only the files whose decoration changed (VS Code fires the union of the old and the new URIs and lets the service refetch all of them); the submodules come from `.gitmodules`, with no process. The old path of a renamed file is decorated too (VS Code's `collectDecorationData`).
513. **The ignored-files provider has one `git check-ignore` for each repository and each batch**, 500 ms after the first queued path (VS Code's `@debounce(500)`), fires the ignored paths, remembers "not ignored" (up to 100,000 paths, then it forgets all), and forgets everything when a `.gitignore` or `.git/info/exclude` is saved or changes on disk and when a repository opens, closes or is untrusted. It is always on (VS Code creates it whatever `git.decorations.enabled` says; the setting turns only the status decorations off).
514. **`kit.NewFixedFileDecorations` is in the kit** (not in a test file) because the tests of the views of an extension live outside `internal/` and reach the kit only through `extension/ui` (`ui.NewFixedFileDecorations`).
515. **The Explorer's "frame" budget is relative.** The software renderer of the tests makes a frame of a 2,000-row tree about 57 ms with no decorations at all, so the test of 100,000 decorated files compares the median of 60 scroll frames with and without them (within 25 % and 2 ms), and bounds the turn of a `Fire` chunk (2,000 files, 16 ms; it measures 8 ms) and the first frame after the last turn (100 ms; it measures 58 ms).

## 2026-10-31: step 24.7, diffs of changes (decisions 520 to 535; decided by the agent, to be reviewed by the user)

520. **The contract has more than the SPEC listed**: `ProvideContent` returns a `ContentRegistration` (`Changed(uris...)`, `Dispose()`) and not a bare dispose function, and `Documents()` gets `OpenDocument(OpenSpec)`, `Tabs()` and `CloseTabs(tabs...)`, and `Workspace()` gets `RevealFile(uri, inOS)`. `OpenDocument` is VS Code's `vscode.open` with a title: Open File (HEAD), the deleted file ("a.go (Deleted)"), a staged addition and an untracked file all open one document under a title of the caller's, which `OpenDiff` cannot do. `Tabs` and `CloseTabs` are what `git.closeDiffOnOperation`, Close All Diff Editors and Close All Unmodified Editors need (VS Code's `window.tabGroups`). `RevealFile` is there because an extension cannot run the commands of the window (there is no general "run a command" in the contract and none of the privileges gives it to Git), and Reveal in Explorer View and Open Containing Folder are commands of the window. `Language` of a provider is optional: the window detects the language from the label, as for a file of that name (`Makefile` and `Dockerfile` included).
521. **The registry of provided documents is the window tab's, over the process's** (`document.NewVirtualRegistryOver`), so that two window tabs both have a Git extension that provides `git`, and a provider goes away with its extension (`UnregisterPrefix`). The window, not the extension, makes the document: the text is made by `Documents.GenerateVirtual` on a goroutine and the document by `AdoptVirtual` on the UI thread (the old `OpenVirtual` generated on the UI thread, which a `git cat-file` must not). A provider's `Text`, `Label` and `Language` are wrapped by the host: a panic fails the extension and is an `ExtensionContentPanicked` error to the caller.
522. **A saved diff of a Git version is dropped when the window comes back** and does not return after a restart. The step says a diff whose provider is gone is dropped on restore; the extensions start as the last step of making a workspace, after the editors were restored, so the provider is never there at that time. Restoring them would need a second pass after the extensions started (the saved editors of an unknown scheme kept in the state and opened when the provider registers). Not done: VS Code restores `git:` diffs because its extension host starts first and the editors wait for it.
523. **The form of the URI**: `emetos-virtual:/git/<id>/<ref>/<path>`, the id being twelve hexadecimal digits of the SHA-256 of the repository root (stable across runs, no path of the machine in the URI), the index an empty ref (`//`), and a submodule's diff the ref `index` or `wt` with `.diff` added to the path (VS Code's `submoduleOf`). `~` is read as the index: VS Code's `sanitizeRef` picks the index when the file has a staged change and HEAD otherwise, and the two texts are equal when the file has none, so the choice needs no model on the goroutine that reads. A ref that starts with `-` or has a character outside letters, digits and `._/~^@{}+-` is refused (`GitContentRefInvalid`): a URI is data that could be made up, and `git cat-file blob -x:path` would take an option.
524. **What the text of a version is**: raw bytes of the blob (`cat-file blob`, never `show --textconv`: the background rules of D35 say no textconv), UTF-8 with the byte order mark dropped and bad bytes shown as U+FFFD (no guess of an encoding: the editor's file reading, which guesses, is not reachable from the extension); a NUL byte in the first 8,000 is binary, as git decides (a UTF-16 file is binary to git and to us). A binary file is the error `ContentBinary` (the contract's `TagContentBinary`) and the window shows the notice of a binary file (`BinaryFileNotice`) and opens nothing; a file over 16 MiB is `GitContentTooLarge` ("the file is too large to compare"), shown as "Cannot open <title>: ...". VS Code shows a missing version as an error ("file not found"); the SPEC says an empty text, and so it is. `classify` knows two more messages of a missing object (`path ... does not exist (neither on disk nor in the index)` and `is in the index, but not at stage 3`), each with a test against the real git.
525. **Every resource runs the same command object.** VS Code builds a `Command` for each Resource; with 100,000 resources that is 100,000 argument lists. Here all rows share `git.openResource` with no arguments of its own, and the Source Control view gives a command with no arguments the resource it belongs to (`{"sourceControl", "group", "uri"}`) plus how the click opens it, as VS Code passes the Resource and the open event. The command reads `git.openDiffOnClick` when it runs, so the setting works at once (VS Code reads it when it makes the resource). `git.openResource` is declared (the extension registers its handler) and is not in the palette.
526. **Preview and kept tabs.** A click on a row, Open Changes and a version opened from the palette are previews (VS Code's `vscode.diff` and `vscode.open` open a preview by default); Enter and a double click in the view (the `pinned` option) keep, and so do Open File, Open File (HEAD) from a menu, and the diffs of Open All Changes. **Open All Changes follows the SPEC and not today's VS Code**: VS Code 1.10x opens the files of the changes (`vscode.open`, in the background), the SPEC of this step says the diff editors of a group and an ask above 10; the diffs are opened kept and without taking the focus, the question being "Are you sure you want to open N changes?" with Open Changes and Cancel, and a deleted file is left out as in VS Code. With no source control or group argument it opens the changes of the work tree and the untracked files, as VS Code's does.
527. **The conflict rows that VS Code opens nothing for open the file here**: VS Code's resolver leaves both sides undefined for "added by us" and "added by them" and `vscode.open` gets `undefined`; Emetos opens the file with its markers (the file is there). "Both deleted" opens nothing, as in VS Code. With `git.mergeEditor` on, a click on "both modified" or "both added" opens the merge editor in VS Code; this needs the merge editor of the window; step 24.14 built it (`OpenSpec.MergeEditor`, decision 768).
528. **The editor title menu is drawn from the menu registry.** The `EditorTitle` items of an extension (the `MenuEditorTitle` constant of step 24.2 had no reader) appear in the title actions of a group beside the `Contributes.EditorActions` icons: an item of a group that starts with `navigation` and has a command icon is a button, any other goes into the "..." menu, a disabled item is left out. The commands get `{"groupId", "uri"}` (VS Code gives the editor's resource to the commands of `editor/title`): the document of a text editor, the modified side of a diff editor. The context of the group gets `isInDiffEditor` for a diff editor and `resourceScheme`, `resourceLangId`, `resourceExtname` and `resourceFilename` of its modified side; `resourceScheme` of a provided document is its scheme (`git`). `scmActiveResourceHasChanges` stays what 24.2 made it (the active text editor's file), so it is false in a diff editor; the Git items that read it say `!isInDiffEditor`.
529. **Menu groups with an order are written `inline@1` in VS Code's package.json and split here** (`item()` in `menus_diff.go`: the group `inline`, the order 1), because the Source Control view compares the group with `inline` exactly. The `navigation@1` and `navigation@2` of the 24.5 repository context menu items were written the other way and are separate groups there (only a separator between them); they are left as they are.
530. **`git.activeResourceHasUnstagedChanges` counts an untracked file** when `git.untrackedChanges` is `mixed` (the default), as VS Code does: the untracked file is in the work tree group. `git.activeResourceHasMergeConflicts` and `git.mergeChanges` wait for step 24.14.
531. **A tab's name is the opener's and stays with the tab**: `SetName` on the input before it is shown; opening a diff that is open already shows the old tab with its old name (the same tab whatever the title); the name is not saved, so a restored diff of two documents is named after them. The tooltip of a tab of a file keeps its path.
532. **Content is made again when the repository changes**, a thing the step does not ask for: after each status, the open documents of the index, the stages and the submodules are given to `ContentRegistration.Changed`, and those of a commit when HEAD moved, so that a diff open while the user stages in a terminal shows the new index. VS Code does the same with `onDidChangeFile` of its file system provider (debounced 1,100 ms; here the status debounce of 500 ms already is). The window makes them again in the background and gives the document the new text when it differs.
533. **`git.openFile` keeps the selection** of the editor it comes from when that editor shows the same file (a Git version of it, or the file itself), as VS Code does (the selection; the visible range is not kept, the editor reveals the selection).
534. **VS Code's tests**: `git.test.ts` has no case for the URIs (`toGitUri` and `fromGitUri` have none anywhere in `extensions/git`), and `ResourceCommandResolver` has none either, so no case is ported: 0 of 0. The tables of `resolver_test.go` (21 rows for the statuses and groups, 2 for submodules) and `uri_test.go` are written from `repository.ts` and `uri.ts`, and say so.
535. **`Workspace().RevealFile` goes through the optional `FileRevealer` of the window's documents** and fails with `ExtensionRevealUnavailable` where the scope cannot; the Explorer reveal (`revealInExplorer`) and the file manager (`revealFileInOS`, an OpenURI portal on a goroutine) are the commands the window has.
536. **A scope that cannot show documents is not warned about**: when `Documents().ProvideContent` fails with `ExtensionDocumentUnavailable` (a host without a workbench), the Git extension logs nothing and goes on without the documents of the versions of files, as it does for `ExtensionDecorationsUnavailable` (24.6). Any other failure is still a warning. Tested by `TestAScopeThatCannotShowDocumentsIsNotWarnedAbout`.

## 2026-10-31: step 24.8, stage, unstage and discard (decisions 540 to 556; decided by the agent, to be reviewed by the user)

540. **The optimistic update is applied before git runs**, not after it succeeded as VS Code's `run()` does (its callback runs after the operation): the step asks that the update is undone when git fails, which only makes sense if it came first. The view shows the move at once; a failure shows "Git: <message>" and the refresh that every command ends with puts the real state back. A status that was already running when the command started may overwrite the update with the old state for a moment; the next status is right.
541. **The questions of a discard use the SPEC's wording on VS Code's structure.** VS Code 1.143 says **Discard File** for one file and has no warning for one tracked file; the SPEC says **Discard Changes** and "This action is irreversible!". Emetos uses the SPEC's button and warning for one file, VS Code's texts for many ("Are you sure you want to discard ALL changes in N files?", **Discard All N Files**, "Your current working set will be FOREVER LOST") plus the SPEC's list of the first five names ("a.go, b.go and 3 more"), and VS Code's texts for untracked files and the mixed question. The message is the first sentence and the rest is the detail (VS Code puts it all in the message).
542. **`Workspace().TrashFiles(ctx, uris) (undo, error)`** is the way to the trash (SPEC §6.3): an extension has no other. It uses the window's `FileOps`, so the discarded files are one operation of the Explorer's history (Ctrl+Z in the Explorer undoes it too); the new `FileOps.TrashUndoable` returns a function that takes back exactly that operation whatever was done after it (a second call, or a call after the Explorer's Undo, fails with `FileOpNothingToUndo`).
